If x<y\nand Delta=g(y)-g(x)>=0, monotonicity and the Lipschitz inequality imply\n\n    g(t)-g(x) >= max(0,Delta-(y-t)),\n    g(y)-g(t) >= max(0,Delta-(t-x)), x<=t<=y.\n\nHere Delta<=y-x. Exact signed interval interface\n\nFor unit cores let T_(1+d)(x)=x-(1+d)*clip(x,[-v,u]), where d>0.\nIt is continuous, fixes0, and has slopes1,-d,1 on its three pieces.\nFor p,q>=0, its image of [-p,q] is EXACTLY [-p',q'], where\n\n    p'=max(d*min(q,u), p-(1+d)*v),\n    q'=max(d*min(p,v), q-(1+d)*u). Exact failure of weighted absorption; a paid physical escape\n\nThe ratio condition needed for weighted absorption can itself be\nlocated exactly, without computation. Define\n\n    T_j=A_j/d_j=T+[(1-eta)/a]*(2/c)^j.\n\nIt strictly decreases to T as j increases. Repeating the induction\nin Section5 shows that weighted absorption at EVERY level through m\nholds whenever max(u,v)/min(u,v)<=T_(m-1), because this implies\nthe needed ratio bound A_j/d_j at every physical bridge j<m.\n\nConversely, suppose u/v=t>T_(m-1). Put\nA=A_(m-1), d=d_(m-1), S=A-1, and start the SAME full word H_m at\ns=A*u. The first H_(m-1) is entirely positive saturated, with\nqueries x=u*(A-p) at prefix mass p in[0,S]; it ends at u. The root\nbridge1+d sends u to -d*u. Since d*u>A*v, the ENTIRE second child\nis negative saturated, with queries\n\n    x=-d*u+v*q, 0<=q<=S,\n\nwhere q is the actual suffix prefix mass. The true terminal is\n\n    x_n=-d*u+S*v < -v.                               (8.1)\n\nThe initial state lies in [-A_m*v,A_m*u], but the terminal is outside\n[-v,u]. This is feasible because A>1. Since\nD_m=4A+2d-1, A>2, and d/A<c-2<1/2,\n\n    D_m*sup E_root\n      =(d/A)^2*(4A+2d-1)/(2A-1)<3/4.                (8.4)\n\nIndeed 4A+2d-1<5A-1<=3(2A-1) for A>=2. First,\nextending the weighted-core induction fails at the actual root above.\nSecond, extending the simultaneous endpoint plateau globally also\nfails: for fixed m, take v=1 and u=U so large that\n\n    U>max(A_m/d_m, S_m/[d_m*(1-b)], T).\n\nThen the negative input -d_m U is saturated throughout H_m and\nPsi_m(-d_m U)=-d_m U+S_m. Its magnitude exceeds bU, since the\ndisplayed stronger condition gives d_m U-S_m>d_m bU>bU\n(d_m>1). Consequently the two-endpoint bound (6.1) at j=m is\nfalse for sufficiently large clipping ratio. These are literal\nphysical trajectories, not a failure of a chosen algebraic certificate.\nThe corresponding exterior objective remains covered by the\nall-saturated formula, so this does not refute a score bound either.\n\n## 9. What remains, precisely\n\nThe full unit-core family would have profile1/D_m if the following\ntwo signed inequalities held for every positive u,v and every R>0:\n\n    2 f(-p_m(R,R)) <= R^2/D_m,\n    2 f( q_m(R,R)) <= R^2/D_m. We claim\n\n    |s|<=A_j => |Psi_j(s)|<=1,\n    |s|>=A_j => Psi_j(s)=sign(s)(|s|-S_j).                 (5.2)\n\nThe second formula follows because every prefix of a word of total mass\nS_j leaves magnitude at least1 on the original side. For the first,\ninduct from A_0=1. If |s|<=A_j, the first child outputs magnitude <=1,\nthe bridge outputs magnitude <=d_j<A_j, and the second child gives <=1.\nFor A_j<s<=A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, the first output is s-S_j>1 and the bridge\noutput is s-A_j-d_j in (-d_j,A_j]. Apply the second child and oddness.\nThis proves (5.2), including thresholds.\n\nFor radius z>=0, the image of [-z,z] under T_(1+d) is the symmetric\ninterval of radius\n\n    E_d(z)=d z if z<=1;\n           d if 1<=z<=1+2d;\n           z-1-d if z>=1+2d.                            (5.3)\n\nFor z>=1 this follows by maximizing the absolute values on the inner\nlinear branch and the exterior affine branch; the maximum is\nmax(d,|z-1-d|). The pieces agree at both thresholds.\n\nPut J_j(z)=max_(|s|<=z)|Psi_j(s)|. Continuity and oddness make the image\nEXACTLY [-J_j(z),J_j(z)], so no incompatible child histories are chosen:\n\n    J_0(z)=z, J_(j+1)=J_j o E_(d_j) o J_j.              (5.4)\n\nAll J_j are nondecreasing. By (5.2), J_j(z)<=1 for z<=A_j and\nJ_j(z)=z-S_j for z>=A_j.\n\nPut b_plateau=a beta. Then 0<b_plateau<1/2 and c b_plateau=beta^2<1.\nFor every 2<=j<=m,\n\n    A_(j-1)<d_j<A_(j-1)+2d_(j-1).                       (5.5)\n\nFor the lower inequality at j=2, multiplying d_2-A_1 by c gives\n\n    beta c^2-2c-beta=2epsilon-lambda^2 epsilon^2(1+epsilon)>0.\n\nThe sign uses lambda^2<6 and 6epsilon(1+epsilon)<2. If d_j>A_(j-1),\nthen d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)d_j-2A_(j-1)>0 because c-1/c>2.\nThe upper inequality follows from\n(c-2)d_(j-1)<A_(j-1), since c-2<1 and d_(j-1)<A_(j-1).\n\nIt follows that\n\n    J_j(d_j)=b_plateau for every 1<=j<=m.                 (5.6)\n\nAt j=1, d_1=beta<1 and J_1=E_a give a beta. For j>=2 the first\nchild sends d_j to d_j-S_(j-1), strictly between1 and 1+2d_(j-1).\nThe bridge envelope sends this whole radius to d_(j-1), and the second\nchild sends that radius to b_plateau by induction. This proves the exact\nplateau through every physical level.\n\n## 6. Scalar all-amplitude bound, including every interval between levels\n\nFor every j>=1 and every integer k>=0 in the preceding scope,\n\n    J_j(d_j b_plateau^k)<=b_plateau^(k+1).                (6.1)\n\nInduct simultaneously in j for all k. At j=1 the input is beta times\nb_plateau^k<1 and (5.3) gives equality. The case k=0 is (5.6).\nFor k>=1 and j>=2,\n\n    d_j b_plateau^k=beta^2 d_(j-1)b_plateau^(k-1)\n                    <=d_(j-1)b_plateau^(k-1).\n\nThe first child thus outputs at most b_plateau^k<=1. The bridge's\ninner branch gives at most d_(j-1)b_plateau^k, and the second child\ngives at most b_plateau^(k+1). This proves (6.1).\n\nFor every real 0<z<=d_j, choose the unique geometric interval with\n\n    d_j b_plateau^(k+1)<z<=d_j b_plateau^k.\n\nEndpoint equality is placed at the upper endpoint. Monotonicity and\n(6.1) give J_j(z)<=b_plateau^(k+1)<z/d_j. For d_j<z<=A_j,\n(5.2) gives J_j(z)<=1<z/d_j. At zero the image is zero. Consequently\n\n    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j for every |s|<=A_j.               (6.2)\n\nThis controls EVERY real amplitude, all inverse threshold preimages,\nboth signs, and all repeated core entries, rather than a finite mesh.\n\nSince a>1/3, A_m<c^m and c^m>2^m>=32>18,\n\n    d_m^2>c^(2m)/9>2c^m>D_m.\n\nFor 0<|s|<=A_m the actual terminal is in the quadratic core, so\n2Hub(Psi_m(s))/s^2<=1/d_m^2<1/D_m. For |s|=z>=A_m,\n(5.2) gives exact payoff (2z-D_m)/z^2. The identity\n\n    z^2-D_m(2z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2\n\nbounds it by1/D_m, attained exactly at z=D_m. Including s=0 by\ncontinuity yields the homogeneous inequality\n\n    2Hub(Psi_m(s))<=s^2/D_m for EVERY real s.             (6.3)\n\nFor ANY positive fixed thresholds b_(e_j), the comparison box runs r\nindependent copies scaled by those thresholds. Summing (6.3) proves\n\n    2F_C(y_n)<=||y_0||^2/D_m                             (6.4)\n\nfor all comparison starts and every finite r. This is the only scalar\ninput to the perturbation calculation; it has been proved within the\nsame joint draft.\n\n## 7. All-history perturbation estimate with the FULL initial radius\n\nLet x_i=(v_i,w_i) be the ACTUAL anisotropic-simplex run, with actual\nprojection g_i=(p_i,q_i). Let y_i be the comparison-box GD initialized at\ny_0=v_0. No initial mixed coordinate is required to vanish. Write R=||x_0||,\nso ||v_0||<=R and ||w_0||<=R separately; the real denominator is their\ncombined squared norm R^2, never the independent part alone.\n\nBecause ||q_i||<=delta, every physical prefix obeys\n\n    ||w_i||<=||w_0||+delta sum_(t<i)h_t<=R+S delta.        (7.1)\n\nFix one such physical input (v,w), its actual projection (p,q), and the\nbox projection c0=P_C(v). Section4 supplies a lift (c0,q0) in D with\n||q0||<=delta. Projection optimality and the box normal condition give\n\n    ||v-p||^2+||w-q||^2<=||v-c0||^2+||w-q0||^2,\n    ||v-p||^2>=||v-c0||^2+||p-c0||^2.\n\nThus\n\n    ||p-c0||^2\n       <=(||w||+delta)^2-max(||w||-delta,0)^2\n       <=4delta||w||+delta^2\n       <=4delta R+(4S+1)delta^2.                        (7.2)\n\nFor ||w||>=delta the middle difference is 4delta||w||; for ||w||<delta,\nits displayed bound follows from ||w||^2<=2delta||w||. This proves\n(7.2) uniformly over all active faces, including threshold ties. No face\nsequence, small independent threshold, or large transverse start breaks it.\n\nFor a scalar clipped gradient, the update s -> s-h clip(s,[-b,b]) is\ncontinuous and piecewise affine with slopes 1-h and1. Its Lipschitz\nconstant is at most ell_h=max(1,|1-h|). The Cartesian comparison map\nhas the same Euclidean Lipschitz bound, by summing squared component\nbounds. Set t_mix=delta/R and\n\n    e_proj=R sqrt(4t_mix+(4S+1)t_mix^2).\n\nFrom (7.2), for e_i=||v_i-y_i||,\n\n    e_(i+1)<=ell_(h_i)e_i+h_i e_proj, e_0=0.\n\nExpanding this finite inequality symbolically in the original order gives\n\n    e_n<=e_proj sum_(i<n) h_i product_(i<j<n)ell_(h_j)\n        <=e_proj S P_amp.                               (7.3)\n\nNo recurrence has been executed. Large-step amplification is paid by the\nactual product P_amp in (4.1). In particular it is not replaced by the\nscalar quadratic product, which would generally be an invalid bound.\n\nFor the comparison function, ||grad F_C||^2<=2F_C. In one coordinate this\nis equality in the core and follows from b^2<=2b|s|-b^2 outside; sum over\ncoordinates. Therefore sqrt(2F_C) is 1-Lipschitz: apply the gradient bound\nto sqrt(2F_C+eta), integrate along a segment, then let eta decrease to0.\nBy (6.4) and (7.3),\n\n    sqrt(2F_C(v_n))/R\n      <=1/sqrt(D_m)+S P_amp sqrt(4t_mix+(4S+1)t_mix^2).   (7.4)\n\nThe full actual function satisfies for every (v,w)\n\n    F_D(v,w)<=F_C(v)+delta||w||,                         (7.5)\n\nby dropping -||q||^2/2 from (2.3), bounding <w,q> by delta||w||,\nand maximizing the independent part over C. Combining (7.1), (7.4),\nand (7.5) proves the useful unrestricted sensitivity interface\n\n    E <= [D_m^(-1/2)+S P_amp\n                      sqrt(4t_mix+(4S+1)t_mix^2)]^2\n                          +2t_mix(1+S t_mix).            (7.6)\n\nIt holds for arbitrary positive mixed weights and arbitrary full starts;\nonly the evaluated small-weight consequence below uses (4.1).\n\nUnder (4.1),\n\n    0<t_mix<=1/[64 D_m^3(1+S)^2 P_amp^2].                (7.7)\n\nSince D_m>=1, P_amp>=1, and 4S+1<=4(1+S)^2, one has\n(4S+1)t_mix<=1/16<1. Consequently the square of the second term in\nbrackets in (7.6) is at most\n\n    5 S^2 P_amp^2 t_mix <=5/(64D_m^3)<1/(4D_m^3).\n\nAlso S t_mix<=1, so 2t_mix(1+S t_mix)<=4t_mix<=1/(16D_m^3).\nAll quantities are nonnegative. Substitution yields\n\n    E <= (1+1/(2D_m))^2/D_m+1/(16D_m^3)\n       = 1/D_m+1/D_m^2+5/(16D_m^3)\n       <= (1+2/D_m)/D_m.                               (7.8)\n\nThis is uniform over every parameter in Mechanism I, every initial full\nunit direction, every positive independent scale, every mixed profile,\nevery finite rank including r=m, and every m>=1536.\n\nThe lower bracket is attained inside the same law. Let q_j^axis be the\nphysical unit vector at coordinate label e_j. On this axis, averaging labels with chi_(e_j)=+1 or -1 gives the two points\n+/-b_(e_j) q_j^axis in D; all other nonzero characters average to zero. Every\nvertex has axis coordinate +/-b_(e_j), so the normal condition proves\n\n    P_D(s q_j^axis)=clip(s,[-b_(e_j),b_(e_j)]) q_j^axis.\n\nChoose x_0=D_m b_(e_1)q_1^axis, set R=D_m b_(e_1), and choose ANY mixed\nweights satisfying (4.1). The full run remains on this axis, has unique\nminimum0 and radius R, and attains E=1/D_m by (6.3). This is a calibration\nmember; the upper theorem is not confined to it. Thus\n\n    1 <= D_m sup_(Mechanism I) E <=1+2/D_m.              (7.9)\n\nSince S>n>=m>=1536, D_m>3073>188. Accepted931 gives\n\n    B_n E < (47/50)(1+2/D_m)<19/20.\n\nMoreover E/K_n(H_m)<=1+2/D_m, and the same bound holds relative to any\nlarger complete channel. The right side tends to1. There is no assertion\nthat E<=K_n exactly, nor that (7.8) bounds unrestricted W.\n\n## 8. Mechanism II: an exact vertex inverse selector, with arbitrary weights\n\nThis is an attempted changing-vertex construction. A common gradient is\nNOT imposed. Choose any finite r>=2, any positive unequal weights b_t\nwith no small-mixed-weight condition, and the original hierarchy. For\nexample one explicit word-dependent dictionary is r=m and\nb_t=1+integer_binary_label(t). Choose a label s_last, put p=z_(s_last),\nand choose ANY vector v satisfying the finite normal inequalities\n\n    <v,z_s-p><=0 for every s.                           (8.1)\n\nThis normal is unbounded and need not be orthogonal to p. Initialize the\nlast queried point x_(n-1)=p+v. Its actual projection is p by (2.4).\n\nNow define earlier points backwards for i=n-2,...,0. Given y=x_(i+1)\nand h=h_i>1, test EVERY original vertex z_s by the explicit inequalities\n\n    <y+(h-1)z_s,z_u-z_s><=0 for EVERY u.                (8.2)\n\nAmong the vertices passing these tests select one minimizing the finite\nlist of numbers ||y+h z_s||^2, with lexicographic tie breaking. Put\nx_i=y+h z_s. This is a fixed finite inverse selector with all guards\nspecified, not a PEP restatement or an optimization oracle for a start.\nIt can alternatively allow arbitrary choices among valid vertices; the\nbound below covers that larger family as well.\n\nThe list is nonempty. Choose any label maximizing <y,z_s> among the\nfinite labels. Since all ||z_s||^2=M and <z_s,z_u><=M,\n\n    <y+(h-1)z_s,z_u-z_s>\n      =<y,z_u-z_s>+(h-1)(<z_s,z_u>-M)<=0.\n\nThis proves existence but does NOT replace the specified minimum-norm\nselector by that particular maximizing vertex. For every retained vertex,\n(8.2) is exactly the normal test for x_i-z_s, so its actual gradient is\nz_s and x_i-h_i z_s=y. All n original queried gradients are vertices.\nSet the ACTUAL terminal x_n=x_(n-1)-h_leaf p, and obtain its gradient\nand value by (2.5)-(2.3), with no vertex or core restriction at the terminal.\nEverything is finite for each m. This inverse dictionary is a textual\nconstruction; no enumeration or trajectory was executed.\n\n## 9. Why every vertex-query itinerary freezes on the entire H_m\n\nWe prove the stronger claim: for this body and the same enclosing m,\nfor every j>=1, ANY actual run over H_j whose gradients at all pre-step\nqueries are vertices has a constant queried gradient. No hypothesis on\nthe block's terminal projection is made.\n\nFirst, for two consecutive vertex gradients p,q with common squared norm\nM, projection's firm inequality and the physical update give\n\n    ||p-q||^2 <= h <p,p-q> = (h/2)||p-q||^2.\n\nIf h<2, they must coincide. This observation alone is insufficient on H_m,\nwhich has very large bridges; the following induction includes them.\n\nAt j=1 there is one queried point, so the claim holds. Suppose it holds\nfor H_j, j>=1, and consider H_(j+1). The queried gradients on the first\nchild are some vertex p, and those on the second child are some vertex q,\nby the induction hypothesis. The last leaf of the first child has step\nh_leaf<2, and its output is the bridge query, also a vertex by assumption.\nThe preceding observation forces the bridge gradient to be p as well.\n\nLet X be the bridge point, b_bridge=1+d_j, and let Y be the last QUERY\nof the second child, not its terminal. The literal physical displacements\nare\n\n    Y=X-b_bridge p-(S_j-h_leaf)q.                       (9.1)\n\nIf p!=q put C_gap=M-<p,q>=||p-q||^2/2>0. The original normal conditions\nat X (projection p) and Y (projection q) respectively give\n\n    <X,p-q>>=C_gap, <Y,p-q><=-C_gap.\n\nSubstituting (9.1) yields the necessary condition\n\n    b_bridge-(S_j-h_leaf)>=2,\n    equivalently d_j+h_leaf>=A_j.                      (9.2)\n\nThe actual SAME-m hierarchy has the strict reverse inequality. From (5.1),\n\n    A_j-d_j=(1-v_mass)2^j+v_mass(3-c)c^j.\n\nBoth coefficients are positive because 0<v_mass<1 and c<5/2<3.\nBoth powers strictly increase in j. At j=1,\n\n    A_1-d_1=2+a-beta>1+a=h_leaf\n\nbecause beta<1. Thus A_j-d_j>h_leaf for every j>=1. This contradicts\n(9.2), proving p=q. Induction proves constant queried gradients on all\nH_j through H_m. Every parameter uses the same enclosing m.\n\nThis proof closes every queried-vertex itinerary in Mechanism II,\nincluding arbitrary inverse tie choices and arbitrary anisotropy. It is\nnot a reparameterized carrier hypothesis: the attempted all-vertex\nswitching is shown impossible using a remote original normal inequality\nat the last query of a complete child. It also strengthens the older\nh_i<2-only obstruction, since no bound is placed on the higher bridges.\n\nWrite p for this now-proved common queried gradient and M=||p||^2.\nAll actual queried points, terminal, and full initial radius reduce to\n\n    x_i=x_0-(sum_(t<i)h_t)p, 0<=i<=n,\n    x_0=(S-h_leaf+1)p+v,\n    x_n=(1-h_leaf)p+v,\n    R_init^2=||(S-h_leaf+1)p+v||^2.                    (9.3)\n\nThe terminal gradient is the unrestricted projection (2.5) at x_n and its\nvalue is (2.3). Formula (9.3) is an exact evaluated all-history interface;\nno normal component has been made free in the denominator.\n\nLet T=<x_0,p>/M. At the last query, comparing its normal with zero in D\ngives T>=S-h_leaf+1>0. Also R_init^2>=M T^2 by Cauchy--Schwarz.\nThe literal outgoing terminal row Q_(n,n-1)<=0 gives, after completing\nthe square in the ACTUAL terminal gradient,\n\n    2F_D(x_n)\n      <=2f_(n-1)+[(h_leaf-1)^2-1]M\n      =M[2T-D_m+h_leaf^2].                             (9.4)\n\nFor explicit verification of the first line, that row says\nF_D(x_n)<=f_(n-1)-h_leaf<g_n,p>-||g_n-p||^2/2.\nComplete the square in g_n+(h_leaf-1)p. The last queried value is\nf_(n-1)=<x_0,p>-(S-h_leaf)M-M/2, yielding the second line.\nNo incoming terminal row is needed for this upper estimate, but the actual\nfunction already satisfies all of them by (3.1).\n\nPut L_mass=D_m-h_leaf^2>0. Combining (9.3)-(9.4), and using\n\n    T^2-L_mass(2T-L_mass)=(T-L_mass)^2>=0,\n\ngives the quantitative WHOLE-law bound\n\n    E<= (2T-L_mass)/T^2 <=1/(D_m-h_leaf^2).              (9.5)\n\nThe numerator is nonnegative for an actual member, because it bounds its\nnonnegative terminal value. Since h_leaf<3/2, h_leaf^2<9/4<3 and D_m>3073,\n\n    B_n E < (47/50) D_m/(D_m-3)<19/20.                 (9.6)\n\nThe last comparison requires only D_m>285. The exact initial choice\nx_0=D_m p is admitted: choose v=(S+h_leaf)p in (8.1). All its normals\nare valid because <p,z_s-p><=0. It gives constant gradients through the\nterminal and E=1/D_m. Even the specified selector admits this start:\nfor the fixed last point, the freeze theorem forces every admissible\nbackwards sequence to use p. Thus\n\n    1 <=D_m sup_(Mechanism II)E\n        <=D_m/(D_m-h_leaf^2) ->1.                       (9.7)\n\nThe same ratio bound holds relative to the COMPLETE K_n and any channel\ncontaining K_n. This is uniform in rank, all weights, all last normals,\nand all inverse selections. It is not an upper bound on W_n(H_m).\n\n## 10. A genuine remaining escape: noncommuting disphenoid edges\n\nThe weighted dictionary does not reduce to a separable box or a scalar\nprocess once nonvertex projections are admitted. We give an explicit\ngeometric counterexample to those possible overextensions. It is a local\nphysical example, not a whole-H_m payoff assertion.\n\nTake r=2, coordinates t=10,01,11, and weights (B,C,E)>0. The four\nvertices are\n\n    (B,C,E), (B,-C,-E), (-B,C,-E), (-B,-C,E).\n\nAll three nonzero characters occur; the third is the product of the first\ntwo and is not an independent sign. The two opposite edges with first\ncoordinate +B and -B have transverse directions\n\n    v_plus=(C,E), v_minus=(C,-E), ell^2=C^2+E^2,\n    P_plus=v_plus v_plus^T/ell^2,\n    P_minus=v_minus v_minus^T/ell^2.\n\nFor x=(u,y), the EXACT positive-edge projection is\n(B,P_plus y) under the two complete guards\n\n    |<y,v_plus>|<=ell^2,\n    2B(u-B)>=|<y-P_plus y,v_minus>|.                    (10.1)\n\nThe first guard puts the candidate on its edge. Its residual is\n(u-B,y-P_plus y); it is perpendicular to the positive edge. Comparing\nwith both negative-edge vertices gives the second guard, and comparisons\nwith the positive endpoints give equality. Thus every original vertex is\nchecked. The negative-edge formula is (-B,P_minus y), with guards\n\n    |<y,v_minus>|<=ell^2,\n    2B(-u-B)>=|<y-P_minus y,v_plus>|.                   (10.2)\n\nOn strict cells, the transverse update is y -> (I-h P_plus)y or\n(I-h P_minus)y. The two projectors need not commute. For B=3,C=2,E=1,\n\n    P_plus=(1/5)[[4,2],[2,1]],\n    P_minus=(1/5)[[4,-2],[-2,1]],\n    P_plus P_minus-P_minus P_plus\n                       =(1/25)[[0,-12],[12,0]] !=0.    (10.3)\n\nThere is also an actual physical transition between these cells. Let h>2,\nput eta=(h-2)/(16h^2)>0, and initialize\n\n    x=(3h/2,eta,0), y=eta(1,0).\n\nThe positive-edge tangent coefficient is 2eta/5<1. Its residual paired\nwith v_minus is 4eta/5<h-2<9(h-2), so (10.1) holds strictly. After the actual\nstep the point is\n\n    x'=(-3h/2,y'), y'=eta(1-4h/5,-2h/5).\n\nSince ||I-hP_plus||=h-1, one has ||y'||<1/16, so the negative-edge tangent\nguard is strict. With n_minus=(1,2) perpendicular to v_minus,\n\n    |<y'-P_minus y',v_plus>|\n          =(4eta/5)|1-8h/5|\n          <=2h eta < h-2 < 9(h-2).\n\nThe middle inequality holds for h>2 because\n(4/5)(1+8h/5)<2h. This proves (10.2) strictly. Thus both projected\ngradients are in relative interiors of different nonparallel edges; all\nfour original vertices participate across the two supports. The residual\nfirst coordinates have magnitudes 3(h/2-1)>0, so these are genuine exterior\npositive-height projections. The whole function remains (2.3), with\nunique minimum0 and true radius sqrt(9h^2/4+eta^2). All its ordered rows\nhold by (3.1).\n\nOne may choose h=1+d_2, an actual bridge value of the diagnostic word,\nsince beta c>1. This still describes only a local transition: it does NOT\nassert a paid full-word initial state reaching x at that bridge, nor a\nuseful full-word terminal. The example is not being promoted to a lower\nbound at B_n. It establishes precisely that nonvertex projection allows\nreal noncommutation; it does not by itself prescribe a whole-H_m\nitinerary. All three weights (3,2,1) are positive and pairwise unequal;\nC!=E is exactly what makes (10.3) nonzero.\n\nA separate immediate scope check rules out extending the whole-H_m\nconstancy assertion to nonvertex queried gradients. Put\nr_in=1/sqrt(sum_(t!=0)b_t^(-2)); (2.2) shows that the open ball of this\nradius is inside D. Take any nonzero x_0 with ||x_0||<r_in/2. Since\na<1/2, its first two gradients on the original H_m are g_0=x_0 and\ng_1=-a x_0, both in the interior and distinct. The same static function\ncontinues legally through all remaining physical steps. This is a\nfull-word existence counterexample to constancy without the vertex\nassumption, with no assertion of a useful terminal score.\n\n## 11. Scope, remaining interface, and author audit\n\nMechanism I fails uniformly because the coupled mixed-coordinate force,\nincluding an arbitrary mixed initial component, is too small after its\nENTIRE large-step amplification is paid. The precise smallness scale is\n(7.7). It is sufficient, not claimed necessary or optimal. The actual\nfunction is full-dimensional and nonseparable. Replacing its projection\nby box clipping at any step would be false; (7.2)-(7.6) quantify the error\nwithout doing so. Allowing thicker mixed weights is a genuine surviving\nparameter direction, not a counterexample to the proved smallness law.\n\nMechanism II permits arbitrary weight ratios and attempted changes of\nvertex, but its all-queried-vertices requirement is load-bearing. The\nremote original normal conditions force constancy. The terminal is not\nassumed to be a vertex and its original outgoing row is paid. Nonvertex\nqueried faces, with their changing norms and noncommuting tangent maps,\nare not covered. Section10 realizes the local geometric distinction.\n\nThe surviving substantive task is now explicit: choose thick mixed weights\nand a fully paid initial state whose ORIGINAL full-H_m nonvertex face\nsequence has a useful terminal. Formula (2.5) supplies all exact guards,\nand (3.2) supplies the true-radius target, but the general full-word\nterminal/radius estimate in that domain is UNRESOLVED. A symbolic face\nrecurrence alone is not counted as success. No third mechanism is opened.\n\nAuthor checks within this unverified draft:\n\n* All new mathematical inputs, including the complete scalar profile,\n  are jointly derived rather than imported from sibling awareness.\n* Every body has all nonzero characters with strictly positive weights;\n  the coordinate relations, real metric, and full Euclidean span are given.\n* Each function is one static globally smooth convex function, with\n  complete attained minimum {0}; no closest-radius transfer is used.\n* All actual queries and both orientations of every original interpolation\n  row follow the same full-body normal condition, including the terminal.\n* The thin-weight law includes arbitrary full initial directions; its\n  mixed initial norm is explicitly paid in (7.1), (7.2), and (7.6).\n* Its amplification is product max(1,|1-h|), not an illicit contraction\n  product; the smallness condition is stated at exactly that scale.\n* The scalar proof covers every real amplitude and threshold equality by\n  continuous interval images. Prior finding0073a83e08ff2e21 was read\nin full as awareness; the reset construction needed below is rederived.\n\nFix m>=1536. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    z=n-1, b=1+a, M=1+b, N=2^(m-1), A=D-b^2,\n    P81_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, Tail81_i=S-b-P81_i (i<z),\n    K81_i=A-P81_i-1=2S-b^2-P81_i (i<z), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nAll children use this same m. Then\nh_i=1+a*c^level(i), leaves are the even indices, and checkpoints the odd\nindices. In particular h_z=b. Write Leaf={0,2,...,z}, Check={1,3,...,n-2}.\n\nElementary bounds used throughout are\n\n    1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<sqrt(2)<3/2,\n    12/5<c<5/2, beta>7/8,\n    d_1=beta, d_2=beta*c>21/10,\n    S>2^m-1, D>2^m, A>0, K81_i>Tail81_i>0.\n\nFor example a>1/3 follows from beta>7/8 and c<5/2. The bound on c\nfollows from lambda<17/7 and epsilon<=1/6144. Also\nb=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]. The other comparisons follow by\nsubstitution. K81_i-Tail81_i=S+b-b^2>0 since b<2 and S>=b. These observations\ninclude positivity of every denominator in the arrays below.\n\nDefine a dyadic cell of height j to be\n\n    I_(j,r)={r*2^j,...,(r+1)*2^j-2},\n    1<=j<=m, 0<=r<2^(m-j).\n\nFor distinct queried indices u,v, J(u,v) is the least height of a cell\ncontaining both. For i in the\nleft half, including the whole-word root, J(i,z)=m.\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and retain independent original\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n. Positions are always\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct labels put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal samples have Q_ij<=0. An original coefficient array with\nout_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) defines\n\n    M_cert(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nAfter value cancellation, it must be PSD on ALL original vectors to be an\nupper certificate. A negative formal vector is not a primal instance.\n\n## 2. Mechanism I: position-sensitive remote routing to the last leaf\n\nFirst construct the entire family with independently chosen 0<=theta_i<=1\nfor each i<z. Set\n\n    r81_i=2theta_i*h_i/K81_i,\n    w81_i=2(1-theta_i)*h_i/Tail81_i.\n\nThe frozen LCA-sensitive law tested in Sections 6--8 is\n\n    eta=1/(4D), theta_i=1-eta/J(i,z). Its support is not contained in E80.\n\nAll signs can be checked without an unknown feasibility condition. Put\nKbase=S+b-b^2>0. Since K81_i=Kbase+Tail81_i, and Tail81_i decreases by h_i as i\nincreases, integration of the decreasing function 1/(Kbase+t) gives\n\n    sum_(i<z) h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i)\n      <= integral_0^(S-b) dt/(Kbase+t)\n      =log((2S-b^2)/(S+b-b^2)) <log 2<1.\n\nIndeed h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i) is bounded by the integral over\n[Tail81_i-h_i,Tail81_i], and these intervals partition [0,S-b]. The ratio is below\n2 because b<2. Thus sum r81_i<2 for every allowed theta. Every coefficient\nin (I.2) is nonnegative, with strict outgoing optimum mass at z.\nAt i<z the two finite directions have net inflow r81_i, exactly discharged\nto star. At z the finite/optimum outgoing net is 2, supplied by (n,z).\nTerminal flow is 2. This proves all value cancellations.\n\nThis is a new source-routing base, not an addition to any inherited\ncertificate. Its terminal distribution is concentrated at the actual last\nleaf; its entire optimum distribution is given in (I.2).\n\n### 2.1 Full polynomial and the paid contact equation\n\nWrite g=g_z, v_i=g_i-g for i<z and u=sum r81_i v_i. Put\n\n    B(v)=sum_(i<z)[r81_i+w81_i(1-h_i)]||v_i||^2\n         +sum_(i<t<z)(h_i*r81_t-w81_i*h_t)<v_i,v_t>,\n    Jrem(v)=B(v)-A||u||^2/4.\n\nThe exact full identity, for EVERY real q, is\n\n    M_I(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n       +q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2\n       -<x_0,u>+A<g,u>+B(v).                         (I.3)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. Starting with rows 2Q_(n,z)+2Q_(z,star),\nreplace r81_i units of Q_(z,star) by Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star), and add w81_i\nunits of Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i). The two added expressions, after GD, are\n\n    Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star)-Q_(z,star)\n       =-<v_i,x_0>+(P81_i+1)<v_i,g>\n                          +||v_i||^2+sum_(t<i)h_t<v_i,v_t>,\n    Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i)\n       =||v_i||^2-Tail81_i<v_i,g>-sum_(i<=t<z)h_t<v_i,v_t>.\n\nThe original base has terminal square ||g_n+(b-1)g||^2 and remainder\nq||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2+2sum h_i<v_i,g>.\nThe defining relation\n\n    w81_i*Tail81_i=2h_i-K81_i*r81_i                              (I.4)\n\nchanges each last cross coefficient to A*r81_i, giving (I.3). In particular,\nremote symmetric pairs were NOT treated as PSD. Their negative diagonal\nand mixed displacement terms appear in B.\n\nAt q=1/A, (I.3) is equivalently\n\n    M_I(1/A)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n      +||x_0-A*g-(A/2)u||^2/A+Jrem(v).               (I.5)\n\nEquation (I.4) is an intentional design restriction of this family. It is\nnot claimed necessary for a general certificate. No zero-contact rule is\nimposed on an unrestricted optimum. The term -A||u||^2/4 is the ONE\nglobal initial/common-gradient charge for this deliberately balanced law.\n\n### 2.2 Full-space failure for every member of the contact family\n\nTake p=2^(m-1)-1, H=h_p>4. Only v_p is nonzero, with v_p=1. The coefficient\nof Jrem on this vector is\n\n    E_p=r81_p-w81_p(H-1)-A*r81_p^2/4.                       (I.6)\n\nIf theta_p<=1/2, then r81_p<=H/K81_p and w81_p>=H/Tail81_p. Thus\nr81_p-w81_p(H-1)<=H/K81_p-H(H-1)/Tail81_p<0 because K81_p>Tail81_p and H>2.\nIf theta_p>=1/2, then r81_p>=H/K81_p>H/A>4/A, and\nr81_p-A*r81_p^2/4<0; subtracting w81_p(H-1)>=0 preserves negativity.\nSo E_p<0 for EVERY choice of the whole profile theta.\n\nThe complete scalar original-vector counterdirection is\n\n    x_0=0,\n    g_i=-r81_p/2 for every queried i!=p,\n    g_p=1-r81_p/2,\n    g_n=(b-1)r81_p/2.                                  (I.7)\n\nIt sets g=-r81_p/2, u=r81_p, makes the terminal square and the square in\n(I.5) zero, and gives M_I(q)=E_p<0 for every real q. The change from\n1/A to q multiplies ||x_0||^2=0. Hence even unlimited initial-norm slack\ncannot repair this coefficient family. This is stronger than rejection\nof the frozen small-eta profile, but only for family (I.2)--(I.4).\n\n## 3. Mechanism II: dense earlier-leaf transport with paid reverse cycles\n\nPut Tc=sum_(p in Check)h_p and t_p=h_p/Tc, so sum t_p=1. For EVERY\ncheckpoint p and earlier leaf ell<p define\n\n    raw_(p,ell)=[1+(p-ell)/(p+1)]/J(p,ell),\n    Z_p=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<p)raw_(p,ell),\n    omega_(p,ell)=raw_(p,ell)/Z_p,\n    mu_ell=sum_(p in Check,p>ell)t_p*omega_(p,ell).    (II.1)\n\nThere is at least one earlier leaf for every checkpoint, so Z_p>0.\nThe weights depend on the exact least common ancestor and position within\nthe enclosing chronological range. They are not uniform fan averages.\nThe formula for mu_ell is used only for ell<z. The new optimum distribution is mu_ell at\nearlier leaves, zero at every checkpoint, and mass 1 at z.\n\nFor a global reverse-cycle amount kappa>=0, the array is\n\n    lambda_(n,z)=2, lambda_(z,star)=1,\n    lambda_(z,p)=t_p, p in Check,\n    lambda_(p,ell)=(1+kappa)t_p*omega_(p,ell),\n    lambda_(ell,p)=kappa*t_p*omega_(p,ell), ell<p,\n    lambda_(ell,star)=mu_ell, ell in Leaf, ell<z.      (II.2)\n\nEvery unlisted entry is zero. The frozen law is kappa=1. All signs are\nnonnegative. One unit flows z->star and one flows z->p->ell->star.\nEvery reverse cycle has zero net flow. Thus (II.2) has exactly terminal\nflow 2, with no incoming terminal or optimum edge. In particular it\ncontains Q_(n-2,0) with a strictly positive coefficient.\n\nThe complete original-row expression is\n\n    M_II(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,z)+Q_(z,star)\n      +sum_p t_p[Q_(z,p)+sum_(ell<p)omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,star))]\n      +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)t_p*omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)). (II.3)\n\nThere are no omitted charges in this expression. Each finite row is its\noriginal ordered interpolation row. Equations (II.1)--(II.3) are a\ncoefficient law, not an instruction to solve for unknown PSD blocks.\n\n### 3.1 Complete quadratic, including all independent prefix gradients\n\nSet W_(p,ell)=t_p*omega_(p,ell). Eliminating only g_n gives\nM_II(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2+R_II, where\n\n    R_II=q||x_0||^2-<x_0,g_z+sum_(ell<z)mu_ell*g_ell>\n       +(2-(b-1)^2)||g_z||^2+sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell||g_ell||^2\n       -sum_p t_p<g_z,g_p>-sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,g_ell>\n       +<g_z,sum_(i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_p t_p<g_z,sum_(p<=i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell<g_ell,sum_(i<ell)h_i*g_i>\n       +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)\n          [||g_ell-g_p||^2\n                  -<g_ell-g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>]. (II.4)\n\nThe formula follows by expanding the four types of prefix rows in\n(II.3). For example, the two half-squared-difference contributions at p\ngive t_p||g_p||^2; the two at each leaf give mu_ell||g_ell||^2.\nBoth original displacements of each reverse cycle remain in the last line.\nNo common-gradient, terminal or initial-point relation is assumed.\n\n### 3.2 A two-checkpoint zero-displacement counterdirection\n\nFor a queried checkpoint r<p put\nOmega_p(r)=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<=r)omega_(p,ell). On the full original\nsubspace x_0=0, every leaf gradient zero and g_n=0, (II.4) becomes\n\n    (1+kappa)[sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(r<p in Check)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)<g_r,g_p>]. (II.5)\n\nThis is a necessary test of the full matrix, not a claim that checkpoint\ncompression is lossless. All omitted formal coordinates are explicitly\nset to zero only to exhibit a negative direction.\n\nChoose r=2^(m-2)-1 and p=3*2^(m-2)-1, the roots of the two children of\nthe whole word. Both physical steps equal H=1+a*c^(m-2), and t_r=t_p.\nThere are 2^(m-3) earlier leaves at or before r and 3*2^(m-3) before p.\nEvery raw weight is at least 1/m and less than 2, so\n\n    Omega_p(r)>1/(6m).\n\nAlso H>2^(m-2)/3>12m. For the latter comparison, 2^(m-2)>36m at\nm=16 and doubling preserves it thereafter. Consequently H*Omega_p(r)>2.\nSet the complete original vector to\n\n    x_0=0, g_r=1, g_p=-1, all other g_i=0, including g_n. (II.6)\n\nIts physical displacement is H-H=0. Equation (II.5) gives\n\n    M_II(q)=(1+kappa)t_p[2-H*Omega_p(r)]<0.           (II.7)\n\nThis holds for EVERY real q and EVERY kappa>=0. Paying a larger common\nreverse-cycle factor cannot repair the law.\n\nMore generally, for arbitrary positive checkpoint masses summing to 1 and receiver\nprobabilities with the same routing support and one common kappa, the\ntwo-coordinate determinant in (II.5) requires\n\n    h_r*Omega_p(r) <= 2*sqrt(t_r/t_p), r<p.          (II.8)\n\nIndeed its diagonal entries are (1+kappa)t_r and (1+kappa)t_p, and its\noff-diagonal entry is (1+kappa)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)/2. Thus comparable\ncheckpoint degrees need receiver leakage at most a constant divided by\nthe earlier physical step. The displayed raw kernel has only an inverse-m\nlower bound on that leakage, which is too large. This requirement alone\ndoes not certify any more localized law; all other directions remain.\n\n## 4. One global initial/common charge and the exact terminal account\n\nBoth laws have no incoming terminal row and lambda_(n,z)=2. Directly,\n\n    -2f_n+2Q_(n,z)\n       =||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2-2f_z+[1-(b-1)^2]||g_z||^2. (4.1)\n\nThus the true terminal is free, with center -(b-1)g_z. Nothing identifies\nit with the last queried gradient. The entire original prefix remains.\n\nFor either law let mu_i=lambda_(i,star), finite K_ij=lambda_ij, and h_n=0\nonly for notation. There are no incoming optimum rows and sum mu_i=2.\nThe complete polynomial is q||x_0||^2-sum mu_i<x_0,g_i>+g^T C g, with\n\n    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                      +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4.2)\n\nTo check this formula, the incident half-squared-gradient terms contribute\nmu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow; each forward row out of i contributes\n-h_i on that diagonal. For i<j the squared differences give the first\nterm, forward rows from i crossing j the second, and rows from j reaching\nindex <=i or star the third. This accounts for every original coordinate.\n\nEliminate g_n using (4.1), obtaining the explicit prefix matrix\nCpre=C_(0:z,0:z)-(b-1)^2 e_z e_z^T, where e_z selects coordinate z.\nLet g_i=G+v_i on the queried prefix, with v_z=0. Define\n\n    u=sum_(i<=z)mu_i*v_i, k=e^T Cpre v, T=v^T Cpre v,\n\nwhere e is the all-ones prefix column. The coefficients Cpre,mu are\nentirely specified by the two literal arrays and (4.2). Substitution of\nall prefix gradients G gives e^T Cpre e=A. Therefore the full prefix form\nis exactly\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,G>+A||G||^2-<x_0,u>+2<G,k>+T. (4.3)\n\nAt q=qtar the initial/common determinant Delta=qA-1 is positive, because\nD>17b^2. Eliminating this ONE two-coordinate block charges\n\n    Charge(u,k)=[A||u||^2/4-<u,k>+q||k||^2]/(qA-1). (4.4)\n\nThis follows by inversion of [[q,-1],[-1,A]] with cross column\n(-u/2,k). No separate local charge is substituted for it. Full PSD is\nequivalent to T>=Charge for every original prefix variation v_z=0.\nAt a singular determinant a range condition would additionally be needed;\nthe advertised qtar is not singular. For Law I, k=A*u/2 by (I.3), and\n(4.4) is exactly A||u||^2/4. Its minimizing initial/common values are\nx_0=0,G=-u/2, explaining why increasing q cannot repair (I.7).\nFor Law II, (4.2) and (II.4) retain k explicitly; (II.6) already gives\na negative value before any attempted elimination. No PSD claim is left\nto an unspecified matrix problem: both full matrices have been refuted.\n\n## 5. Complete reconstruction of the E80 reset transcript\n\nFor reuse as a SCREEN only, define Rreset=a*c^2/M=beta*c/M and sbit(k)\nto be the number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0. Put\n\n    v_k=(-1)^k Rreset^k c^(-sbit(k))/M, 0<=k<N.\n\nAt leaf 2k prescribe x=M v_k,g=v_k,f=(b+1/2)v_k^2. At checkpoint\n2k+1<n prescribe x=g=v_k,f=v_k^2/2. Binary carry gives\nsbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-v2(k+1), hence\nv_(k+1)/v_k=-a*c^(1+v2(k+1))/M. Thus the leaf step takes M v_k to v_k\nand the following actual checkpoint step takes v_k to M v_(k+1).\nEvery physical update is satisfied, x_0=1, and the final position is\n\n    t=x_n=v_(N-1)=-Rreset^(N-1)/(M*c^(m-1)).\n\nComplete the actual terminal by g_n=u=2t/(b+2), f_n=t^2/(b+2), and set\nthe optimum jet to zero. This is a scalar Gram transcript; no function is\nasserted to interpolate it. Substitution gives the full row table:\n\n    Q_(leaf(v),star)=0, Q_(star,leaf(v))=-b*v^2;\n    both optimum rows at check(w) are 0;\n    Q_(check(w),check(w'))=0;\n    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0;\n    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v);\n    Q_(leaf(v),leaf(w))=b*w*(v-w);\n    Q_(n,check(w))=Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=-b*u^2/4;\n    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2.                       (5.1)\n\nIn particular both adjacent queried directions are nonpositive: following\na leaf has w=v, while at the preceding checkpoint the next leaf parameter\nis -d*w/M with d>0. Every E80 row is accounted for by this table. E80 means\nboth optimum orientations at every finite index, all checkpoint pairs,\nall leaf-to-checkpoint rows, adjacent checkpoint-to-leaf rows, and all\noutgoing actual-terminal rows. No leaf-to-leaf or remote checkpoint-to-\nleaf rows belonged to E80.\n\nThe growth estimate is included, rather than imported from the old draft.\nSince M=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n\n    Rreset-1=epsilon[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/M\n              >epsilon/8=1/(32m).\n\nFor the strict bound, epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 make the bracket exceed\n32/45-5/16>5/16, while M<5/2. For m>=16, induction gives\n2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2: the base is 32767>=24576 and the induction difference\n96m^2-192m-95 is positive. Hence N-1>32m(2m+1).\nThe binomial inequality (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2 yields\nRreset^(N-1)>2^(2m+1). Because M*c^(m-1)<2^(2m), we have t<-2.\n\nThis transcript is explicitly not interpolable. At pbad=n-2 the checkpoint\nparameter w=v_(N-2)=-M*t/beta>2M, whereas v_0=1/M. Consequently\n\n    Q_(pbad,0)=b*v_0*(w-v_0)>0, Q_(0,pbad)=0.        (5.2)\n\nLaw I omits this row. Law II includes it with positive weight. The new\nfailure below is insufficient paid action even though that violated row\nis now present. Neither (5.1) nor (5.2) is a lower bound on unrestricted W.\n\n### 5.1 A symbolic tail bound; no trajectory or recurrence was executed\n\nFor k=N-1-j, binary complementation gives exactly\n\n    |v_k/t|=Rreset^(-j)c^sbit(j), 1<=j<N.\n\nSince an integer with r binary ones is at least 2^r-1,\nc^(2sbit(j))<=(j+1)^4. Put rreset=Rreset^(-2). The previous lower bound\non Rreset gives 1-rreset>=1/(64m): with zeta=1/(32m)<1, use\n1-(1+zeta)^(-2)>=zeta/2.\nFor every integer d>=1,\nj^d<=d!*binom(j+d-1,d), and summing the geometric-series identity gives\nsum_(j>=1)j^d r^j<=d!/(1-r)^(d+1), 0<r<1. This identity follows by\nmultiplying d+1 geometric series and counting compositions, or by d\nsuccessive differentiations of their convergent series. Therefore\n\n    sum_(k<N-1)v_k^2/t^2 <=768(64m)^5=:Moment5,\n    sum_(p=2k+1 in Check)h_p*v_k^2/t^2\n                                  <=92160(64m)^7=:Moment7. (5.3)\n\nFor the second bound, v2(k+1)=v2(j), so\nh_p=1+beta*c^v2(j)<=1+j^2<=2j^2. Apply the d=6 inequality to\n2j^2(j+1)^4<=128j^6. For the first use (j+1)^4<=16j^4; the displayed\nconstant 768 is a harmless enlargement of 16*4!.\n\nAll coarse exponential comparisons used below follow from\n\n    2^(m-1)>2^24(64m)^7, m>=1536.                    (5.4)\n\nAt m=1536, m<2^11 makes the right side less than 2^143, whereas the\nleft side is 2^1535. The ratio of the left side to m^7 increases at each\ninteger increment, since (1+1/m)^7<sum_(j>=0)(7/m)^j<2 for m>=1536.\nThis proves (5.4) for all relevant m by induction. This is symbolic\nreasoning, not a generated arithmetic table or executed recurrence.\n\n## 6. Complete reset screen for both frozen laws\n\nOn (5.1), Law I has exact value\n\n    M_I(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2\n      +b*sum_(earlier leaves ell=2k)\n           [r81_ell*v_k*(t-v_k)-w81_ell*(t-v_k)^2]\n      +b*sum_(checkpoints p=2k+1)w81_p*t*(v_k-t). These are scoped coefficient obstructions, not W bounds.\n\n## 1. Scope, accepted inputs and literal hierarchy\n\nFor every n>=0, L>0 and predetermined h in [0,infinity)^n, W_n(h) is the\nsupremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\ndimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,\nevery such x_star and x_0!=x_star, with all n actual updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns\nlim_n B_n inf_h W_n(h), with every nonnegative word, including zero and\narbitrarily large steps. No result below changes those quantifiers.\n\nThe benchmark is the original all-integer-split recurrence\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290, read in full, supplies the original interpolation\norientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation and exact\nactual-terminal completion. It deletes no queried-prefix inequalities.\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201, read in full, supplies B_n/D<47/50 for the\nfollowing SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy. Its implication from W<=17/(16D)\nis conditional; that hypothesis is still unproved. No other accepted\nmathematical result is used here. Prior finding0073a83e08ff2e21 was read\nin full as awareness; the reset construction needed below is rederived.\n\nFix m>=1536. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    z=n-1, b=1+a, M=1+b, N=2^(m-1), A=D-b^2,\n    P81_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, Tail81_i=S-b-P81_i (i<z),\n    K81_i=A-P81_i-1=2S-b^2-P81_i (i<z), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nAll children use this same m. Index z is the last queried leaf, not the\nunqueried terminal n. All these hierarchy symbols are local to this report.\nLet level(i)=v2(i+1), the exponent of 2 dividing i+1. Then\nh_i=1+a*c^level(i), leaves are the even indices, and checkpoints the odd\nindices. In particular h_z=b. Write Leaf={0,2,...,z}, Check={1,3,...,n-2}.\n\nElementary bounds used throughout are\n\n    1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<sqrt(2)<3/2,\n    12/5<c<5/2, beta>7/8,\n    d_1=beta, d_2=beta*c>21/10,\n    S>2^m-1, D>2^m, A>0, K81_i>Tail81_i>0.\n\nFor example a>1/3 follows from beta>7/8 and c<5/2. The bound on c\nfollows from lambda<17/7 and epsilon<=1/6144. Also\nb=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]. The other comparisons follow by\nsubstitution. K81_i-Tail81_i=S+b-b^2>0 since b<2 and S>=b. These observations\ninclude positivity of every denominator in the arrays below.\n\nDefine a dyadic cell of height j to be\n\n    I_(j,r)={r*2^j,...,(r+1)*2^j-2},\n    1<=j<=m, 0<=r<2^(m-j).\n\nFor distinct queried indices u,v, J(u,v) is the least height of a cell\ncontaining both. Such a cell exists and its height is unique. This defines\nthe exact least common dyadic ancestor in ORIGINAL indices. For i in the\nleft half, including the whole-word root, J(i,z)=m.\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and retain independent original\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n. Positions are always\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct labels put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal samples have Q_ij<=0. An original coefficient array with\nout_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) defines\n\n    M_cert(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nAfter value cancellation, it must be PSD on ALL original vectors to be an\nupper certificate. A negative formal vector is not a primal instance.\n\n## 2. Mechanism I: position-sensitive remote routing to the last leaf\n\nFirst construct the entire family with independently chosen 0<=theta_i<=1\nfor each i<z. Set\n\n    r81_i=2theta_i*h_i/K81_i,\n    w81_i=2(1-theta_i)*h_i/Tail81_i.\n\nThe frozen LCA-sensitive law tested in Sections 6--8 is\n\n    eta=1/(4D), theta_i=1-eta/J(i,z).                    (I.1)\n\nEvery remote pair then has positive weight. The literal nonzero array is\n\n    lambda_(n,z)=2,\n    lambda_(z,star)=2-sum_(i<z)r81_i,\n    lambda_(i,star)=r81_i,\n    lambda_(z,i)=r81_i+w81_i, lambda_(i,z)=w81_i, i<z.       (I.2)\n\nAll unlisted rows are zero, including all incoming terminal and optimum\nrows. This array includes checkpoint-to-last-leaf rows at arbitrary\ndistance and leaf-to-last-leaf rows. Its support is not contained in E80.\n\nAll signs can be checked without an unknown feasibility condition. Put\nKbase=S+b-b^2>0. Since K81_i=Kbase+Tail81_i, and Tail81_i decreases by h_i as i\nincreases, integration of the decreasing function 1/(Kbase+t) gives\n\n    sum_(i<z) h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i)\n      <= integral_0^(S-b) dt/(Kbase+t)\n      =log((2S-b^2)/(S+b-b^2)) <log 2<1.\n\nIndeed h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i) is bounded by the integral over\n[Tail81_i-h_i,Tail81_i], and these intervals partition [0,S-b]. The ratio is below\n2 because b<2. Thus sum r81_i<2 for every allowed theta. Every coefficient\nin (I.2) is nonnegative, with strict outgoing optimum mass at z.\nAt i<z the two finite directions have net inflow r81_i, exactly discharged\nto star. At z the finite/optimum outgoing net is 2, supplied by (n,z).\nTerminal flow is 2. This proves all value cancellations.\n\nThis is a new source-routing base, not an addition to any inherited\ncertificate. Its terminal distribution is concentrated at the actual last\nleaf; its entire optimum distribution is given in (I.2).\n\n### 2.1 Full polynomial and the paid contact equation\n\nWrite g=g_z, v_i=g_i-g for i<z and u=sum r81_i v_i. Put\n\n    B(v)=sum_(i<z)[r81_i+w81_i(1-h_i)]||v_i||^2\n         +sum_(i<t<z)(h_i*r81_t-w81_i*h_t)<v_i,v_t>,\n    Jrem(v)=B(v)-A||u||^2/4.\n\nThe exact full identity, for EVERY real q, is\n\n    M_I(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n       +q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2\n       -<x_0,u>+A<g,u>+B(v).                         (I.3)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. Starting with rows 2Q_(n,z)+2Q_(z,star),\nreplace r81_i units of Q_(z,star) by Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star), and add w81_i\nunits of Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i). The two added expressions, after GD, are\n\n    Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star)-Q_(z,star)\n       =-<v_i,x_0>+(P81_i+1)<v_i,g>\n                          +||v_i||^2+sum_(t<i)h_t<v_i,v_t>,\n    Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i)\n       =||v_i||^2-Tail81_i<v_i,g>-sum_(i<=t<z)h_t<v_i,v_t>.\n\nThe original base has terminal square ||g_n+(b-1)g||^2 and remainder\nq||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2+2sum h_i<v_i,g>.\nThe defining relation\n\n    w81_i*Tail81_i=2h_i-K81_i*r81_i                              (I.4)\n\nchanges each last cross coefficient to A*r81_i, giving (I.3). In particular,\nremote symmetric pairs were NOT treated as PSD. Their negative diagonal\nand mixed displacement terms appear in B.\n\nAt q=1/A, (I.3) is equivalently\n\n    M_I(1/A)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n      +||x_0-A*g-(A/2)u||^2/A+Jrem(v).               (I.5)\n\nEquation (I.4) is an intentional design restriction of this family. It is\nnot claimed necessary for a general certificate. No zero-contact rule is\nimposed on an unrestricted optimum. The term -A||u||^2/4 is the ONE\nglobal initial/common-gradient charge for this deliberately balanced law.\n\n### 2.2 Full-space failure for every member of the contact family\n\nTake p=2^(m-1)-1, H=h_p>4. Only v_p is nonzero, with v_p=1. The coefficient\nof Jrem on this vector is\n\n    E_p=r81_p-w81_p(H-1)-A*r81_p^2/4.                       (I.6)\n\nIf theta_p<=1/2, then r81_p<=H/K81_p and w81_p>=H/Tail81_p. Thus\nr81_p-w81_p(H-1)<=H/K81_p-H(H-1)/Tail81_p<0 because K81_p>Tail81_p and H>2.\nIf theta_p>=1/2, then r81_p>=H/K81_p>H/A>4/A, and\nr81_p-A*r81_p^2/4<0; subtracting w81_p(H-1)>=0 preserves negativity.\nSo E_p<0 for EVERY choice of the whole profile theta.\n\nThe complete scalar original-vector counterdirection is\n\n    x_0=0,\n    g_i=-r81_p/2 for every queried i!=p,\n    g_p=1-r81_p/2,\n    g_n=(b-1)r81_p/2.                                  (I.7)\n\nIt sets g=-r81_p/2, u=r81_p, makes the terminal square and the square in\n(I.5) zero, and gives M_I(q)=E_p<0 for every real q. The change from\n1/A to q multiplies ||x_0||^2=0. Hence even unlimited initial-norm slack\ncannot repair this coefficient family. This is stronger than rejection\nof the frozen small-eta profile, but only for family (I.2)--(I.4).\n\n## 3. Mechanism II: dense earlier-leaf transport with paid reverse cycles\n\nPut Tc=sum_(p in Check)h_p and t_p=h_p/Tc, so sum t_p=1. For EVERY\ncheckpoint p and earlier leaf ell<p define\n\n    raw_(p,ell)=[1+(p-ell)/(p+1)]/J(p,ell),\n    Z_p=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<p)raw_(p,ell),\n    omega_(p,ell)=raw_(p,ell)/Z_p,\n    mu_ell=sum_(p in Check,p>ell)t_p*omega_(p,ell).    (II.1)\n\nThere is at least one earlier leaf for every checkpoint, so Z_p>0.\nThe weights depend on the exact least common ancestor and position within\nthe enclosing chronological range. They are not uniform fan averages.\nThe formula for mu_ell is used only for ell<z. The new optimum distribution is mu_ell at\nearlier leaves, zero at every checkpoint, and mass 1 at z.\n\nFor a global reverse-cycle amount kappa>=0, the array is\n\n    lambda_(n,z)=2, lambda_(z,star)=1,\n    lambda_(z,p)=t_p, p in Check,\n    lambda_(p,ell)=(1+kappa)t_p*omega_(p,ell),\n    lambda_(ell,p)=kappa*t_p*omega_(p,ell), ell<p,\n    lambda_(ell,star)=mu_ell, ell in Leaf, ell<z.      (II.2)\n\nEvery unlisted entry is zero. The frozen law is kappa=1. All signs are\nnonnegative. One unit flows z->star and one flows z->p->ell->star.\nEvery reverse cycle has zero net flow. Thus (II.2) has exactly terminal\nflow 2, with no incoming terminal or optimum edge. In particular it\ncontains Q_(n-2,0) with a strictly positive coefficient.\n\nThe complete original-row expression is\n\n    M_II(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,z)+Q_(z,star)\n      +sum_p t_p[Q_(z,p)+sum_(ell<p)omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,star))]\n      +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)t_p*omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)). (II.3)\n\nThere are no omitted charges in this expression. Each finite row is its\noriginal ordered interpolation row. Equations (II.1)--(II.3) are a\ncoefficient law, not an instruction to solve for unknown PSD blocks.\n\n### 3.1 Complete quadratic, including all independent prefix gradients\n\nSet W_(p,ell)=t_p*omega_(p,ell). Eliminating only g_n gives\nM_II(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2+R_II, where\n\n    R_II=q||x_0||^2-<x_0,g_z+sum_(ell<z)mu_ell*g_ell>\n       +(2-(b-1)^2)||g_z||^2+sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell||g_ell||^2\n       -sum_p t_p<g_z,g_p>-sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,g_ell>\n       +<g_z,sum_(i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_p t_p<g_z,sum_(p<=i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell<g_ell,sum_(i<ell)h_i*g_i>\n       +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)\n          [||g_ell-g_p||^2\n                  -<g_ell-g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>]. (II.4)\n\nThe formula follows by expanding the four types of prefix rows in\n(II.3). For example, the two half-squared-difference contributions at p\ngive t_p||g_p||^2; the two at each leaf give mu_ell||g_ell||^2.\nBoth original displacements of each reverse cycle remain in the last line.\nNo common-gradient, terminal or initial-point relation is assumed.\n\n### 3.2 A two-checkpoint zero-displacement counterdirection\n\nFor a queried checkpoint r<p put\nOmega_p(r)=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<=r)omega_(p,ell). On the full original\nsubspace x_0=0, every leaf gradient zero and g_n=0, (II.4) becomes\n\n    (1+kappa)[sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(r<p in Check)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)<g_r,g_p>]. (II.5)\n\nThis is a necessary test of the full matrix, not a claim that checkpoint\ncompression is lossless. All omitted formal coordinates are explicitly\nset to zero only to exhibit a negative direction.\n\nChoose r=2^(m-2)-1 and p=3*2^(m-2)-1, the roots of the two children of\nthe whole word. Both physical steps equal H=1+a*c^(m-2), and t_r=t_p.\nThere are 2^(m-3) earlier leaves at or before r and 3*2^(m-3) before p.\nEvery raw weight is at least 1/m and less than 2, so\n\n    Omega_p(r)>1/(6m).\n\nAlso H>2^(m-2)/3>12m. For the latter comparison, 2^(m-2)>36m at\nm=16 and doubling preserves it thereafter. Consequently H*Omega_p(r)>2.\nSet the complete original vector to\n\n    x_0=0, g_r=1, g_p=-1, all other g_i=0, including g_n. (II.6)\n\nIts physical displacement is H-H=0. Equation (II.5) gives\n\n    M_II(q)=(1+kappa)t_p[2-H*Omega_p(r)]<0.           (II.7)\n\nThis holds for EVERY real q and EVERY kappa>=0. Paying a larger common\nreverse-cycle factor cannot repair the law.\n\nMore generally, for arbitrary positive checkpoint masses summing to 1 and receiver\nprobabilities with the same routing support and one common kappa, the\ntwo-coordinate determinant in (II.5) requires\n\n    h_r*Omega_p(r) <= 2*sqrt(t_r/t_p), r<p.          (II.8)\n\nIndeed its diagonal entries are (1+kappa)t_r and (1+kappa)t_p, and its\noff-diagonal entry is (1+kappa)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)/2. Thus comparable\ncheckpoint degrees need receiver leakage at most a constant divided by\nthe earlier physical step. The displayed raw kernel has only an inverse-m\nlower bound on that leakage, which is too large. This requirement alone\ndoes not certify any more localized law; all other directions remain.\n\n## 4. One global initial/common charge and the exact terminal account\n\nBoth laws have no incoming terminal row and lambda_(n,z)=2. Directly,\n\n    -2f_n+2Q_(n,z)\n       =||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2-2f_z+[1-(b-1)^2]||g_z||^2. (4.1)\n\nThus the true terminal is free, with center -(b-1)g_z. Nothing identifies\nit with the last queried gradient. The entire original prefix remains.\n\nFor either law let mu_i=lambda_(i,star), finite K_ij=lambda_ij, and h_n=0\nonly for notation. There are no incoming optimum rows and sum mu_i=2.\nThe complete polynomial is q||x_0||^2-sum mu_i<x_0,g_i>+g^T C g, with\n\n    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                      +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4.2)\n\nTo check this formula, the incident half-squared-gradient terms contribute\nmu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow; each forward row out of i contributes\n-h_i on that diagonal. For i<j the squared differences give the first\nterm, forward rows from i crossing j the second, and rows from j reaching\nindex <=i or star the third. This accounts for every original coordinate.\n\nEliminate g_n using (4.1), obtaining the explicit prefix matrix\nCpre=C_(0:z,0:z)-(b-1)^2 e_z e_z^T, where e_z selects coordinate z.\nLet g_i=G+v_i on the queried prefix, with v_z=0. Define\n\n    u=sum_(i<=z)mu_i*v_i, k=e^T Cpre v, T=v^T Cpre v,\n\nwhere e is the all-ones prefix column. The coefficients Cpre,mu are\nentirely specified by the two literal arrays and (4.2). Substitution of\nall prefix gradients G gives e^T Cpre e=A. Therefore the full prefix form\nis exactly\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,G>+A||G||^2-<x_0,u>+2<G,k>+T. (4.3)\n\nAt q=qtar the initial/common determinant Delta=qA-1 is positive, because\nD>17b^2. Eliminating this ONE two-coordinate block charges\n\n    Charge(u,k)=[A||u||^2/4-<u,k>+q||k||^2]/(qA-1). (4.4)\n\nThis follows by inversion of [[q,-1],[-1,A]] with cross column\n(-u/2,k). No separate local charge is substituted for it. Full PSD is\nequivalent to T>=Charge for every original prefix variation v_z=0.\nAt a singular determinant a range condition would additionally be needed;\nthe advertised qtar is not singular. For Law I, k=A*u/2 by (I.3), and\n(4.4) is exactly A||u||^2/4. Its minimizing initial/common values are\nx_0=0,G=-u/2, explaining why increasing q cannot repair (I.7).\nFor Law II, (4.2) and (II.4) retain k explicitly; (II.6) already gives\na negative value before any attempted elimination. No PSD claim is left\nto an unspecified matrix problem: both full matrices have been refuted.\n\n## 5. Complete reconstruction of the E80 reset transcript\n\nFor reuse as a SCREEN only, define Rreset=a*c^2/M=beta*c/M and sbit(k)\nto be the number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0. Put\n\n    v_k=(-1)^k Rreset^k c^(-sbit(k))/M, 0<=k<N.\n\nAt leaf 2k prescribe x=M v_k,g=v_k,f=(b+1/2)v_k^2. At checkpoint\n2k+1<n prescribe x=g=v_k,f=v_k^2/2. Binary carry gives\nsbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-v2(k+1), hence\nv_(k+1)/v_k=-a*c^(1+v2(k+1))/M. Thus the leaf step takes M v_k to v_k\nand the following actual checkpoint step takes v_k to M v_(k+1).\nEvery physical update is satisfied, x_0=1, and the final position is\n\n    t=x_n=v_(N-1)=-Rreset^(N-1)/(M*c^(m-1)).\n\nComplete the actual terminal by g_n=u=2t/(b+2), f_n=t^2/(b+2), and set\nthe optimum jet to zero. This is a scalar Gram transcript; no function is\nasserted to interpolate it. Substitution gives the full row table:\n\n    Q_(leaf(v),star)=0, Q_(star,leaf(v))=-b*v^2;\n    both optimum rows at check(w) are 0;\n    Q_(check(w),check(w'))=0;\n    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0;\n    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v);\n    Q_(leaf(v),leaf(w))=b*w*(v-w);\n    Q_(n,check(w))=Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=-b*u^2/4;\n    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2.                       (5.1)\n\nIn particular both adjacent queried directions are nonpositive: following\na leaf has w=v, while at the preceding checkpoint the next leaf parameter\nis -d*w/M with d>0. Every E80 row is accounted for by this table. E80 means\nboth optimum orientations at every finite index, all checkpoint pairs,\nall leaf-to-checkpoint rows, adjacent checkpoint-to-leaf rows, and all\noutgoing actual-terminal rows. No leaf-to-leaf or remote checkpoint-to-\nleaf rows belonged to E80.\n\nThe growth estimate is included, rather than imported from the old draft.\nSince M=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n\n    Rreset-1=epsilon[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/M\n              >epsilon/8=1/(32m).\n\nFor the strict bound, epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 make the bracket exceed\n32/45-5/16>5/16, while M<5/2. For m>=16, induction gives\n2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2: the base is 32767>=24576 and the induction difference\n96m^2-192m-95 is positive. Hence N-1>32m(2m+1).\nThe binomial inequality (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2 yields\nRreset^(N-1)>2^(2m+1). Because M*c^(m-1)<2^(2m), we have t<-2.\n\nThis transcript is explicitly not interpolable. At pbad=n-2 the checkpoint\nparameter w=v_(N-2)=-M*t/beta>2M, whereas v_0=1/M. Consequently\n\n    Q_(pbad,0)=b*v_0*(w-v_0)>0, Q_(0,pbad)=0.        (5.2)\n\nLaw I omits this row. Law II includes it with positive weight. The new\nfailure below is insufficient paid action even though that violated row\nis now present. Neither (5.1) nor (5.2) is a lower bound on unrestricted W.\n\n### 5.1 A symbolic tail bound; no trajectory or recurrence was executed\n\nFor k=N-1-j, binary complementation gives exactly\n\n    |v_k/t|=Rreset^(-j)c^sbit(j), 1<=j<N.\n\nSince an integer with r binary ones is at least 2^r-1,\nc^(2sbit(j))<=(j+1)^4. Put rreset=Rreset^(-2). The previous lower bound\non Rreset gives 1-rreset>=1/(64m): with zeta=1/(32m)<1, use\n1-(1+zeta)^(-2)>=zeta/2.\nFor every integer d>=1,\nj^d<=d!*binom(j+d-1,d), and summing the geometric-series identity gives\nsum_(j>=1)j^d r^j<=d!/(1-r)^(d+1), 0<r<1. This identity follows by\nmultiplying d+1 geometric series and counting compositions, or by d\nsuccessive differentiations of their convergent series. Therefore\n\n    sum_(k<N-1)v_k^2/t^2 <=768(64m)^5=:Moment5,\n    sum_(p=2k+1 in Check)h_p*v_k^2/t^2\n                                  <=92160(64m)^7=:Moment7. (5.3)\n\nFor the second bound, v2(k+1)=v2(j), so\nh_p=1+beta*c^v2(j)<=1+j^2<=2j^2. Apply the d=6 inequality to\n2j^2(j+1)^4<=128j^6. For the first use (j+1)^4<=16j^4; the displayed\nconstant 768 is a harmless enlargement of 16*4!.\n\nAll coarse exponential comparisons used below follow from\n\n    2^(m-1)>2^24(64m)^7, m>=1536.                    (5.4)\n\nAt m=1536, m<2^11 makes the right side less than 2^143, whereas the\nleft side is 2^1535. The ratio of the left side to m^7 increases at each\ninteger increment, since (1+1/m)^7<sum_(j>=0)(7/m)^j<2 for m>=1536.\nThis proves (5.4) for all relevant m by induction. This is symbolic\nreasoning, not a generated arithmetic table or executed recurrence.\n\n## 6. Complete reset screen for both frozen laws\n\nOn (5.1), Law I has exact value\n\n    M_I(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2\n      +b*sum_(earlier leaves ell=2k)\n           [r81_ell*v_k*(t-v_k)-w81_ell*(t-v_k)^2]\n      +b*sum_(checkpoints p=2k+1)w81_p*t*(v_k-t).
