Its terminal distribution is concentrated at the actual last\nleaf; its entire optimum distribution is given in (I.2).\n\n### 2.1 Full polynomial and the paid contact equation\n\nWrite g=g_z, v_i=g_i-g for i<z and u=sum r81_i v_i. Put\n\n    B(v)=sum_(i<z)[r81_i+w81_i(1-h_i)]||v_i||^2\n         +sum_(i<t<z)(h_i*r81_t-w81_i*h_t)<v_i,v_t>,\n    Jrem(v)=B(v)-A||u||^2/4.\n\nThe exact full identity, for EVERY real q, is\n\n    M_I(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n       +q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2\n       -<x_0,u>+A<g,u>+B(v).                         (I.3)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. Starting with rows 2Q_(n,z)+2Q_(z,star),\nreplace r81_i units of Q_(z,star) by Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star), and add w81_i\nunits of Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i). The two added expressions, after GD, are\n\n    Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star)-Q_(z,star)\n       =-<v_i,x_0>+(P81_i+1)<v_i,g>\n                          +||v_i||^2+sum_(t<i)h_t<v_i,v_t>,\n    Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i)\n       =||v_i||^2-Tail81_i<v_i,g>-sum_(i<=t<z)h_t<v_i,v_t>.\n\nThe original base has terminal square ||g_n+(b-1)g||^2 and remainder\nq||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2+2sum h_i<v_i,g>.\nThe defining relation\n\n    w81_i*Tail81_i=2h_i-K81_i*r81_i                              (I.4)\n\nchanges each last cross coefficient to A*r81_i, giving (I.3). In particular,\nremote symmetric pairs were NOT treated as PSD. Their negative diagonal\nand mixed displacement terms appear in B.\n\nAt q=1/A, (I.3) is equivalently\n\n    M_I(1/A)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n      +||x_0-A*g-(A/2)u||^2/A+Jrem(v).               (I.5)\n\nEquation (I.4) is an intentional design restriction of this family. It is\nnot claimed necessary for a general certificate. No zero-contact rule is\nimposed on an unrestricted optimum. The term -A||u||^2/4 is the ONE\nglobal initial/common-gradient charge for this deliberately balanced law.\n\n### 2.2 Full-space failure for every member of the contact family\n\nTake p=2^(m-1)-1, H=h_p>4. Only v_p is nonzero, with v_p=1. The coefficient\nof Jrem on this vector is\n\n    E_p=r81_p-w81_p(H-1)-A*r81_p^2/4.                       (I.6)\n\nIf theta_p<=1/2, then r81_p<=H/K81_p and w81_p>=H/Tail81_p. Thus\nr81_p-w81_p(H-1)<=H/K81_p-H(H-1)/Tail81_p<0 because K81_p>Tail81_p and H>2.\nIf theta_p>=1/2, then r81_p>=H/K81_p>H/A>4/A, and\nr81_p-A*r81_p^2/4<0; subtracting w81_p(H-1)>=0 preserves negativity.\nSo E_p<0 for EVERY choice of the whole profile theta.\n\nThe complete scalar original-vector counterdirection is\n\n    x_0=0,\n    g_i=-r81_p/2 for every queried i!=p,\n    g_p=1-r81_p/2,\n    g_n=(b-1)r81_p/2.                                  (I.7)\n\nIt sets g=-r81_p/2, u=r81_p, makes the terminal square and the square in\n(I.5) zero, and gives M_I(q)=E_p<0 for every real q. The change from\n1/A to q multiplies ||x_0||^2=0. Hence even unlimited initial-norm slack\ncannot repair this coefficient family. This is stronger than rejection\nof the frozen small-eta profile, but only for family (I.2)--(I.4).\n\n## 3. Mechanism II: dense earlier-leaf transport with paid reverse cycles\n\nPut Tc=sum_(p in Check)h_p and t_p=h_p/Tc, so sum t_p=1. For EVERY\ncheckpoint p and earlier leaf ell<p define\n\n    raw_(p,ell)=[1+(p-ell)/(p+1)]/J(p,ell),\n    Z_p=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<p)raw_(p,ell),\n    omega_(p,ell)=raw_(p,ell)/Z_p,\n    mu_ell=sum_(p in Check,p>ell)t_p*omega_(p,ell).    (II.1)\n\nThere is at least one earlier leaf for every checkpoint, so Z_p>0.\nThe weights depend on the exact least common ancestor and position within\nthe enclosing chronological range. They are not uniform fan averages.\nThe formula for mu_ell is used only for ell<z. The new optimum distribution is mu_ell at\nearlier leaves, zero at every checkpoint, and mass 1 at z.\n\nFor a global reverse-cycle amount kappa>=0, the array is\n\n    lambda_(n,z)=2, lambda_(z,star)=1,\n    lambda_(z,p)=t_p, p in Check,\n    lambda_(p,ell)=(1+kappa)t_p*omega_(p,ell),\n    lambda_(ell,p)=kappa*t_p*omega_(p,ell), ell<p,\n    lambda_(ell,star)=mu_ell, ell in Leaf, ell<z.      (II.2)\n\nEvery unlisted entry is zero. The frozen law is kappa=1. All signs are\nnonnegative. One unit flows z->star and one flows z->p->ell->star.\nEvery reverse cycle has zero net flow. Thus (II.2) has exactly terminal\nflow 2, with no incoming terminal or optimum edge. In particular it\ncontains Q_(n-2,0) with a strictly positive coefficient.\n\nThe complete original-row expression is\n\n    M_II(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,z)+Q_(z,star)\n      +sum_p t_p[Q_(z,p)+sum_(ell<p)omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,star))]\n      +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)t_p*omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)). (II.3)\n\nThere are no omitted charges in this expression. Each finite row is its\noriginal ordered interpolation row. Equations (II.1)--(II.3) are a\ncoefficient law, not an instruction to solve for unknown PSD blocks.\n\n### 3.1 Complete quadratic, including all independent prefix gradients\n\nSet W_(p,ell)=t_p*omega_(p,ell). Eliminating only g_n gives\nM_II(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2+R_II, where\n\n    R_II=q||x_0||^2-<x_0,g_z+sum_(ell<z)mu_ell*g_ell>\n       +(2-(b-1)^2)||g_z||^2+sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell||g_ell||^2\n       -sum_p t_p<g_z,g_p>-sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,g_ell>\n       +<g_z,sum_(i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_p t_p<g_z,sum_(p<=i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell<g_ell,sum_(i<ell)h_i*g_i>\n       +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)\n          [||g_ell-g_p||^2\n                  -<g_ell-g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>]. (II.4)\n\nThe formula follows by expanding the four types of prefix rows in\n(II.3). For example, the two half-squared-difference contributions at p\ngive t_p||g_p||^2; the two at each leaf give mu_ell||g_ell||^2.\nBoth original displacements of each reverse cycle remain in the last line.\nNo common-gradient, terminal or initial-point relation is assumed.\n\n### 3.2 A two-checkpoint zero-displacement counterdirection\n\nFor a queried checkpoint r<p put\nOmega_p(r)=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<=r)omega_(p,ell). On the full original\nsubspace x_0=0, every leaf gradient zero and g_n=0, (II.4) becomes\n\n    (1+kappa)[sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(r<p in Check)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)<g_r,g_p>]. (II.5)\n\nThis is a necessary test of the full matrix, not a claim that checkpoint\ncompression is lossless. All omitted formal coordinates are explicitly\nset to zero only to exhibit a negative direction.\n\nChoose r=2^(m-2)-1 and p=3*2^(m-2)-1, the roots of the two children of\nthe whole word. Both physical steps equal H=1+a*c^(m-2), and t_r=t_p.\nThere are 2^(m-3) earlier leaves at or before r and 3*2^(m-3) before p.\nEvery raw weight is at least 1/m and less than 2, so\n\n    Omega_p(r)>1/(6m).\n\nAlso H>2^(m-2)/3>12m. For the latter comparison, 2^(m-2)>36m at\nm=16 and doubling preserves it thereafter. Consequently H*Omega_p(r)>2.\nSet the complete original vector to\n\n    x_0=0, g_r=1, g_p=-1, all other g_i=0, including g_n. (II.6)\n\nIts physical displacement is H-H=0. Equation (II.5) gives\n\n    M_II(q)=(1+kappa)t_p[2-H*Omega_p(r)]<0.           (II.7)\n\nThis holds for EVERY real q and EVERY kappa>=0. Paying a larger common\nreverse-cycle factor cannot repair the law.\n\nMore generally, for arbitrary positive checkpoint masses summing to 1 and receiver\nprobabilities with the same routing support and one common kappa, the\ntwo-coordinate determinant in (II.5) requires\n\n    h_r*Omega_p(r) <= 2*sqrt(t_r/t_p), r<p.          (II.8)\n\nIndeed its diagonal entries are (1+kappa)t_r and (1+kappa)t_p, and its\noff-diagonal entry is (1+kappa)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)/2. Thus comparable\ncheckpoint degrees need receiver leakage at most a constant divided by\nthe earlier physical step. The displayed raw kernel has only an inverse-m\nlower bound on that leakage, which is too large. This requirement alone\ndoes not certify any more localized law; all other directions remain.\n\n## 4. One global initial/common charge and the exact terminal account\n\nBoth laws have no incoming terminal row and lambda_(n,z)=2. Directly,\n\n    -2f_n+2Q_(n,z)\n       =||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2-2f_z+[1-(b-1)^2]||g_z||^2. (4.1)\n\nThus the true terminal is free, with center -(b-1)g_z. Nothing identifies\nit with the last queried gradient. The entire original prefix remains.\n\nFor either law let mu_i=lambda_(i,star), finite K_ij=lambda_ij, and h_n=0\nonly for notation. There are no incoming optimum rows and sum mu_i=2.\nThe complete polynomial is q||x_0||^2-sum mu_i<x_0,g_i>+g^T C g, with\n\n    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                      +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4.2)\n\nTo check this formula, the incident half-squared-gradient terms contribute\nmu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow; each forward row out of i contributes\n-h_i on that diagonal. For i<j the squared differences give the first\nterm, forward rows from i crossing j the second, and rows from j reaching\nindex <=i or star the third. This accounts for every original coordinate.\n\nEliminate g_n using (4.1), obtaining the explicit prefix matrix\nCpre=C_(0:z,0:z)-(b-1)^2 e_z e_z^T, where e_z selects coordinate z.\nLet g_i=G+v_i on the queried prefix, with v_z=0. Define\n\n    u=sum_(i<=z)mu_i*v_i, k=e^T Cpre v, T=v^T Cpre v,\n\nwhere e is the all-ones prefix column. The coefficients Cpre,mu are\nentirely specified by the two literal arrays and (4.2). Substitution of\nall prefix gradients G gives e^T Cpre e=A. Therefore the full prefix form\nis exactly\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,G>+A||G||^2-<x_0,u>+2<G,k>+T. (4.3)\n\nAt q=qtar the initial/common determinant Delta=qA-1 is positive, because\nD>17b^2. Eliminating this ONE two-coordinate block charges\n\n    Charge(u,k)=[A||u||^2/4-<u,k>+q||k||^2]/(qA-1). (4.4)\n\nThis follows by inversion of [[q,-1],[-1,A]] with cross column\n(-u/2,k). No separate local charge is substituted for it. Full PSD is\nequivalent to T>=Charge for every original prefix variation v_z=0.\nAt a singular determinant a range condition would additionally be needed;\nthe advertised qtar is not singular. For Law I, k=A*u/2 by (I.3), and\n(4.4) is exactly A||u||^2/4. Its minimizing initial/common values are\nx_0=0,G=-u/2, explaining why increasing q cannot repair (I.7).\nFor Law II, (4.2) and (II.4) retain k explicitly; (II.6) already gives\na negative value before any attempted elimination. No PSD claim is left\nto an unspecified matrix problem: both full matrices have been refuted.\n\n## 5. Complete reconstruction of the E80 reset transcript\n\nFor reuse as a SCREEN only, define Rreset=a*c^2/M=beta*c/M and sbit(k)\nto be the number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0. Put\n\n    v_k=(-1)^k Rreset^k c^(-sbit(k))/M, 0<=k<N.\n\nAt leaf 2k prescribe x=M v_k,g=v_k,f=(b+1/2)v_k^2. At checkpoint\n2k+1<n prescribe x=g=v_k,f=v_k^2/2. Binary carry gives\nsbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-v2(k+1), hence\nv_(k+1)/v_k=-a*c^(1+v2(k+1))/M. Thus the leaf step takes M v_k to v_k\nand the following actual checkpoint step takes v_k to M v_(k+1).\nEvery physical update is satisfied, x_0=1, and the final position is\n\n    t=x_n=v_(N-1)=-Rreset^(N-1)/(M*c^(m-1)).\n\nComplete the actual terminal by g_n=u=2t/(b+2), f_n=t^2/(b+2), and set\nthe optimum jet to zero. This is a scalar Gram transcript; no function is\nasserted to interpolate it. Substitution gives the full row table:\n\n    Q_(leaf(v),star)=0, Q_(star,leaf(v))=-b*v^2;\n    both optimum rows at check(w) are 0;\n    Q_(check(w),check(w'))=0;\n    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0;\n    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v);\n    Q_(leaf(v),leaf(w))=b*w*(v-w);\n    Q_(n,check(w))=Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=-b*u^2/4;\n    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2.                       (5.1)\n\nIn particular both adjacent queried directions are nonpositive: following\na leaf has w=v, while at the preceding checkpoint the next leaf parameter\nis -d*w/M with d>0. Every E80 row is accounted for by this table. E80 means\nboth optimum orientations at every finite index, all checkpoint pairs,\nall leaf-to-checkpoint rows, adjacent checkpoint-to-leaf rows, and all\noutgoing actual-terminal rows. No leaf-to-leaf or remote checkpoint-to-\nleaf rows belonged to E80.\n\nThe growth estimate is included, rather than imported from the old draft.\nSince M=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n\n    Rreset-1=epsilon[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/M\n              >epsilon/8=1/(32m).\n\nFor the strict bound, epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 make the bracket exceed\n32/45-5/16>5/16, while M<5/2. For m>=16, induction gives\n2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2: the base is 32767>=24576 and the induction difference\n96m^2-192m-95 is positive. Hence N-1>32m(2m+1).\nThe binomial inequality (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2 yields\nRreset^(N-1)>2^(2m+1). Because M*c^(m-1)<2^(2m), we have t<-2.\n\nThis transcript is explicitly not interpolable. At pbad=n-2 the checkpoint\nparameter w=v_(N-2)=-M*t/beta>2M, whereas v_0=1/M. Consequently\n\n    Q_(pbad,0)=b*v_0*(w-v_0)>0, Q_(0,pbad)=0.        (5.2)\n\nLaw I omits this row. Law II includes it with positive weight. The new\nfailure below is insufficient paid action even though that violated row\nis now present. Neither (5.1) nor (5.2) is a lower bound on unrestricted W.\n\n### 5.1 A symbolic tail bound; no trajectory or recurrence was executed\n\nFor k=N-1-j, binary complementation gives exactly\n\n    |v_k/t|=Rreset^(-j)c^sbit(j), 1<=j<N.\n\nSince an integer with r binary ones is at least 2^r-1,\nc^(2sbit(j))<=(j+1)^4. Put rreset=Rreset^(-2). The previous lower bound\non Rreset gives 1-rreset>=1/(64m): with zeta=1/(32m)<1, use\n1-(1+zeta)^(-2)>=zeta/2.\nFor every integer d>=1,\nj^d<=d!*binom(j+d-1,d), and summing the geometric-series identity gives\nsum_(j>=1)j^d r^j<=d!/(1-r)^(d+1), 0<r<1. This identity follows by\nmultiplying d+1 geometric series and counting compositions, or by d\nsuccessive differentiations of their convergent series. Therefore\n\n    sum_(k<N-1)v_k^2/t^2 <=768(64m)^5=:Moment5,\n    sum_(p=2k+1 in Check)h_p*v_k^2/t^2\n                                  <=92160(64m)^7=:Moment7. (5.3)\n\nFor the second bound, v2(k+1)=v2(j), so\nh_p=1+beta*c^v2(j)<=1+j^2<=2j^2. Apply the d=6 inequality to\n2j^2(j+1)^4<=128j^6. For the first use (j+1)^4<=16j^4; the displayed\nconstant 768 is a harmless enlargement of 16*4!.\n\nAll coarse exponential comparisons used below follow from\n\n    2^(m-1)>2^24(64m)^7, m>=1536.                    (5.4)\n\nAt m=1536, m<2^11 makes the right side less than 2^143, whereas the\nleft side is 2^1535. The ratio of the left side to m^7 increases at each\ninteger increment, since (1+1/m)^7<sum_(j>=0)(7/m)^j<2 for m>=1536.\nThis proves (5.4) for all relevant m by induction. This is symbolic\nreasoning, not a generated arithmetic table or executed recurrence.\n\n## 6. Complete reset screen for both frozen laws\n\nOn (5.1), Law I has exact value\n\n    M_I(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2\n      +b*sum_(earlier leaves ell=2k)\n           [r81_ell*v_k*(t-v_k)-w81_ell*(t-v_k)^2]\n      +b*sum_(checkpoints p=2k+1)w81_p*t*(v_k-t).      (6.1)\n\nEvery selected original row appears here: outgoing optimum rows vanish,\nlast-leaf-to-checkpoint rows vanish, leaf pairs and both other orientations\nare evaluated by (5.1). For the frozen eta=1/(4D), w81_p<=2eta. Use\nv(t-v)<=t^2/4 and t(v-t)<=v^2/4, and sum r81_ell<2. Formula (5.3) and\n(5.4) give\n\n    last sum in (6.1) <=(b*eta/2)Moment5*t^2 <t^2/8.\n\nIndeed its coefficient is at most 96b(64m)^5/D, which is less than 1/8\nby b<3/2, D>2^m and (5.4). The leaf relay contribution is at most\nb*t^2/2 and its symmetric-pair part is nonpositive. Finally\n\n    2/(b+2)+2b*(b+1)^2/(b+2)^2-b/2\n       >4/7+7b/18 >121/126.\n\nHere (b+1)/(b+2)>2/3 and b>1. Thus\n\n    M_I(qtar)<qtar-(3/4)t^2<0.                       (6.2)\n\nThe inequality 121/126-1/8>3/4 and t^2>4 justify the displayed margin.\nThis reset conclusion is for the frozen eta law. The broader arbitrary-\ntheta contact family is excluded instead by (I.7), without a claim that\nevery such theta has the same sign on the reset transcript.\n\nFor Law II with kappa=1, the EXACT corresponding expression is\n\n    M_II(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2\n       +2b*sum_(p=2k+1)t_p*sum_(ell=2s<p)\n                         omega_(p,ell)*v_s*(v_k-v_s). (6.3)\n\nThe reverse leaf-to-checkpoint rows are zero, as are the source-to-\ncheckpoint and outgoing optimum rows. The displayed 2 is the actual\n1+kappa forward weight. Since v_s(v_k-v_s)<=v_k^2/4, the last sum is\nat most (b/2)sum_p t_p*v_k^2. The total checkpoint mass satisfies\nTc>=h_(2^(m-1)-1)>2^(m-1)/3. Equations (5.3)--(5.4) prove\n\n    sum_p t_p*v_k^2/t^2 <=Moment7/Tc<1/4.\n\nTherefore\n\n    M_II(qtar)<qtar-[2/(b+2)-b/8]*t^2\n                       <qtar-t^2/4<0.              (6.4)\n\nWe used 2/(b+2)-b/8>4/7-3/16>1/4 and qtar<1. The actual negative\nterminal-row cost was retained in (6.3) and only dropped for this upper\nbound. The included remote rows are too weak under this global degree\nnormalization. Their mere presence does not pay the required reset cost.\n\n## 7. The older two-gradient deep waves\n\nFor 2<=j<=m-3 set p=2^j-1, H=h_p, x_0=0, g_0=-H/b, g_p=1, and every\nother original gradient, including g_n, zero. The physical displacement\nis exactly zero. This is a formal full-space screen only.\n\nFor the frozen Law I, direct substitution in (I.3) gives\n\n    M_I=(r81_0+w81_0)H^2/b^2-(H-1)(r81_p+w81_p).           (7.1)\n\nBoth J(0,z) and J(p,z) equal m. Define, only for this calculation,\n\n    F(P)=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2-P)+(2eta/m)/(S-b-P).\n\nThen r81_i+w81_i=h_i F(P81_i) for i=0,p, and F is strictly increasing.\nFor j>=3, H>4 and b/(b-1)<4; using F(P81_p)>F(0) in (7.1) gives\n\n    M_I<F(0)H[H/b-(H-1)]<0.                        (7.2)\n\nAt j=2, H=1+beta*c and P81_p=2b+1+beta<6. Now\nb-(b-1)H=1-beta^2>epsilon. Also\n\n    [F(P81_p)-F(0)]/F(0) <= P81_p/(S-b-P81_p).\n\nIt follows from (7.1) that M_I>0 if\n15/(S-8)<1/(6m), which follows from S>90m+8. This last inequality\nholds since S>2^m-1 and m>=1536 (the ratio 2^m/m increases for m>1,\nand the asserted comparison already holds at m=16).\nThus Law I passes exactly this j=2 screen and fails all j>=3 in its range.\n\nLaw II with kappa=1 passes every one of these old deep waves. Write\n\n    L_le=sum_(r in Check,r<=p)t_r*omega_(r,0),\n    L_gt=sum_(r in Check,r>p)t_r*omega_(r,0).\n\nIts exact value is\n\n    M_II=[(2-b)L_le+2L_gt]*H^2/b^2\n                 +2t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)*(2b-3)*H/b>0. (7.3)\n\nTo derive it, the routed base contributes\n(L_le+L_gt)H^2/b^2+t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)(b-1)H/b.\nFor a reverse cycle ending before p the interval displacement is b*g_0;\nfor one ending after p it is zero. The cycle at p contributes the\nremaining mixed term with coefficient b-2 and its checkpoint diagonal.\nAdding these gives (7.3). Its signs follow from b<3/2 and t_p>0.\nThe new equal-scale wave (II.6) still refutes its full matrix.\n\n## 8. The older H4 positive/negative block direction\n\nSet x_0=0, g_i=1 at i=0,1,2,3, g_i=-1 at i=8,9,10,11, and all other\noriginal gradients zero, including g_n. The two active four-query blocks\nhave identical physical mass, so displacement is zero. Put\n\n    H1=1+beta, H2=1+beta*c,\n    P2=b+H1, B4=2b+H1+H2, C4=b+H2.\n\nHere P2<7/2, B4<9, C4>4. All active original prefix masses P81_i are\nbelow 100, their total active step mass is 2B4<18 (in fact <17), and\nall J(i,z)=m.\n\nFor Law I put k0=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2) and l0=(2eta/m)/(S-b).\nFreeze r81_i=k0 h_i,w81_i=l0 h_i only within this calculation. Zero physical\ndisplacement makes the exact frozen polynomial\n\n    (k0+l0)/2 * sum_active h_i(2-h_i).\n\nThe sum is twice\n2b(2-b)+(1+beta)(1-beta)+(1+beta*c)(1-beta*c), which is less than -1:\nthe first term is <=2, the second <1/4, and the third <1-(21/10)^2.\nThus the full sum is <-2 and the frozen value is <-(k0+l0).\n\nThe actual coefficients differ by at most\n|r81_i-k0 h_i|<=200 k0 h_i/S and\n|w81_i-l0 h_i|<=200 l0 h_i/S for each active i, since P81_i<100 and S>400.\nIn the polynomial (I.3) with g_z=x_0=0, the factor multiplying each\nr81_i or w81_i has absolute value at most 18: |g_i|<=1 and the total\nactive step mass is <17. Consequently M_I<0.\n\nFor Law II use its exact routed expression (II.3), setting all values\nf_i=0 after their cancellation. Checkpoints p<=13 contribute at most\n4000/Tc in absolute value. To verify this bound, there are seven such\ncheckpoints, h_p<8, every formal position has absolute value <9,\neach finite row has absolute quadratic value <=20, and each optimum\nrow <=19/2. The bracket\nQ_(z,p)+sum omega[2Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)+Q_(ell,star)]\nhas absolute value <=70. Summing t_p<=56/Tc gives less than 4000/Tc.\n\nFor p>=15, g_p=0 and its ancestor height J(p,ell) is the SAME for all\nfour active leaves ell=0,2,8,10. The full bracket equals\n\n    2*sum_active_leaves omega_(p,ell)*L_ell,\n    (L_0,L_2,L_8,L_10)=(1,1+P2,1-B4,1-B4+P2).\n\nSince raw_(p,ell)=(2p+1-ell)/[(p+1)J], its numerator before the positive\ndenominator (p+1)J Z_p is\n\n    N_p=8p-16+6P2+(16-4p)C4 <69-8p <=-3(p+1), p>=15.\n\nThis uses C4>4 and P2<7/2. Hence every such checkpoint contributes\nnegatively. At the whole root p0=2^(m-1)-1, J=m and Z_p0<=p0+1,\nso its contribution is at most\n\n    -6h_p0/[Tc*m*(p0+1)].\n\nThis one term already dominates 4000/Tc. Indeed\nh_p0/[m(p0+1)]>(7/16)(6/5)^(m-2)/m>4000.\nAt m=1536 use (6/5)^4>2 and 383 blocks of four powers, which give a\nlower bound exceeding 2^383 before division by m and multiplication by\n7/16. Thereafter (6/5)^(m-2)/m increases since m>5. Therefore M_II<0.\nThis is an all-depth analytical sign proof for the actual first-block\nembedding, not a finite numeric H4 experiment or a statement about all\nfour-block embeddings.\n\n## 9. What is excluded, omitted rows, and the remaining interface\n\nLaw I fails for every independent theta_i in [0,1] satisfying its\ndefinition, even though the exact optimum distribution and all remote\nfan weights are allowed to vary. The obstruction uses its deliberately\nbalanced contact equation (I.4). It does not exclude a different contact\nrelation, a different terminal distribution, or added independent rows.\nOne omitted potentially relevant row is Q_(n-2,0), explicitly positive\non the reset transcript. Adding it would define a different law, not\nrepair (I.2) within its parameters.\n\nLaw II fails for the literal LCA/position kernel (II.1), every common\nkappa>=0, and degrees t_p proportional to h_p. The necessary condition\n(II.8) applies to the specified routed support with arbitrary degrees\nand receiver probabilities, but is only one necessary two-coordinate\ntest. It is not an enlarged-support impossibility theorem. For example,\nthe omitted direct checkpoint pair Q_(p,r), with r,p from (II.6), and\nits reverse are not in (II.2). Either could alter that two-coordinate\nmatrix; neither is presumed to be a free PSD repair. Non-last leaf-to-\nleaf rows and nonuniform reverse-cycle amounts are also outside this law.\n\nThe concrete failure mechanisms are different. Passing the older two-gradient waves does not address this.\n\nAn unresolved full-function construction must pay the reset while\ncontrolling all such ancestor-crossing mixed terms, and must also prove\nthe complete remainder after the single charge (4.4) and actual terminal\nsquare (4.1). Merely requiring unknown blocks to be PSD restates the\nmissing theorem. We give no third coefficient law or automatic next round.\n\nNo positive primal candidate is asserted. Consequently no static function,\nminimum set, closest radius or attained W lower bound is attributed to any\nof the formal negative directions or to the incompatible reset transcript.\nThe original unrestricted exact-profile minimax question remains OPEN.\n\n## 10. Mathematical/numerical programs, arithmetic or\ntrajectory/recurrence replays, solvers, benchmark DP, formal calls,\nfact_submit, subagents, installations, and paid consults: all ZERO.\nThere is no automatic renewal or project-completion action.\n\n## 1. Scope and accepted premises\n\nFix ANY integer m>=1536. Set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon_h=1/(4m), beta_h=1-epsilon_h,\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon_h), a=beta_h/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l),\n    S_l=sum H_l, X_l=1+S_l, n=2^m-1, Z=D_m=1+2S_m.\n\nThese hierarchy symbols are report-local aliases of the hierarchy in\naccepted931b9653ce0f7201; H_l here is a word, not the conjugate quadratic\ncalled H_j in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Every child retains the SAME\nenclosing m, a, c, beta_h, and epsilon_h. The actual final step is\nd0=1+a. All indices are zero based.\n\nFor every pair 0<=j<k<=n-2 consider ONE static globally convex\ndifferentiable function f with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained\nminimum. Its actual queried gradients must have exactly the form\n\n    g_i=p for 0<=i<=j,\n    g_i=q for j+1<=i<=k,\n    g_i=r for k+1<=i<n.                                      (1.1)\n\nThere is no restriction on the real vectors p,q,r, their lengths,\nangles, dimension, sampled heights, affine offsets, or x_0 orientation.\nFamily E also requires g_n=r. Family Q permits every legal terminal\njet and every static completion of the entire queried prefix. For\neach function and ANY of its own minimizers x_star!=x_0 put\n\n    V=2(f(x_n)-min f)/||x_0-x_star||^2.\n\nIn particular the bound, when proved, applies to the function's OWN\nclosest minimizer. For L>0, translate x_star to0 and replace f by\n(f-min f)/L. Then this is exactly 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),\nthe original W=2R normalization, with unchanged physical steps.\n\nThe complete candidate conclusion established in Sections2--8 is,\nfor every pair with k outside the complete subtree rooted at j,\n\n    sup_E V=1/Z,\n    1/Z <= sup_Q V <=1/(Z-d0^2),\n    B_n sup_E V<47/50,\n    B_n sup_Q V<940/999<19/20.                              (1.2)\n\nIt is a restricted-family conclusion only. The original every-n,\nevery-nonnegative-word minimax problem, including zero and arbitrarily\nlarge steps, is unchanged and OPEN. No restriction (1.1) has been\nshown lossless for W. In particular the conditional full-W hypothesis\nW_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) in accepted931b9653ce0f7201 is still unproved.\n\nCorrectness premises imported from the fact graph, at their scopes:\n\n* 1252b8823bb5a290: all ordered smooth-convex interpolation rows,\n  necessity and sufficiency, and its explicit conjugate interpolant.\n* b98bb807d514e80d: the definition and lower-witness scope of the\n  COMPLETE Huber/single-cut maximum K_n, and original benchmark notation.\n* 931b9653ce0f7201: B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 at precisely the hierarchy\n  above, and only the unconditional part of that accepted statement.\n\nTheir relevant actual statements/proofs were read. The complete shared\nreports37987ddad8be4c0b and6b8cd30a96a9165b were recalled as UNVERIFIED\nawareness. None of their new mathematical claims is an accepted premise.\nAll new formulas used below are jointly rederived here.\n\n## 2. Physical classification before any certificate choice\n\nFor index i let l_i=nu_2(i+1), the largest nonnegative integer l with\n2^l dividing i+1. Literal concatenation shows\n\n    h_i=1+a*c^(l_i),\n    I_i=[i-(2^(l_i)-1), i+(2^(l_i)-1)].                    (2.1)\n\nThe interval I_i is the complete physical copy of H_(l_i+1) rooted\nat i; the two children are copies of H_(l_i). These assertions follow\nby induction through (H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l): the middle position has\ni+1=2^l, and translating a child by2^l preserves valuations below l.\n\nTo classify a pair, descend from the whole root while both indices\nare in the same child. At the least common ancestor one of exactly\nthree things happens: j is that root and k is in its right child;\nk is that root and j is in its left child; or j and k lie in its\ndifferent children. An ancestor with both points in the same child\nis not the least common ancestor. This covers adjacent positions,\nshallow common subtrees, and arbitrarily separated checkpoint levels.\n\nAt that least common subtree write E_pre and E_post for the sums of\nALL physical steps before and after the subtree. If P_loc,M_loc,T_loc\nare the sums before j, between j and k, and after k inside it, the\nactual full masses are\n\n    P=E_pre+P_loc, M=M_loc, T=T_loc+E_post,\n    b=h_j, c_step=h_k, d=b-1, e=c_step-1,\n    u=P+1, v=M+1, z=T+1,\n    A=u+d=P+b, B=v+e=M+c_step, U=u+v+d.                  (2.2)\n\nNo outer mass is discarded or presumed monotone. All later formulas\nuse these exact u,v,z. In particular U is 1 plus the entire mass\nbefore the second checkpoint.\n\nBecause j<k, k belongs to I_j if and only if it is in j's right child.\nCall this the remaining ancestor case. Every other pair has k>I_j's\nright endpoint and is covered by (1.2). This includes all pairs in\ndifferent children of their least common ancestor and all pairs with\nk as their least common ancestor. Section7 treats the exceptional\nconsecutive roots on the leftmost branch without deleting their tail.\n\n## 3. Exact endpoint system and one global completion\n\nNormalize the chosen optimum jet to (0,0,0). Let F_p,F_q,F_r be the\nvalues at the first query of the three sectors, and set\n\n    Z_p=x_0, Z_q=x_0-Ap, Z_r=x_0-Ap-Bq.\n\nAt every ACTUAL cumulative mass s inside its sector the jets are\n\n    (Z_p-sp,p,F_p-s||p||^2), 0<=s<=P,\n    (Z_q-sq,q,F_q-s||q||^2), 0<=s<=M,\n    (Z_r-sr,r,F_r-s||r||^2).                            (3.1)\n\nThe last range is 0<=s<=T for E and 0<=s<=T-d0 for the queried part\nof Q. The separate terminal position is x_n=Z_r-Tr. Equal gradients\nforce the displayed values by the two convexity inequalities between\nany two samples. Thus these formulas pay the full physical decrease\ninside each constant-gradient sector.\n\nRetain both actual endpoints of each range, the optimum, and the\nseparate terminal jet for Q, identifying coincident index labels.\nFor ALL ordered distinct retained samples impose\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (3.2)\n\nThere is no omitted remote or optimum condition. For source sector a\nat mass s and target sector b at mass t, substitution gives\n\n    Q_(a(s),b(t))=F_a-F_b+<g_a,Z_b-Z_a>\n       +||g_a-g_b||^2/2+t(||g_b||^2-<g_a,g_b>).          (3.3)\n\nThe source mass cancels and target dependence is affine. Rows with\nan optimum or a separate terminal are also affine in the sector mass.\nConsequently every original query row is an endpoint convex\ncombination; same-sector rows are zero. Endpoints are actual samples,\nso necessity holds as well. Singleton sectors, including k=n-2 with\nthe sole r query n-1, are included.\n\nFor each retained jet put y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nThe explicit function supplied by accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is\n\n    H(vect)=||vect||^2/2+max_i{<y_i,vect>-delta_i},\n    F(x)=sup_vect{<x,vect>-H(vect)}.                    (3.4)\n\nIt is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and globally1-smooth,\nand interpolates all retained jets. Interior anchors are affine\ncombinations of the two endpoint anchors because\ny(s)=Z_a-(s+1)g_a and delta(s)=F_a-(s+1/2)||g_a||^2.\nAdding every original interior anchor changes neither H nor F.\nInduction through every physical update proves that the supplied\npoints are the actual GD transcript of this ONE static function.\n\nOptimum rows give delta_i>=0, with the zero optimum anchor present.\nHence H(0)=0, min F=0, and its complete attained minimum set is\n\n    argmin F=conv{y_i:delta_i=0}.                       (3.5)\n\nIndeed grad F(x)=0 precisely when x is a subgradient of H at0.\nThe active affine slopes at0 are exactly those displayed in (3.5).\nTheir convex hull gives supporting slopes; separation of a point\noutside that hull gives the reverse inclusion. This is the entire\nminimum set, not a selected subset. A different completion may have\na different minimum set. The upper proof starts at each actual\nminimizer of THAT completion, so it never inherits another function's\nradius. Finite-dimensional closed convex minimum sets have closest\npoints, and (3.5) is compact for the explicit completion.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: original rows, backward cycle, and full identity\n\nLet L_p=j, L_q=k, R_0=k+1. Directly expanding (3.2) gives\n\n    Q_(L_p,star)=F_p-<p,x_0>+||p||^2/2,\n    Q_(L_q,star)=F_q-<q,x_0>+A<p,q>+||q||^2/2,\n    Q_(R_0,star)=F_r-<r,x_0>+A<p,r>+B<q,r>+||r||^2/2,\n    Q_(L_q,L_p)=F_q-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2+||q||^2/2+d<p,q>,\n    Q_(R_0,L_q)=F_r-F_q+(M+1/2)||q||^2+||r||^2/2+e<q,r>,\n    Q_(L_p,L_q)=F_p-F_q+(1/2-A)||p||^2\n                          +(M+1/2)||q||^2-v<p,q>.\n\nThe exact paid cycle is\n\n    C_pq=Q_(L_p,L_q)+Q_(L_q,L_p)\n         =-d||p||^2+(d-v)<p,q>+v||q||^2.               (4.1)\n\nIts negative p-square is retained. Choose alpha,beta,gamma,eta with\nalpha+beta+gamma=1, theta=alpha+beta, and use the rows\n\n| row | multiplier mu |\n|---|---:|\n| Q_(L_p,star) | alpha |\n| Q_(L_q,star) | beta |\n| Q_(R_0,star) | gamma |\n| Q_(L_q,L_p) | alpha+eta |\n| Q_(L_p,L_q) | eta |\n| Q_(R_0,L_q) | theta |\n\nAll other core multipliers are0. At L_p and L_q incoming and outgoing\nflows agree; R_0 emits1 and the optimum absorbs1. The cycle changes\nno value flow. With t_pay a terminal charge and w=t_pay+1/2, the\nweighted core rows plus t_pay||r||^2 equal\n\n    F_r-<gbar,x_0>+Q, gbar=alpha*p+beta*q+gamma*r,\n    Q=(alpha*u-eta*d)||p||^2+(theta+eta)*v||q||^2+w||r||^2\n       +[alpha*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v)]<p,q>\n       +gamma*A<p,r>+[theta*e+gamma*B]<q,r>.            (4.2)\n\nPut D=U+e+w. The common-gradient coefficient is D. The p row sum\nis [alpha*u+A-eta*(d+v)]/2, and the cumulative p,q row sum is\n[(1+theta)U+e]/2. Thus the exact contact equations are\n\n    theta=(U+e)/(2D-U),\n    alpha=[A-eta*(d+v)]/(2D-u).                       (4.3)\n\nWriting s=p-q and tvec=q-r, subtraction of D||gbar||^2 gives the\nENTIRE increment quadratic J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2,\nwhere\n\n    J11=alpha*u-eta*d-D*alpha^2,\n    J22=theta*(U-D*theta),\n    J12=(1-theta)*(2D*alpha-A)/2.                     (4.4)\n\nFor the mixed term, its coefficient before subtracting the square is\nalpha*u+theta*A-eta*(d+v); substitution of (4.3) yields exactly2J12.\nThis checks the full matrix, including mixed directions.\n\nFor E use t_pay=T and add Q_(n,R_0) with multiplier1. For Q let\nN_-=n-1, g_n=s_n, use t_pay=T-d0^2/2, and add Q_(N_-,R_0) and\nQ_(n,N_-) with multiplier1 each. Omit the first if N_-=R_0.\nThe respective terminal expressions are\n\n    f_n-F_r+T||r||^2,\n    f_n-F_r+(T-d0^2/2)||r||^2+||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2/2.     (4.5)\n\nThe latter follows from x_n=x_(N_-)-d0*r and\nQ_(n,N_-)=f_n-f_(N_-)+d0<s_n,r>+||s_n-r||^2/2.\nNo leaf is removed and the free terminal vector is unrestricted.\nSet lambda_ij=2mu_ij including the terminal path. The full identity is\n\n    ||x_0||^2/(2D)-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n     =2D||gbar-x_0/(2D)||^2\n       +2[J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2]\n       +terminal_square,                             (4.6)\n\nwhere terminal_square is0 for E and ||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2 for Q.\nEvery original finite node has zero flow except n, which emits2;\nthe optimum absorbs2. All sampled values and heights cancel by these\nexplicit original rows. Formula (4.6) is on ALL real (x_0,p,q,r,s_n),\nwith s_n omitted for E, and therefore on the entire equal-sector\nsubspace of the original formal gradients. It is NOT claimed PSD\nwhen gradients inside a sector become independent.\n\nWhenever the stated multipliers are nonnegative, D>0 and J is PSD,\n(4.6) and Q_ij<=0 imply 2f_n<=||x_0||^2/(2D).\n\n## 5. Uniform hierarchy and last-step estimates\n\nLiteral summation gives\n\n    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1,\n    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2).             (5.1)\n\nThe closed form has the stated initial value and satisfies the\nrecurrence because v_h(c-2)=a. This is a symbolic identity, not an\nexecuted recurrence. Squaring positive rationals gives\n7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70. Therefore\n\n    12/5<c<(169/70)(6145/6144)<29/12,\n    2/5<a<c-2<5/12, 0<v_h<1,\n    2^l<=X_l<=c^l,\n    2/5<a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2<5/12.                       (5.2)\n\nFor the upper c comparison, cross multiplication reduces to\n169*12<2*6144. For a>2/5 use beta_h>99/100 and c<29/12.\nFor a<c-2 use c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta_h. The last mass ratio\nfollows by dividing (5.1) by c^l: its denominator lies in (v_h,1].\n\nFor both terminal regimes define\n\n    epsilon_E=1/2, epsilon_Q=(1+d0^2)/2,\n    epsilon=epsilon_E or epsilon_Q,\n    w=T+1-epsilon=z-epsilon,\n    D=X_m-epsilon,\n    2D=Z for E, 2D=Z-d0^2 for Q.                     (5.3)\n\nHere epsilon is the terminal charge, distinct from epsilon_h.\nSince 1<d0<17/12<3/2, 0<epsilon<13/8. Also k<=n-2 implies T>=d0,\nso\n\n    w>=1-a^2/2>9/10.                                (5.4)\n\nIf the second checkpoint has level l and X=X_l, both of its complete\nchildren occur in the full prefix and suffix. Consequently\n\n    U>=X, z>=X, e<(5/12)X,\n    e/w<(5/12)(1+epsilon/w)<505/432<6/5.             (5.5)\n\nThe last bound uses epsilon/w<65/36. We will need the STRICT inequality\n\n                      U*w>e*(e+w).                   (5.6)\n\nFor l>=2, X>=4 and w>=X-epsilon>(19/32)X. Thus\n(U-e)w>(7/12)(19/32)X^2>25X^2/144>e^2.\nFor l=1, X=2+a>12/5, e=a*c=beta_h<1 and (5.4) give\n(U-e)w>(7/5)(9/10)=63/50>1>e^2.\nFor l=0, U>=1, e=a<5/12 and (5.4) give\n(U-e)w>(7/12)(9/10)>25/144>e^2.\nThese cases prove (5.6), including the final admissible checkpoint\nk=n-2 and singleton last queried sector. In particular theta in (4.3)\nsatisfies 0<theta<1 and\n\n    J22=(U+e)[U*w-e*(e+w)]/(2D-U)^2>0.               (5.7)\n\n## 6. Every non-ancestor pair except consecutive leftmost roots\n\nLet l=l_j, X=X_l, d=a*c^l. If k is outside I_j, all of j's right\nchild is between j and k. Hence u>=X and v>=X.\n\nIf j is not the first occurrence of level l, its index is at least\n3*2^l-1. Before that occurrence there is a complete H_(l+1), the\nnext bridge of excess c*d, and an H_l. Their total gives\n\n    u>=3X+(1+c)d>3X.                                (6.1)\n\nAll further prefixes only add actual positive steps. In that case\n\n    d*(u+v+d)/(u*v)\n      <(5/12)[1+1/3+(5/12)/3]=265/432<1.             (6.2)\n\nIf j is the first occurrence, j=2^l-1 and u=X. The immediately\nfollowing ancestor is k0=2^(l+1)-1. At k=k0 one has v=X.\nIf k>k0, the middle includes j's right child and the actual parent\nstep1+c*d. Thus v>=X+1+c*d>(7/5)X, and\n\n    d*(u+v+d)/(u*v)\n      <(5/12)[1+(1+5/12)*(5/7)]=845/1008<1.           (6.3)\n\nThere is no index between I_j's right endpoint and k0. Therefore\nthese cases exhaust k outside I_j except\n\n    j=2^l-1, k=2^(l+1)-1, 0<=l<=m-2.                (6.4)\n\nFor every pair covered by (6.2) or (6.3), set\n\n    alpha=A/(2D), eta=alpha*u/(d+v),\n    theta=(U+e)/(2D-U), beta=theta-alpha, gamma=1-theta.\n\nThe contact equations hold because eta(d+v)=alpha*u and A=2D*alpha.\nEvery multiplier is strictly positive except possible unused rows:\neta>0, alpha>0, gamma>0, and theta>alpha since U+e>A and 2D-U<2D.\nDirect substitution gives\n\n    J12=0,\n    J11=alpha*[u*v-d*(u+v+d)]/[2(d+v)]>0.             (6.5)\n\nFor (6.5), expand 2uv-A(d+v)=uv-d(u+v+d). Equations (5.7),(6.5)\nprove full positivity of the increment matrix with both charges.\nTogether with (4.6) this proves (1.2)'s upper bounds on this entire\nphysical region, with all free terminal vectors retained.\n\n## 7. Consecutive leftmost roots, including shallow subtrees\n\nFor (6.4) put X=X_l, t=d/X, E=e/X=c*t, Ubar=U/X=2+t,\ny=D/X. The actual outer prefix is0; the entire outer suffix remains\nin D. The following uniform estimates hold:\n\n    2/5<t<5/12, 12/5<c<29/12, y>=23/4,\n    A/X=1+t<3/2,\n    Cbar=Ubar+E>10/3, Ubar-E>4/3, Ubar^2<6,\n    23/4<2Ubar+E<6.                                 (7.1)\n\nFor y, if l<=m-3, repeated X_(i+1)>2X_i gives X_m>=8X, hence\ny>8-13/(8X)>=51/8>23/4. If l=m-2, then\nX_m/X=4+(2+c)t, and X>=2^(m-2)>=2048, so\ny>4+(22/5)(2/5)-1/1000>23/4.\nThe other bounds follow by substitution: Cbar>84/25>10/3;\nUbar-E=2-(c-1)t>203/144>4/3;\nUbar<29/12 so Ubar^2<6; and\n144/25<2Ubar+E<841/144<6. All are strict except the convenient weak\nlower y bound.\n\nUse eta=0, alpha=A/(2D-u), theta=(U+e)/(2D-U), beta=theta-alpha,\ngamma=1-theta. All row weights are nonnegative: both denominators\nare positive, 0<alpha<theta since A<U+e and 2D-u>2D-U, and\ntheta<1 by w>0,e>0. In units of X the exact matrix entries are\n\n    J11/X=(1+t)[(1-t)y-1]/(2y-1)^2,\n    J22/X=Cbar[(Ubar-E)y-Ubar^2]/(2y-Ubar)^2,\n    J12/X=(1+t)(2y-2Ubar-E)/[2(2y-1)(2y-Ubar)].         (7.2)\n\nThe two diagonals are positive by (7.1). After clearing the positive\ndenominators, det J>0 follows from positivity of\n\n    Cbar[(1-t)y-1][(Ubar-E)y-Ubar^2]\n                     -(1+t)(2y-2Ubar-E)^2/4.         (7.3)\n\nBoth factors in the first product are positive; also\n0<2y-2Ubar-E<2y-23/4. Thus (7.3) is strictly greater than\n\n    (10/3)[(7/12)y-1][(4/3)y-6]-(3/8)(2y-23/4)^2.\n\nWriting x=y-23/4>=0, this last polynomial is EXACTLY\n\n    (59/54)x^2+(1097/216)x+2351/3456>0.               (7.4)\n\nEvery coefficient is positive. This supplies a symbolic full-PSD\nproof for every such actual pair, including arbitrarily shallow\nleast-common subtrees inside the huge enclosing word. No monotonicity\nof the endpoint problem under suffix addition was assumed; (7.4)\nproves the needed inequality at the actual D.\nThe complete increment square is\nJ11||s+(J12/J11)tvec||^2+(J22-J12^2/J11)||tvec||^2.\n\nSections6 and7 exhaust ALL k outside I_j. In particular every\nwhole-root-second pair is included, but that old draft was not used\nas a premise.\n\n## 8. Sharp member, complete K, and quantitative benchmark comparison\n\nPut kappa=1/Z and define the one scalar Huber function\n\n    f_H(x)=x^2/2 if |x|<=kappa,\n    f_H(x)=kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2 if |x|>=kappa,\n    x_0=1.\n\nIts derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz; its\nunique minimizer is0. At every actual cumulative physical mass s<=S_m,\nx=1-kappa*s>kappa, the gradient is kappa, and the value is\nkappa*(1-kappa*s)-kappa^2/2. All physical updates are therefore\nexecuted, with p=q=r=g_n=kappa, closest radius1, and\n\n    2f_H(x_n)=2/Z-Z/Z^2=1/Z.                          (8.1)\n\nAll original ordered rows hold because these are jets of this one\nstatic function; same-gradient rows are identically zero. This proves\nthe matching fixed-terminal lower bound for every pair.\n\nFor completeness, the COMPLETE accepted channel is\n\n    K_n(h)=max{1/Z, max_i d_i^2/[u_i^2*(1+2T_i)]},\n    u_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t, d_i=h_i-1.\n\nIf X=X_(l_i), its physical children give u_i>=X,T_i>=X-1 and\n0<d_i<(5/12)X. Consequently\n\n    (u_i-d_i)(1+2T_i)>(7/12)X(2X-1)\n                         >2*(25/144)X^2>2d_i^2.\n\nHere 2X-1>=X and7/12>25/72. Since S_m=u_i+d_i+T_i,\n\n    u_i^2(1+2T_i)-d_i^2 Z\n      =(u_i+d_i)[(u_i-d_i)(1+2T_i)-2d_i^2]>0.\n\nEvery single-cut term is below1/Z, so K_n(H_m)=1/Z with EVERY cut\nincluded. This does not identify unrestricted W with K.\n\nThe benchmark retains every original integer split:\nC_0=B_0=1, Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\nG(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\nC_n=max_(0<=i<n)Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),\nB_n=max_(0<=i<n)G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/Z<47/50. Also\nZ=2X_m-1>=2^(m+1)-1>1000*(9/4)>1000*d0^2.\nThus (1.2) follows, including\nB_n/(Z-d0^2)<(47/50)(1000/999)=940/999<19/20.\n\n## 9. Mechanism2: fully paid triangular and bidirectional geometry\n\nThe remaining case has j an ancestor of k. We now derive a broader\nsupport for ANY pair without asserting its feasibility. Start with\nnonnegative alpha,beta,gamma,ell satisfying\nalpha+beta+gamma=1, 0<=ell<=alpha. Add the pq cycle with weight eta>=0.\nThe core rows are\n\n| row | multiplier mu |\n|---|---:|\n| Q_(L_p,star) | alpha |\n| Q_(L_q,star) | beta |\n| Q_(R_0,star) | gamma |\n| Q_(L_q,L_p) | ell+eta |\n| Q_(L_p,L_q) | eta |\n| Q_(R_0,L_q) | beta+ell |\n| Q_(R_0,L_p) | alpha-ell |\n\nThe remote row is the ACTUAL expression\n\n    Q_(R_0,L_p)=F_r-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2+||r||^2/2\n                                      +d<p,r>+B<q,r>.\n\nFor a second paid cycle use the last available r endpoint R_1:\nR_1=n for E and R_1=n-1 for Q. Its sector mass is t_r=T or T-d0,\nand put z_q=t_r+1. Add both Q_(L_q,R_1) and Q_(R_1,L_q) with\nweight xi>=0. Their EXACT sum is\n\n    C_qr=-e||q||^2+(e-z_q)<q,r>+z_q||r||^2.           (9.1)\n\nIt follows either by substituting the two rows or by the same-sector\nvalue equalities with the actual target mass t_r. For Q this uses\nthe queried endpoint, never an assumed terminal equality.\n\nThe full gradient quadratic after the same terminal charge is\n\n    Q=(alpha*u-eta*d)||p||^2\n      +[(beta+ell+eta)*v-xi*e]||q||^2+(w+xi*z_q)||r||^2\n      +[ell*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v)]<p,q>\n      +[(alpha-ell)*d+gamma*A]<p,r>\n      +[(beta+ell)*e+(1-beta-ell)*B+xi*(e-z_q)]<q,r>.\n                                                               (9.2)\n\nBoth cycles have zero value flow. The triangular rows balance at\nL_p and L_q and emit1 at R_0, with the same terminal path as Section4.\nThus EVERY row multiplier and flow remains explicit, even if R_1\ncoincides with R_0. Retain the complete primal endpoint system of\nSection3; a zero multiplier never deletes a legality condition.\n\nUse the local abbreviations\n\n    Lc=d+v, E_c=2D-u, K_c=2D-U, C=U+e,\n    theta=alpha+beta, R=alpha-ell.\n\nHere R is a remote-row multiplier, not the original normalization\nR_n and not a radius. Taking p and cumulative p,q row sums of (9.2)\ngives the PAID contact equations\n\n    alpha*E_c=A-eta*Lc,\n    theta*K_c=C-R*Lc-xi*(e+z_q).                     (9.3)\n\nOn s=p-q,tvec=q-r the entire remainder Q-D||gbar||^2 is\n\n    J11=alpha*u-eta*d-D*alpha^2,\n    J22=theta*U-R*Lc-xi*e-D*theta^2,\n    J12=[(1-theta)*(2D*alpha-A)-R*d]/2.               (9.4)\n\nFor the mixed coefficient in Q, substitute ell=alpha-R and\nbeta=theta-alpha into 2(alpha*u-eta*d) plus the pq coefficient;\nthen use eta*Lc=A-alpha*E_c. The result minus2Dalpha theta is\n(1-theta)(2Dalpha-A)-R d. Cycle (9.1) has no s term, so (9.4)\naccounts for every increment and both backward costs. The full\ninitial-point and terminal-square identity is exactly (4.6) with\nthis J and this row table. Signs and PSD have NOT been assumed.\n\n## 10. Exact residual for xi=0; no unspecified optimization oracle\n\nSet xi=0 for the explicit scalar interface below. Define\n\n    h_c=e+2w, sigma=1-theta,\n    R(sigma)=(K_c*sigma-h_c)/Lc,\n    alpha_max=A/E_c,\n    alpha_opt(sigma)=(u*v+2D*d)/(2D*Lc)\n                              -d*h_c*sigma/(2w*Lc),\n    Delta=u^2*v^2*w-d^2*e^2*D.                       (10.1)\n\nThese are closed formulas in the actual masses. R(sigma)>=0 is\nsigma>=sigma0=h_c/K_c. The remaining sign conditions are EXACTLY\n\n    R(sigma)<=alpha<=min(alpha_max,1-sigma),\n    0<=sigma<=1,\n    eta=[A-E_c*alpha]/Lc,\n    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha-R(sigma).\n\nFor a strict J22>0 certificate take sigma<sqrt(w/D). Its admissible\nscalar interval is therefore\n\n    sigma0<=sigma<sqrt(w/D),\n    sigma<=1,\n    sigma<=(h_c+Lc*alpha_max)/K_c,\n    sigma<=(h_c+Lc)/(K_c+Lc).                         (10.2)\n\nThe latter two constraints ensure the alpha interval is nonempty.\nAt sigma0 it is nonempty and J22>0 by (5.6); this fact alone does\nnot guarantee PSD. Define alpha_clamp(sigma) to be alpha_opt if it\nlies in that interval, and otherwise the nearer endpoint of the\ninterval (equivalently max(lower,min(upper,alpha_opt))).\n\nHere is the exact determinant calculation. Set t_c=2Dalpha-A and\ny_c=t_c-d*K_c/Lc. Equations (9.3)--(9.4) give\n\n    J22=w-D*sigma^2,\n    J11=[u^2*v^2+4d^2D(e+w)]/(4D*Lc^2)-y_c^2/(4D),\n    J12=(sigma*y_c+d*h_c/Lc)/2.                      (10.3)\n\nFor the J11 constant, use\nA*Lc-d*U=u*v and\nA*Lc*(u*v-d*U)+d^2*U^2=u^2*v^2.\nSince K_c^2-U^2=4D(e+w), (10.3) follows by completing its square.\nExpanding det J and completing the square in y_c gives the EXACT identity\n\n    det J=(w-D*sigma^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2)\n                         -D*w*(alpha-alpha_opt(sigma))^2.       (10.4)\n\nIndeed the y_c-square coefficient is -w/(4D), its linear coefficient\nis -sigma*d*h_c/(2Lc), and\n4w(e+w)-h_c^2=-e^2; these yield precisely (10.4).\n\nConsequently the following is the exact remaining inequality for\nthis explicitly advertised support, with all signs already encoded:\n\n  Find sigma satisfying (10.2) such that\n\n    D*w*[alpha_clamp(sigma)-alpha_opt(sigma)]^2\n       <=(w-D*sigma^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2).              (10.5)\n\nIf (10.5) holds, the nonnegative multipliers just printed and (4.6)\ngive the desired bound. J22>0 and det J>=0 imply J11>=0, and the full\nincrement quadratic is\nJ22||tvec+(J12/J22)s||^2+(det J/J22)||s||^2.\nThis checks full PSD, not only the two diagonal entries.\n\nFor xi=0 and strict J22>0, (10.5) is necessary and sufficient for\na certificate within this row support: for fixed sigma, (10.4) is\nmaximized on the permitted alpha interval at alpha_clamp. It is NOT\na necessary condition for the full endpoint problem, which retains\nadditional original ordered rows; nor is xi>0 exhausted.\n\nNo mathematical program, SDP, numerical pair enumeration, or search\nover an unspecified feasible set is invoked here. Equation (10.5)\nis an explicit scalar inequality. Its uniform feasibility is the\nremaining theorem-level gap in this attempted all-pair certificate.\n\n## 11. Delta is positive on EVERY remaining ancestor pair\n\nThis section removes one possible obstruction in (10.5), but does\nnot prove its multiplier-clamping term small enough.\n\nIf j is an ancestor of k, let l=l_j and X=X_l, d=a*c^l. Its right\nchild is a complete H_l containing k. Write z_loc=1 plus the mass\nafter k inside that right child, and O for the FULL mass after I_j.\nThen the exact identities and child bounds are\n\n    u>=X, v+e+z_loc=X, O>=0,\n    w=O+z_loc-epsilon,\n    D=u+d+X+O-epsilon,\n    d<(5/12)X, e<(5/12)min(v,z_loc).                 (11.1)\n\nThe left child of k is inside the middle block, and its right child\nis inside z_loc, proving the last inequality. Necessarily l>=1.\nIn this section set tau=5/12. We prove\n\n           d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)<1,                 (11.2)\n\nwhich is Delta>0.\n\nFirst suppose l>=4, so X>=16, and z_loc>=13/4. At fixed u,d,v,e,z_loc,\nD/w=1+(u+d+v+e)/w decreases as O increases. Since\nz_loc-epsilon>z_loc/2, it is enough to bound the expression at O=0.\nThere\n\n    D/u^2 <=(2+tau)/X,\n    e^2/[v^2*(z_loc-epsilon)]\n       <2tau^2*min(v,z_loc)^2/(v^2*z_loc)\n       <=2tau^2/max(v,z_loc)\n       <=2tau^2*(2+tau)/X.\n\nThe first estimate uses u>=X and d<tau X. The middle estimate\nsplits into v<=z_loc and v>=z_loc. The last follows from\nX=v+e+z_loc<=(2+tau)max(v,z_loc). Multiplying these estimates with\nd^2<tau^2 X^2 bounds (11.2)'s left side by\n2tau^4(2+tau)^2<2*(1/2)^4*(5/2)^2=25/32<1.\nThis monotonicity is proved for this explicit scalar expression only;\nit asserts no monotonicity of the endpoint optimization problem.\n\nStill with l>=4, suppose z_loc<13/4. Then e<65/48<3/2,\nv=X-e-z_loc>X-5, e^2<3, and by (5.5), e^2/w<2. Since\nD=w+u+d+v+e and d+v+e<(1+tau)X<3X/2,\n\n    d^2e^2D/(u^2v^2w)\n      <tau^2*X^2/(u^2v^2) * [3+2u+3X]\n      <=tau^2*(3+5X)/v^2\n      <(3+5X)/[4(X-5)^2]<1.\n\nFor the final inequality write X=16+x, x>=0; then\n4(X-5)^2-(3+5X)=4x^2+83x+401>0.\n\nFinally let l<=3. From (5.2), X<=c^3<(5/2)^3<16, so d<7,e<7.\nThe whole-word D=X_m-epsilon>2^20. If u>=D/4, then v>=1,w>9/10 give\n\n    d^2e^2D/(u^2v^2w)<384160/(9D)<1.\n\nIf u<D/4, (11.1) gives\nO=D-u-d-X+epsilon>3D/4-23>D/2,\nand w>O-13/8>D/3. Also d/u<tau and e/v<tau. Hence\nd^2e^2D/(u^2v^2w)<3tau^4<3/16<1.\nThis exhausts the shallow cases while retaining their actual huge\nouter prefix or suffix. Thus Delta>0 for EVERY ancestor pair and both\nterminal charges. Positivity of Delta does not remove the nonnegative\nmultiplier constraints in (10.2)--(10.5).\n\n## 12. A physical uncovered configuration and a complete legal test\n\nAn explicit physical pair outside the portion (1.2) is\n\n    j=2^(m-2)-1, k=j+1.\n\nHere j is the root of the whole word's left child and k its immediately\nfollowing leaf. Neither checkpoint is the whole root. Put\nX=X_(m-2), d=a*c^(m-2). The FULL masses, including the entire right\nhalf of the original word, are\n\n    u=X, v=1, e=a,\n    X_m=4X+(2+c)d,\n    T=3X+(1+c)d-2-a,\n    D=4X+(2+c)d-epsilon.                              (12.1)\n\nThe diagonal-cycle condition fails physically:\nuv-dU=X-d(X+1+d)<0, because d>(2/5)X and X>=2048.\nThis is a failure of that choice only. It does not refute (10.5),\nthe full endpoint upper bound, or the all-pair assertion.\n\nTo exhibit a LEGAL full transcript at this configuration, rather\nthan substitute an arbitrary indefinite Gram direction, take scalar\np=1,q=1/2,r=1/4. Use A=u+d, B=v+e from the ACTUAL masses and define\n\n    Z_r=r*(T+1), Z_q=Z_r+B*q,\n    x_0=Z_q+A*p, x_n=r,\n    t_+=max(t,0),\n    f(x)=x^2/2-(x-r)_+^2/2\n      +(x-Z_r)_+^2/2-(x-Z_r-(q-r))_+^2/2\n      +(x-Z_q)_+^2/2-(x-Z_q-(p-q))_+^2/2.            (12.2)\n\nThis is ONE static global function. Its derivative equals x for\nx<=r; it is r on [r,Z_r], increases with slope1 from r to q on\n[Z_r,Z_r+q-r], is q up to Z_q, increases with slope1 from q to p\non [Z_q,Z_q+p-q], and is p thereafter. The ramps do not overlap:\nT>=0 and B*q>=q-r since B>=c_step>1. Thus f' is continuous,\nnondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. The derivative is negative for x<0,\nzero only at0, and positive for x>0. Its COMPLETE minimum set is\n{0}, with minimum0 and OWN closest radius x_0>0.\n\nEvery actual query is explicitly\n\n    x_i=x_0-p*sum_(t<i)h_t                         (i<=j),\n    x_i=Z_q-q*sum_(j<t<i)h_t                     (j<i<=k),\n    x_i=Z_r-r*sum_(k<t<i)h_t                     (k<i<=n).\n\nThe last p query is Z_q+b*p>=Z_q+p-q; the first q query is Z_q;\nthe last q query is Z_r+c_step*q>=Z_r+q-r; the first r query is Z_r;\nand all r queries through the ACTUAL terminal r are on its r plateau.\nThus the derivative of (12.2) at every displayed query is precisely\n(1.1), and every physical GD step is used. Set each f_i=f(x_i),\ng_i=f'(x_i). ALL original ordered rows, including remote, terminal,\nand optimum rows, are the explicit expressions\n\n    f(x_i)-f(x_j)+f'(x_i)(x_j-x_i)\n                      +[f'(x_i)-f'(x_j)]^2/2<=0.\n\nThey hold by the necessity proof in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 applied\nto this globally convex1-smooth function, not by a Gram-only test.\nEquivalently one can insert these actual values into (3.3) and the\noptimum/terminal rows; the same global necessity argument proves\neach sign. Heights and offsets in this construction are fixed by\n(12.2), never chosen independently after a query.\n\nThe actual final value is r^2/2. Hence\n\n    V=r^2/x_0^2,\n    x_0=A*p+B*q+(T+1)*r >=(S_m+1)*r,\n    V<=1/(S_m+1)^2<1/(1+2S_m)=1/Z.                   (12.3)\n\nThis is a non-refuting example escaping the PROVED INDEX REGION,\nnot an example escaping the proposed UPPER BOUND. It verifies that\nthe remaining region contains physical full transcripts with two\ngenuine gradient changes. No legal counterexample beating the channel\nby a nonvanishing amount was obtained, and no such claim is made.\n\n## 13. Remaining interface and honesty of the handoff\n\nThe candidate portion (1.2), the exact endpoint lift, the general\npaid identity, the positive determinant factor, and the legal example\nare jointly derived above, but remain UNVERIFIED. The counterexample test gives\n(12.2)--(12.3), not a refutation of the upper bound. Therefore neither\nan all-pair theorem nor a physical upper-bound counterexample is\nasserted at the cap.\n\nEven a future proof of (10.5) for every pair would only establish the\nthree-sector family bound. Then (3.1), the affine endpoint\nlift, and the restricted formal-space PSD identity cease to be a\nlossless representation. No result here licenses deleting those\ndirections from the original function-class supremum.\n\nApplied skills: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples.\nMatlas was called once for finite smooth-convex interpolation; it\nreturned two unrelated statements, neither used. One web background\nsearch located Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, \"Smooth Strongly Convex\nInterpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods\",\narXiv:1502.05666, https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666. {"id": "21d26350c9141917", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T18:35:31.663225+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2r72 complete JOINT UNVERIFIED positive-height cone-simplex report: Law I exact whole-family supremum1/D_m; Law II arbitrary multilevel heights, moving vertical coordinate, changing faces and strict closest-optimum slack still give E<=beta^(2(2^m-1))c^(-2m). A general zero-query-normal-height screen forces some positive queried cap multiplier for any improvement. Two scoped laws only; original target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Positive-height cone simplices: two whole-word obstructions\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit is authorized. Original target OPEN.**\n\nWorker max2, assigned invocation72 after ended baseline71, controller cap1,\nended ceiling72 / attempted73. This is the complete joint mathematical draft\nfor exactly TWO declared laws. Its lemmas are not accepted facts and may not\nbe reused as accepted premises. No mathematical program, numerical or\narithmetic replay, executed trajectory, solver, formal call, or subagent was\nused. The analysis is symbolic. No automatic renewal is authorized.\n\n## 1. Scope, verdict, and the fixed physical word\n\nThe two laws do not produce a stronger lower witness. The proposed uniform\nobstructions are:\n\n* Law I, acute two-level heights with a full anisotropic Walsh dictionary:\n  the supremum of the actual closest-radius terminal score is exactly\n  1/D_m. The entire declared law, including every cap/core/absorption case,\n  is covered.\n* Law II, arbitrary label heights on a regular Walsh dictionary with an\n  explicit schedule-selected cap dilation: the score is at most\n  beta^(2(2^m-1)) c^(-2m). Face changes, different gradient norms, and strict\n  outgoing-optimum slack at the TRUE closest initial minimizer are allowed.\n  In fact the ratio to complete K/ray is less than 2^(1-m).\n\nLaw II also yields a broader necessary screen: for ANY cone simplex of the\nform conv(0,v_s), a trajectory with zero normal height at every QUERIED point\nhas the same product bound, irrespective of dimension, cone geometry, or\noutgoing-optimum slack. A useful witness must have positive normal height\nat a queried point. This is not a bound on arbitrary cap-active trajectories.\n\nFix any integer m>=1536 ONCE and put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nEvery child uses these same enclosing parameters. Write h=H_m,\nn=2^m-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D_m=1+2S, and P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t.\nAll physical steps, including the actual last leaf 1+a, remain present.\nEvery h_i>1. We use smoothness1; replacing F by L F gives the original GD\nsteps h_i/L with exactly the same points and normalized score.\n\nFor a constructed objective F with complete minimum set Z and initial point\nX_0 outside Z, the score in this report is\n\n    E=2 F(X_n)/dist(X_0,Z)^2.\n\nIt is an attained lower witness for the full W_n(h), not an upper bound on W.\nThe complete K_n(h) of accepted b98bb807d514e80d includes 1/D_m and ALL\nsingle cuts. The accepted statement of 1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives\nK_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n. We use only those inclusions. We do not evaluate or\nidentify the full ray-channel optimum. Accepted 931b9653ce0f7201 gives\nB_n/D_m<47/50 at exactly this scope. Its hypothetical full-function upper\nbound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) remains unproved.\n\nThe original all-n/all-h minimax problem, every integer benchmark split,\nattained minima, arbitrary finite dimensions, and W=2R are unchanged.\nNo original-target affirmative or negative conclusion follows from this draft.\n\n## 2. Literal finite-field realization and one static smooth objective\n\nLet r>=2 be finite, N=2^r, and let labels s,t range over F_2^r. For each\nnonzero t choose a real b_t>0. Set chi_t(s)=(-1)^(t dot s), where the dot\nproduct in the exponent is in F_2, and define ordinary REAL vectors\n\n    z_s=(b_t chi_t(s))_(t!=0) in R^(N-1),\n    v_s=(z_s,k_s) in R^N,\n    D=conv(0,{v_s:s in F_2^r}),\n    K=cone{v_s:s in F_2^r}.\n\nThe heights satisfy k_s>=0 and sum_s k_s>0. In the task's notation,\nk_s=eta_s/R with eta_s=R k_s; R>0 is the initial vertical magnitude.\nEvery nonzero character, including dependent characters, is present. No\nexpected Gram or change of Euclidean metric is being used. The exact Gram is\n\n    <v_s,v_u>=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2 chi_t(s)chi_t(u)+k_s k_u.       (2.1)\n\nFor completeness, sum_s chi_t(s)=0 when t!=0: translate the sum by a label\non which chi_t=-1. Also sum_s chi_t(s)chi_u(s)=N if t=u and0 otherwise,\nby applying the same argument to t+u. These give the Fourier identities below.\n\nPut mu=N^(-1)sum_s k_s>0 and khat_t=N^(-1)sum_s k_s chi_t(s). If\ng=(w,y)=sum_s alpha_s v_s and tau=sum_s alpha_s, then\n\n    alpha_s=[tau+sum_(t!=0)chi_t(s)w_t/b_t]/N,\n    tau=[y-sum_(t!=0)khat_t w_t/b_t]/mu = <ell,g>,\n    ell=((-khat_t/(mu b_t))_(t!=0),1/mu).                  (2.2)\n\nIndeed the horizontal equations determine all nonconstant Fourier\ncoefficients of alpha; the vertical equation determines its constant\ncoefficient. Thus the N columns v_s are linearly independent. The cone K is\nclosed, being the image of the closed nonnegative orthant under an\ninvertible real linear map, and\n\n    D={g in K:<ell,g><=1}.                                (2.3)\n\nThe point0 is an actual vertex, and D is a full-dimensional N-simplex.\n\nUse the SINGLE static objective\n\n    F_D(X)=max_(g in D){<X,g>-||g||^2/2}.                  (2.4)\n\nCompleting the square makes its unique maximizer G(X)=P_D(X). Here and\nbelow P denotes ordinary Euclidean metric projection. Existence follows\nfrom compactness of D, and strict convexity of squared distance gives\nuniqueness. The projection normal condition is\n\n    g in D, <X-g,-g><=0,\n    <X-g,v_s-g><=0 for EVERY original label s.             (2.5)\n\nNecessity follows by taking a one-sided derivative along each segment in D.\nSufficiency follows by expanding ||X-u||^2-||X-g||^2 for u in D. It is\nenough to check0 and the listed vertices because the expression is affine\nin u. Consequently (2.5) includes every remote label and every tie.\n\nIf g=P_D(X) and q=P_D(Y), adding their two normal inequalities gives\n\n    ||g-q||^2<=<g-q,X-Y>, hence ||g-q||<=||X-Y||.\n\nMaximization in (2.4) bounds its first-order remainder between0 and\n<G(Y)-G(X),Y-X>. This is at most ||Y-X||^2 and is o(||Y-X||).\nTherefore F_D is differentiable, convex, and grad F_D=P_D is 1-Lipschitz.\nThere are interior points of D at which P_D is locally the identity, so\nthe least global Lipschitz constant is1. No rescaled gradient dynamics occur.\n\nThe COMPLETE minimum is\n\n    min F_D=0,\n    Z=K^polar={X:<X,v_s><=0 for every s}.                  (2.6)\n\nThe forward implication is as follows. If some <X,v_s> is positive,\nthen a sufficiently small positive multiple theta v_s in D gives a positive\nvalue in (2.4). If every listed inner product is nonpositive, every g in D\nhas nonpositive <X,g>, and g=0 attains0. In particular (0,-R) is a true\nminimizer. It is not generally the closest one.\n\n## 3. All physical rows, drift, and real Grams\n\nAt each actual original point X_i define\n\n    g_i=P_D(X_i), r_i=X_i-g_i,\n    F_i=<X_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n    delta_i=<r_i,g_i>>=0,\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i.                                 (3.1)\n\nThe terminal point X_n and g_n are included. If g_i=sum_s alpha_(i,s)v_s,\nthen alpha_(i,s)>=0, sum_s alpha_(i,s)<=1, and the actual vertical coordinate is\n\n    Y_i=-R-sum_(t<i)h_t sum_s alpha_(t,s) k_s <= -R.        (3.2)\n\nIt is never frozen or deleted. For the scaled dictionary of Law II the\nsame formula uses its actual heights M k_s and its actual coefficients.\n\nAll ordered smooth-convex interpolation rows reduce exactly to\n\n    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                               (3.3)\n\nThe inequality is (2.5) for the real point g_i in D. If X_star is ANY\npoint of the complete set Z, set g_star=F_star=0. Both optimum orientations\nare retained:\n\n    Q_(i,star)=<X_star,g_i><=0,\n    Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0.                               (3.4)\n\nStrict outgoing-optimum slack means -<X_star,g_i>>0. It is distinct from\nthe normal height delta_i. A cone trajectory can have the former positive\nand the latter zero. This distinction is essential here.\n\nLet V be the matrix of real columns v_s and Gamma=V^T V from (2.1). Then\nevery gradient Gram entry is alpha_i^T Gamma alpha_j. For any chosen\ninitial displacement Z_0=X_0-X_star, entries involving it are\n<Z_0,g_i>=Z_0^T V alpha_i and <Z_0,Z_0>=||Z_0||^2. Coordinates of every\nphysical X_i follow from X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t V alpha_t. This is the full\nliteral real Gram; positive semidefiniteness follows by writing any\nquadratic form as a squared Euclidean norm. No restricted formal gradient\nspace, remote-row deletion, or terminal-jet substitution is involved.\n\n## 4. Law I: unequal two-level heights and an acute quotient\n\nChoose any finite r>=2, every b_t>0 arbitrarily, one nonzero character u,\nR>0, two heights p,q>0 with p!=q, and any real X. Write b=b_u. Declare\n\n    k_s=p if chi_u(s)=+1, k_s=q if chi_u(s)=-1,\n    pq>=b^2,\n    X_0=(X e_u,-R).                                      (4.1)\n\nHere e_u is the physical unit coordinate at character u. All these data\nare fixed before the run. This entire domain, not only a selected start,\nis Law I. Rank may equal m; all other weights may be large, unequal, or\ndepend on the whole word. A fully specified representative is r=m,\nb_t=1+j(t)/N in binary label order, u the first basis label, p=2b,\nq=b, R=1, with X specified in the attainment formula below.\n\nFor each a in the subgroup u dot a=0, the real orthogonal map that\nmultiplies horizontal coordinate t by chi_t(a) and fixes the vertical\ncoordinate permutes the vertices by s -> s+a and preserves D. Its common\nfixed subspace consists exactly of the coordinate u and the vertical\ncoordinate: the only characters trivial on that subgroup are0 and u.\nProjection uniqueness forces every projected gradient of a point in that\nplane to stay there. The complete trajectory therefore stays there under\nordinary GD, without any coordinate rescaling.\n\nThe fixed-plane section of D is exactly the triangle\n\n    T=conv(0,w_+,w_-), w_+=(b,p), w_-=(-b,q).              (4.2)\n\nTo see equality, averaging each vertex over the subgroup gives the\ncorresponding w_sign, so T is contained in the section. Conversely, the\nhorizontal/vertical components of any convex combination, when projected\nto that plane, belong to T. The same symmetry argument applies to the\nclosest projection onto the full polar Z: group averaging of a minimizer\ncannot increase distance from the invariant initial point, and uniqueness\nputs the closest point in the fixed plane.\n\nAt (X,-R), the two vertex scores are bX-pR and -bX-qR. They cannot both\nbe positive. If both are nonpositive the run is already at a minimizer;\nwe omit that zero-radius case from normalized scores. Otherwise let w be\nthe uniquely positive one, w' the other, l=||w||, e=w/l, and\n\n    t_0=<X_0,e>>0, X_star=X_0-t_0 e.                     (4.3)\n\nThis is the TRUE closest initial minimizer. For example if w=w_+, then\n\n    X_star=[(pX+bR)/(b^2+p^2)](p,-b),\n    <X_star,w_->=-b(p+q)(pX+bR)/(b^2+p^2)<0,\n    <X_star,w_+>=0.                                     (4.4)\n\nHere bX>pR implies X>0. Thus X_star lies in Z and its residual t_0e is\nin K and perpendicular to X_star. For any Y in Z,\n<t_0e,Y-X_star><=0; expanding squared distance proves closestness. The\nnegative sign case follows by replacing X by -X and exchanging p,q.\nThe exact radius is t_0, whereas ||X_0-(0,-R)||=|X| is only another\nminimizer's distance. We do not substitute the latter in the upper bound.\n\nThe acute condition is <w,w'>=pq-b^2>=0. Whenever the inactive score is\nnonpositive, set t=<X_i,e>. The exact projection is\n\n    g_i=clip(t,[0,l]) e.                                 (4.5)\n\nHere is a direct guard check, not merely a symmetry assertion. For\n0<t<l the residual is perpendicular to e; its inner product with w'\nis <X_i,w'>-t<e,w'><=0. For t>=l, its inner product with g_i is\nl(t-l)>=0, its guard against w is equality, and its inner product with\nw' is nonpositive. The guard against w'-g_i is therefore nonpositive.\nFor t<=0 both vertex scores are nonpositive and g_i=0. These prove (2.5)\non0,w,w'. Every original remote vertex has, against a fixed-plane\nresidual, the same inner product as the corresponding w_sign. Thus every\noriginal normal inequality holds. Subtracting h_i g_i cannot increase\nthe inactive score because h_i>=0 and <w,w'>>=0. Induction proves (4.5)\nfor the WHOLE trajectory, including a crossing into the polar minimum.\n\nThe exact scalar dynamics and physical points are\n\n    t_(i+1)=t_i-h_i clip(t_i,[0,l]),\n    X_i=X_star+t_i e.                                   (4.6)\n\nThis identity pays the vertical drift e_vertical times every scalar update.\nIt also makes the full Gram explicit: <g_i,g_j>=z_i z_j and\n<X_0-X_star,g_i>=t_0 z_i, where z_i=clip(t_i,[0,l]).\n\nOne can write every query without an implicit optimization. Let k be the\nfirst i<n with t_0-lP_i<=l, or k=n if none exists. For 0<=i<=k,\nt_i=t_0-lP_i. If k<n and t_k<=0, set t_i=t_k for all i>=k. If k<n and\n0<t_k<=l, set t_i=(1-h_k)t_k<=0 for every i>=k+1. These are all cases,\nsince the earlier gradients equal l and a nonpositive state is absorbing.\nThe true minimum set remains the full polar cone, not this one ray.\n\n## 5. Law I: exact whole-word terminal and sharp obstruction\n\nSince every h_i>1, a queried t_i in (0,l] is sent to a nonpositive\nstate and never recovers. Consequently a positive terminal is possible\nonly if all queried gradients equal le. It occurs exactly when\nt_0>lS, and then t_n=t_0-lS>0. To prove the converse direction, if\nt_0>lS then every queried state in the cap formula is\nt_0-lP_i>l(S-P_i)>=l h_i>l, so that formula is indeed valid through\nthe actual final update. If t_0<=lS, either absorption occurs earlier or\nthe all-cap formula gives a nonpositive terminal. Thus no case is omitted.\n\nFor z=t_0/l>0, define the one-sided unit Huber function\n\n    Hplus(v)=0 for v<=0;\n             v^2/2 for 0<=v<=1;\n             v-1/2 for v>=1.\n\nEquation (2.4), or the direct cap projection, gives the exact score\n\n    E=2 Hplus(z-S)/z^2.                                 (5.1)\n\nThis is an exact formula for the static body's ACTUAL terminal value,\neven when the absorbed physical point differs from X_star+(t_0-lS)e.\nFor z<=S it is0. For S<z<S+1 it is (z-S)^2/z^2, increasing in z and\nat most 1/(S+1)^2<=1/(2S+1). For z>=S+1 it is\n(2z-D_m)/z^2, and\n\n    z^2-D_m(2z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2>=0.\n\nTherefore E<=1/D_m for every parameter choice in Law I. Equality is\nattained at z=D_m: choose the positive side and select explicitly\n\n    X=[(b^2+p^2)D_m+pR]/b.                              (5.2)\n\nThen t_0=lD_m and X_n has positive Huber argument S+1. No supremum-only\nlimiting instance or incomplete minimum set is involved. Thus\n\n    sup_(Law I) E=1/D_m<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h),\n    B_n E<47/50.                                        (5.3)\n\nPositive heights relative to the nearby minimizer (0,-R) do not help this\nlaw: at the closest minimizer X_star, <X_star,g_i>=0 for every nonzero\ngradient. This conclusion was derived, not imposed as a frozen carrier.\n\n## 6. Cone geometry, independently derived for Law II\n\nFor a closed convex cone K let g=P_K(X). Taking competitors0 and2g in\nthe normal condition gives <X-g,g>=0; taking arbitrary cone points gives\nr=X-g in K^polar. Conversely g in K, r in K^polar, and <r,g>=0 prove\nthe projection normal inequalities. Moreover r is the closest point in\nK^polar: for z in K^polar, <g,z-r><=0, and squared-distance expansion\nproves ||X-r||<=||X-z||. Consequently\n\n    dist(X,K^polar)=||P_K(X)||,\n    max_(g in K){<X,g>-||g||^2/2}=||P_K(X)||^2/2.          (6.1)\n\nThis is a complete direct proof of the cone decomposition used here;\nno unverified epigraph/universality finding is a premise.\n\nProjection existence onto the closed cone follows by restricting a\ndistance-minimizing sequence to a bounded ball and taking a convergent\nsubsequence; strict convexity gives uniqueness. Thus closed unbounded\ncones cause no attainment issue in this argument.\n\nFor h>=1, a conic gradient step is X'=r-(h-1)g. Since r is a genuine\nminimizer in K^polar,\n\n    dist(X',K^polar)<=(h-1)||g||.                        (6.2)\n\nThis inequality is Euclidean and dimension-independent. It does not require\ntwo rays, fixed support, commutative face projectors, or equal gradient norms.\n\nThere is also a diagnostic equivalence for the FINITE simplex D. If\ng=P_D(X) and delta=<X-g,g>, then\n\n    delta=0  iff  g=P_K(X) belongs to D.                 (6.3)\n\nIndeed delta=0 and the vertex guards imply <X-g,v_s><=0 for every s,\nwhile the residual is perpendicular to g. Equation (6.1) identifies the\ncone projection. Conversely a cone projection in D remains the closest\npoint in D and has zero delta. If delta>0, writing g=sum alpha_s v_s\nand using <X-g,v_s><=delta yields\ndelta<=delta sum alpha_s<=delta. Hence sum alpha_s=1. It is the finite\ncap, with positive normal multiplier, that distinguishes this case from\nconic motion. Merely lying on the cap with zero multiplier is insufficient.\n\n## 7. Law II: arbitrary multilevel heights and an explicit paid start\n\nChoose any finite r>=2, a scalar b>0, R>0, all label heights k_s>=0\nwith positive total, and a coefficient A>R k_0/[b^2(N-1)]. There is no\ntwo-level, common-height, or small-height restriction. Use base vectors\n\n    z_s^0=(b chi_t(s))_(t!=0), u_s=(z_s^0,k_s),\n    B=b^2(N-1), L0=sqrt(B+k_0^2),\n    X_0=(A z_0^0,-R),\n    t0=(AB-Rk_0)/(B+k_0^2)>0,\n    r0=(AB-Rk_0)/L0>0,\n    gamma0=(R+A k_0)/(B+k_0^2)>0,\n    X_star=(k_0 gamma0 z_0^0,-B gamma0).                 (7.1)\n\nThe symbol B here is a local squared vertex norm and is NOT benchmark B_n.\nThe regular Walsh Gram is\n\n    Gamma^0_su=<u_s,u_u>=b^2(N 1_(s=u)-1)+k_s k_u.       (7.2)\n\nWe have X_0-X_star=t0 u_0, <X_star,u_0>=0, and for EVERY s!=0,\n\n    <X_star,u_s>=-gamma0(b^2 k_0+B k_s)<=0.              (7.3)\n\nThus X_star is the closest point of the FULL cone polar by (6.1), and\nthe exact initial distance is r0. Formula (7.3) is an all-label guard,\nincluding zero heights and ties. It does not replace the full minimum by\nthe origin. For comparison, the nearby minimizer (0,-R) has distance\nA sqrt(B), generally larger than r0.\n\nLet ell0 be the vector (2.2) for these base columns u_s. Define the literal,\nunevaluated schedule quantity\n\n    Q=product_(i<n) max(1,h_i-1)\n     =product_(j=0)^(m-1) max(1,d_j)^(2^(m-1-j)),\n    M=1+2 ||ell0|| r0 Q.                                (7.4)\n\nThis is an explicit finite formula from the known word and selected\nparameters, not an optimizer, trajectory-dependent scale, numerical replay,\nor after-the-fact change of dictionary. Now freeze the actual body\n\n    v_s=M u_s,\n    D=conv(0,{M u_s}), K=cone{u_s},\n    actual b_t=M b, actual eta_s=R M k_s.                (7.5)\n\nThe actual Gram is M^2 Gamma^0, the true polar is unchanged, and the\nstatic smooth function is (2.4) for this scaled body. Scaling ALL columns\nselects the body size; it does not scale a coordinate of the GD metric.\n\nA completely specified schedule-only member, if one wants no free\nparameter selection, is r=m, b=R=1, k_s=(1+j(s))/D_m with j(s) the\nbinary integer label in {0,...,N-1}, and\nA=(R k_0+1)/B. Then AB-Rk_0=1, all N heights are distinct and positive,\nand (7.4) fixes M. This example is included in the obstruction; no gain\nis claimed for it. All members of the much broader parameter domain above\nare covered uniformly.\n\n## 8. Law II: every face, physical query, and cap guard\n\nLet pi_i=product_(t<i)(h_t-1), with pi_0=1. Then 0<pi_i<=Q for every\n0<=i<=n. We prove simultaneously by induction that the actual finite-body\ngradient is the cone projection and\n\n    ||g_i||<=r0 pi_i,\n    <ell0,g_i>/M<1/2.                                   (8.1)\n\nAt i=0 the base cone projection is t0 u_0 and has norm r0. By\nCauchy--Schwarz its base coefficient sum is\n<ell0,g_0><=||ell0||r0<M/2, so g_0 is in the actual simplex and its\nprojection normal inequalities hold against every M u_s and0. Suppose\nthe statement is known at a queried i. Equation (6.2) bounds the distance\nof X_(i+1) to the full polar by (h_i-1)||g_i||<=r0 pi_(i+1).\nThe cone projection at X_(i+1) therefore has norm at most this amount.\nIts base coefficient sum is nonnegative and at most\n||ell0||r0 Q<M/2. It belongs to D and (6.3) identifies it with the\nactual finite-body gradient. This proves (8.1) through the terminal n.\n\nAll cone faces are allowed; no itinerary was assumed in this induction.\nFor an exact piecewise formula for every physical query, let U_J denote\nthe matrix of base columns u_s for a subset J of labels. Every nonempty\nprincipal Gram Gamma^0_J is positive definite, because the complete\nmatrix of columns is invertible. Define\n\n    alpha_J(X)=(Gamma^0_J)^(-1) U_J^T X,\n    P_J=U_J (Gamma^0_J)^(-1) U_J^T. (8.3)\n\nOn that region g=P_J X and its actual simplex coefficients are\nalpha_s/M on J,0 off J, and the coefficient of the actual vertex0 is\n1-sum alpha_s/M>1/2. For J empty all listed vertex scores are\nnonpositive and g=0. Boundary zeros are removed from J, so the positive\nsupport is unique; inactive normal equalities are allowed. These formulas\nfollow from the normal condition, not from an expected face or a projected\ntranscript supplied by another construction.\n\nWriting J_i for the uniquely specified region of X_i, every original\npoint and query is exactly\n\n    X_i=(I-h_(i-1)P_(J_(i-1))) ... (I-h_0 P_(J_0)) X_0,\n    g_i=P_(J_i)X_i, 0<=i<=n,                            (8.4)\n\nwith the empty product at i=0. The order of these generally noncommuting\nmatrices is the physical order of H_m. (8.5)\n\nHere e_0 in the Gram formula is the unit vector at label0 in the\ncoefficient space R^N, not a new physical coordinate or a changed metric.\n\nEvery coordinate, common component, cap guard, optimum row, and terminal\nrow is covered. The exact terminal value is F_D(X_n)=||g_n||^2/2,\nand the exact score is alpha_n^T Gamma^0 alpha_n/r0^2. This is a real\nbody with an explicit initialization, not a representation theorem for a\npreexisting adverse transcript.\n\n## 9. This symbolic induction retains the enclosing\nm and every actual final leaf. It is not an evaluated recurrence.\nAt the physical horizon n=2^m-1,\n\n    E<=beta^(2n)c^(-2m). Substitution into\nA_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, A_0=1, gives\n\n    A_j=1+sum H_j=(1-v)2^j+v c^j.\n\nHence A_m<c^m and D_m=2A_m-1<2c^m.
