It does not exclude all scalar functions, activate two\nindependent core curvatures, or prove an upper bound on the full W.\nThe original every-n/every-h minimax target remains OPEN.\n\n## 1. Original scope and provenance\n\nFor a predetermined finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))\nand fixed L>0, the original iteration is\n\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad F(x_i),\n    W_n(h)=sup 2*(F(x_n)-F(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2).\n\nThe supremum is over every finite Euclidean dimension, every finite\nconvex differentiable globally L-smooth F with an attained minimum,\nand every permitted initial point and minimizer. Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2.\nThe target is W_n^star/U_n^ZJ ->1 or a rigorous negative resolution,\nwith W_n^star=inf_(h>=0) W_n(h) and the original all-integer-split\nbenchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. No original quantifier is modified here.\n\nAccepted fact_id `931b9653ce0f7201` was read in full. It supplies the\nunconditional comparison B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 for the SAME hierarchy\nbelow. Its proposed full-class bound17/(16D_m) is explicitly unproved.\nOnly its unconditional benchmark comparison is used in Section10.\nNo other accepted input is needed for the new mathematical proofs.\nThe project glossary was inspected; hierarchy a,c,H_j,A_j and local\nenergy functions below are scoped notation, not replacements of\nunrelated project symbols with the same letters.\n\nThe complete prior joint report `05423f48b823e3bf` was read as awareness.\nIts ratio-band theorem is not imported as an accepted premise. Every\npremise needed for(T) and(P) is proved jointly below. In particular,\nno ratio-free extension of its weighted capture or common-radius\nplateau is assumed. TASK.md and guidance `4455d6e36806d677` govern this\ninvocation; the subsequent `231e15a7ee479cc6` leaves this allocation\nunchanged. Latest guidance `5fee1ea5f63fd8fc` was also read and explicitly\ncontinues max3 invocation76 under its existing cap. The recorded plan is\n`a50f743debae0e74`.\n\n## 2. Same-enclosing-m word and all parameter guards\n\nFix any integer m>=1536 ONCE. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_j=1+2*S_j=2*A_j-1.\n\nAll children retain this exact m,epsilon,beta,c,a. H_j has2^j-1 steps.\nThe largest PHYSICAL bridge parameter is d_*=d_(m-1); d_m is only\nan auxiliary ruler. Let ell=1+a be the first and last physical step,\nand b=a*beta. Every step is greater than1, and every bridge of level\nat least1 is immediately preceded and followed by a leaf ell. This\nlast property follows inductively from the literal concatenation;\nthere are no adjacent nonleaf bridges.\n\nHere epsilon<=1/6144<1/100, beta>99/100, and\n\n    2<c<(49/20)*(101/100)<5/2,\n    1/3<99/250<a<1/lambda<1/2,\n    0<b<1/2, c*b=beta^2<1.\n\nThe c bound uses sqrt(2)<29/20. Set eta=a/(c-2). Since\nlambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda, one has c*(c-2)>1 and0<eta<1.\nSumming the literal word gives\n\n    A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,\n    d_j/A_j <= c-2 <1/2,                            (2.1)\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1.\n\nThe closed expression has initial value1 and the correct update because\n2*eta+a=c*eta. The ratio in(2.1) is strictly increasing in j: its\nreciprocal is\n\n    A_j/d_j = 1/(c-2)+((1-eta)/a)*(2/c)^j.            (2.2)\n\nTwo placement guards will be used repeatedly. First,\n\n    A_j-d_j>ell for every j>=1.                      (2.3)\n\nIndeed A_j-d_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*(3-c)*c^j. Both coefficients\nare positive and both powers increase with j. At j=1 its value is\n2+a-beta>1+a=ell. In particular a*d_j<A_j-ell.\n\nSecond, for every2<=j<=m,\n\n    d_j>A_(j-1),\n    (c-1)*d_(j-1)-A_(j-1)<0.                        (2.4)\n\nAt j=2,\n\n    c*(d_2-A_1)=2*epsilon-lambda^2*epsilon^2*(1+epsilon)>0,\n\nsince lambda^2<6 and6*epsilon*(1+epsilon)<2. If d_j>A_(j-1),\nthen d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)*d_j-2*A_(j-1)>0, because\nc-1/c>lambda-1/lambda=2. The second inequality in(2.4) follows\nfrom(2.1) and(c-1)*(c-2)<(3/2)*(1/2)<1.\n\nAt the final level,\n\n    d_m^2>D_m.                                      (2.5)\n\nTo see this, a>1/3 and A_m<c^m imply d_m^2>c^(2m)/9, while\nD_m<2*c^m. Since c^m>2^m>=32>18 for m>=5, the first quantity\nexceeds the second. All advertised m satisfy this.\n\nFinally Gamma_m>d_*>1. The latter follows from d_2=beta*c>1 and\nm-1>=2. With A=A_(m-1), d=d_*, one has d<A/2 and\nD_m=4*A+2*d-1>4*A, since d>1. Thus\n2*a*D_m>(8/3)*A>d, proving Gamma_m^2>d^2. These estimates justify\nevery use of an inactive positive clipping threshold under(T).\n\n## 3. Static functions, complete queries, minima, and every row\n\nFor u,v>0 let\n\n    g(x)=clip(x,[-v,u]), f(x)=x*g(x)-g(x)^2/2.\n\nEquivalently f is x^2/2 on[-v,u], is u*x-u^2/2 on x>=u,\nand is -v*x-v^2/2 on x<=-v. Its derivative g is continuous,\nnondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. Partitioning an interval at the two\nthresholds proves the Lipschitz assertion directly. Consequently f\nis finite, convex, differentiable, globally1-smooth and has the\ncomplete minimum set{0}, with f(0)=0. For general L set F=L*f.\nThe original updates with steps h_i/L then coincide with\n\n    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),\n    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n.                  (3.1)\n\nThese specify ALL physical queries and the original terminal on one\nfixed function. The initial radius about the complete minimum is\nexactly |s|. All theorem statements cover this full literal trajectory,\nnot an auxiliary trajectory with freely chosen gradients.\n\nLet y_i=x_i-g_i. Direct substitution of the displayed f gives every\nordered interpolation residual, including terminal and optimum,\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2\n        =y_j*(g_i-g_j)<=0.                          (3.2)\n\nIf y_j>0, then g_j=u and g_i<=u. If y_j<0, then g_j=-v and\ng_i>=-v. If y_j=0 the residual is zero. This proves both orders,\nall remote rows, threshold equalities and repeated points. At the\noptimum x=g=f=0 the same formula applies. The complete Gram matrix\nof the initial displacement and all gradients is an ordinary scalar\nEuclidean Gram: every quadratic form is the square of a linear\ncombination of these real numbers, hence is nonnegative. Scaling by\nL gives the original L-smooth residuals without changing the score.\n\nThe one-sided comparison function is\n\n    g_infty(x)=max(x,-v),\n    f_infty(x)=x^2/2 for x>=-v,\n               -v*x-v^2/2 for x<=-v.                (3.3)\n\nIt has the same regularity, complete minimum{0}, true radius |s|,\nphysical query definition(3.1), and all residuals(3.2). For this\nfunction y_j is either0 or negative, so the proof of(3.2) is unchanged.\nIts positive curvature is1, not0. All its definitions are explicit;\nno limit interchange u->infinity is invoked.\n\nFor either function f(x)<=x^2/2 everywhere. On a clipped ray the\ndifference is (|x|-b_side)^2/2. Reflection swaps u and v, changes the\nsign of every iterate, and preserves the score. We may therefore\nassume u>=v when proving(T), and reflect the complete conclusion.\n\nFor reference retain BOTH signed endpoints. For a finite-clipping\nstep1+d, the exact image of[-p,q], p,q>=0, is[-p',q'], with\n\n    p'=max(d*min(q,u), p-(1+d)*v),\n    q'=max(d*min(p,v), q-(1+d)*u).                   (3.4)\n\nThe first terms are nonnegative. The opposite-sign extremum occurs\nat the included threshold or endpoint on each ray; the remaining\nsame-sign extremum occurs at the outer endpoint. Continuity fills\nthe interval. Thus this formula includes all threshold equalities.\nIf Psi_j denotes the whole H_j map and Phi_j its signed interval\nmap, then Phi_0 is the identity and\n\n    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j o E_(d_j) o Phi_j.               (3.5)\n\nEach Phi_j is coordinatewise nondecreasing by interval inclusion.\nAll histories in(3.5) are compatible images of one continuous map;\nthere is no independent choice of child maxima. For(3.3), the exact\none-step formula becomes\n\n    p'=max(d*q,p-(1+d)*v), q'=d*min(p,v).             (3.6)\n\nThese formulas are used as symbolic identities only. No trajectory\nor recurrence was run as a program.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: a genuine signed-energy obstruction\n\nOne tempting ratio-free primitive envelope is\n\n    |Psi_m(s)+S_m*g(Psi_m(s))| <= |s|.               (4.1)\n\nFor this clipped function its squared left side equals the signed\nobjective/gradient expression\n\n    x_n^2+2*S_m*f_n+S_m*(S_m+1)*g_n^2,\n\nbecause f_n=x_n*g_n-g_n^2/2. Thus(4.1) retains the actual clipped\nobjective weight and is stronger than a bare radius claim. It would\nimply the desired objective bound: on either ray, maximizing\n2f(x)/(x+S_m*g(x))^2 gives1/D_m by completing the saturated-ray\nsquare, while the core ratio is1/A_m^2<=1/D_m.\n\nNevertheless(4.1) is false for every advertised m. Put\n\n    A=A_(m-1), d=d_(m-1), S=A-1,\n    choose u>0, v=d*u/A, s=A*u.                     (4.2)\n\nHere0<v<u since d/A<1/2. Every query of the first child is\nx=u*(A-P), where P is its actual prefix mass, and that child ends\nat u. The root sends u to-d*u=-A*v. Every query of the second\nchild is x=-v*(A-Q), where Q is its actual prefix mass. The ACTUAL\nterminal after its last leaf is -v. All queried saturated guards hold\nbecause both children end at their threshold and all earlier prefix\nmasses are smaller. The function, rows, complete minimum and radius\nare those of Section3, with own radius A*u.\n\nTo check the strict violation, first prove d/A>1/lambda. At level2,\n\n    lambda*d_2-A_2\n      =epsilon*(1+4/[lambda*(1+epsilon)]-lambda^2*epsilon)>0.\n\nHere lambda^2*epsilon<6/6144<1. The ratios d_j/A_j increase by(2.2),\nso the same strict comparison holds at m-1>=2. If t=d/A, then\n\n    |x_n+S_m*g_n|/|s|=A_m*d/A^2=t*(2+t)>1,           (4.3)\n\nbecause (1/lambda)*(2+1/lambda)=1. This includes the small-side\nclipping weight exactly. It is not a claim that the objective itself\nincreases beyond the target: this trajectory has score d^2/A^4, and\n\n    D_m*d^2/A^4 < (5*A-1)/(4*A^2)<1                 (4.4)\n\nsince d/A<1/2, D_m<5*A-1 and A>2. The latter inequality follows\nfrom4*A^2-5*A+1=(4*A-1)*(A-1)>0 for A>2.\n\nA second direct energy-only closure also loses necessary history.\nIf one replaces a child's actual image by the entire sublevel\n2f<=R^2/D_j, then on a saturated ray with threshold1 the permissible\nradius is (R^2/D_j+1)/2. Passing this enlarged symmetric interval\nthrough a bridge and another child objective bound yields, for large\nR, a bound with leading growth R^4/(4*D_j^3). The desired parent\nbound grows only as R^2/D_(j+1). For sufficiently large R the former\ncannot prove the latter. This is an obstruction to that enlargement,\nnot an actual trajectory counterexample: the omitted affine prefix\nhistory is precisely the missing information.\n\nMechanism1 is therefore not promoted to a uniform invariant. The next\nmechanism keeps threshold-conditioned affine exterior branches and\nexact signed interval capture, rather than assuming(4.1).\n\n## 5. Mechanism2: exact capture for one-sided clipping\n\nThroughout Sections5-6 use ONE fixed function(3.3), with v>0.\nFor j>=1 define the positive capacity\n\n    Q_j=(A_j-ell)/a.\n\nBy(2.3), Q_j>0 and d_j<Q_j. For every j>=1 the following exact\ninterval identity holds:\n\n    Psi_j([-A_j*v,Q_j*v])=[-v,a*v].                 (5.1)\n\nThe two exterior maps are also exact:\n\n    Psi_j(-p)=-p+S_j*v,        p>=A_j*v,\n    Psi_j( q)=-a*q+(S_j-ell)*v, q>=Q_j*v.           (5.2)\n\nFor the first branch every original query is negative saturated and\nthe terminal is at most-v. For the second, the first physical leaf\nsends q to-a*q; the ENTIRE remaining physical suffix is negative\nsaturated and ends at most-v, because\n\n    a*q >= (A_j-ell)*v=(1+S_j-ell)*v.\n\nThus no suffix query or final leaf is discarded. At equality the\nterminal is exactly-v. These exterior formulas do not rely on(5.1).\nThe global positive output bound\n\n    positive endpoint of Psi_j(I) <= a*v            (5.3)\n\nholds for every interval I and j>=1, because the final leaf sends\nany positive input negative and any negative input to at most a*v.\n\nAt j=1, A_1=2+a and Q_1=1/a. Formula(3.6) applied to the capacity\nrectangle gives p'=max(a*Q_1*v,(A_1-ell)*v)=v and q'=a*v.\nThis proves(5.1) in the base case.\n\nAssume(5.1) at j. For the parent capacity rectangle the exact first\nchild image, using(5.1),(5.2),(5.3), is\n\n    [-(A_j+d_j+1)*v, a*v].\n\nIndeed the two outer endpoints have this same negative magnitude,\nsince A_(j+1)-S_j=A_j+d_j+1 and\na*Q_(j+1)-(S_j-ell)=A_j+d_j+1. The central rectangle has image\n[-v,a*v], and the two exterior pieces only extend the negative\nendpoint. The bridge then has exact image\n\n    [-A_j*v,d_j*v].\n\nFor its negative endpoint use a*d_j<A_j, from(2.3), and subtract\n(1+d_j)*v from the previous negative magnitude. For its positive\nendpoint use that the prior negative magnitude exceeds v. Since\nd_j<Q_j, this bridge image lies inside the child capacity rectangle.\nThe second child therefore maps into[-v,a*v]. Both endpoint values\nare actually attained: input -A_j*v produces-v, while input\n-(A_j-ell)*v produces+a*v by remaining negative saturated until the\nlast query -v and then taking the final leaf. Both inputs belong to\n[-A_j*v,d_j*v]. Continuity fills the interval. This proves(5.1)\nwith compatible full-child histories.\n\nThis is a different rectangle from the failed finite-ratio weighted\ncapture claim. Its upper capacity Q_j and upper output a*v are fixed\nby the first/last leaf of this one-sided static function.\n\n## 6. All-amplitude one-sided profile, including every intermediate scale\n\nWrite Phi_j^infty for the exact signed interval map of(3.3). For all\nj>=1 and every integer r>=0 we claim the two coordinate bounds\n\n    Phi_j^infty(d_j*v*b^r,d_j*v*b^r)\n          <= (v*b^(r+1),v*b^(r+1)).                 (6.1)\n\nThe inequality is coordinatewise; the actual image need not be\nsymmetric. At j=1, d_1=beta<1. The whole input interval lies in the\nquadratic region[-v,infinity), and the leaf is exactly x->-a*x.\nIts endpoint magnitudes are b*v*b^r. This proves the base for ALL r.\n\nFor the unscaled case r=0 at j>=2, abbreviate A=A_(j-1),d=d_(j-1).\nThe input radius d_j*v is greater than A*v by(2.4), but is less\nthan Q_(j-1)*v, since\n\n    a*d_j=beta*d < A-ell                              (6.2)\n\nby(2.3). Thus(5.1)-(5.3) give the exact first-child signed endpoints\n\n    p=(d_j-S_(j-1))*v>v, q=a*v.\n\nThe bridge maps these to\n\n    p'=a*d*v, q'=d*v.\n\nIn fact the competing term in p' is\n[d_j-A-d]*v=[(c-1)*d-A]*v<0 by(2.4). This exact asymmetric image\nis contained in[-d*v,d*v]. The second child's r=0 induction bound\nthen gives both output magnitudes at most b*v.\n\nFor r>=1, the input radius satisfies\n\n    d_j*v*b^r=(c*b)*d_(j-1)*v*b^(r-1)\n               <=d_(j-1)*v*b^(r-1).\n\nThe first child's induction hypothesis and interval inclusion give\nactual endpoints p,q<=v*b^r<v. The bridge is therefore linear on\nthe entire actual image, with endpoints d*q,d*p. These are at most\nd_(j-1)*v*b^r. The second child's induction hypothesis at that same\nr bounds both outputs by v*b^(r+1). This completes simultaneous\ninduction in j with ALL integers r retained. No optimized children\nare identified or combined.\n\nFor ANY real0<R<=d_j*v, choose the unique applicable geometric cell\n\n    d_j*v*b^(r+1)<R<=d_j*v*b^r, r>=0.\n\nExact upper boundaries are included in the right-hand inequality.\nInterval inclusion and(6.1) give both endpoints at most\nv*b^(r+1)<R/d_j. Therefore\n\n    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j, 0<|s|<=d_j*v.              (6.3)\n\nIf s is in[-A_j*v,Q_j*v] and |s|>d_j*v, identity(5.1) bounds\n|Psi_j(s)| by v<|s|/d_j. Thus(6.3) holds on the WHOLE capacity\nrectangle. This treats amplitudes crossing arbitrarily many levels;\nit is not a finite-mesh argument. At s=0 the output is0.\n\nAt j=m, f_infty<=x^2/2 and(2.5) give the strict interior bound\n\n    2*f_infty(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <=1/d_m^2<1/D_m.         (6.4)\n\nFor negative exterior s=-z*v, z>=A_m, formula(5.2) gives score\n\n    (2*z-D_m)/z^2 <=1/D_m,\n\nbecause z^2-D_m*(2*z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2>=0. Equality is attained at\nz=D_m, with every physical query negative saturated, terminal-A_m*v,\ntrue radius D_m*v and gap D_m*v^2/2.\n\nFor positive exterior q>=Q_m*v, put z=q/v and M=D_m-2*ell>0.\nThe exact terminal is negative saturated by(5.2), and its score is\n\n    (2*a*z-M)/z^2 <=a^2/M.                          (6.5)\n\nThis follows from a^2*z^2-M*(2*a*z-M)=(a*z-M)^2. Furthermore\n\n    (1-a^2)*D_m >= (1-a^2)*(3+2*a)>2*(1+a)=2*ell,\n\nsince\n\n    (1-a^2)*(3+2*a)-2*(1+a)=(1+a)^2*(1-2*a)>0.\n\nHere D_m>=D_1=3+2*a. Consequently a^2/(D_m-2*ell)<1/D_m.\nThis proves the exact all-amplitude one-sided profile\n\n    sup_(s!=0) 2*f_infty(Psi_m(s))/s^2=1/D_m.        (6.6)\n\nEvery positive or negative start, both capacity equalities and the\nliteral final leaf have been covered. The positive exterior formula\nis a true affine saturated suffix, not a rescaled effective word.\n\n## 7. Uniform whole-word bound for every single-crossing history\n\nWe will need an elementary bound for EVERY position of H_m. At index i\nwrite P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t. If that step has level k,\nso h_i=1+d_k, then\n\n    d_k^2/[(1+P_i)^2*(1+2*T_i)] <=1/D_m.             (7.1)\n\nThis is an upper estimate of a scalar expression; it does not turn the\nusual single-cut lower witness into an upper bound on unrestricted W.\nHere is a direct proof with all occurrences covered.\n\nEvery level-k bridge has a complete H_k immediately before and after\nit, so P_i,T_i>=S_k. Put A=A_k,d=d_k and\nM=S_m-S_(k+1)>=0. There is some0<=x<=M for which\nP_i=S_k+x and T_i=S_k+M-x. Their denominator is\n\n    (A+x)^2*(2*A-1+2*M-2*x).\n\nIts logarithm is concave on[0,M], because it is the sum of logarithms\nof positive affine functions. Hence its minimum is at an endpoint.\nThe endpoint x=M minus the endpoint x=0 is\n\n    M*[2*A*(A-1)+M*(2*A-1)]>=0.\n\nThe denominator is therefore at least A^2*(2*A-1+2*M). Also\nD_m=4*A+2*d-1+2*M. It remains to prove\n\n    d^2*(4*A+2*d-1) <= A^2*(2*A-1), d^2<=A^2.\n\nThe second follows from(2.1). At k=0 the first is\n a^2*(3+2*a)<1, since a<1/2. At k>=1, A=A_k>2 and d/A<1/2,\nso\n\n    (d/A)^2*(4*A+2*d-1)/(2*A-1)\n      <(1/4)*(5*A-1)/(2*A-1) <=3/4<1,\n\nwhere5*A-1<=3*(2*A-1) is equivalent to A>=2. Adding2*M*d^2\nversus2*M*A^2 proves(7.1), including the first and last leaf.\n\nNow take any finite u,v and a positive start s for which the physical\ntrajectory has a FIRST positive-to-negative crossing at step i and\nNO subsequent return to positive. Zero states have zero final gap\nand can be omitted from this discussion. Before step i every query\nis positive saturated: an unsaturated positive query would cross at\nits very next step because all h_t>1. Thus the incoming query is\n\n    y=s-P_i*u>0,\n    min(y,u)<=s/(1+P_i).\n\nThe latter inequality follows separately from y>=u, which implies\ns>= (1+P_i)*u, and y<=u, which implies s=P_i*u+y>=(1+P_i)*y.\nThe crossing lands at-r, with\n\n    0<r<=d_k*min(y,u)<=d_k*s/(1+P_i).                (7.2)\n\nFor y<=u the first inequality is equality r=d_k*y. For y>=u,\nr=(1+d_k)*u-y<=d_k*u.\n\nWith no return, all subsequent negative queries must be saturated;\nan unsaturated one would become positive at its next step. The\nterminal magnitude is w=r-T_i*v>0. If w>=v, its energy is\n\n    2*f(-w)=2*v*r-(2*T_i+1)*v^2<=r^2/(2*T_i+1).\n\nIf0<w<=v, then v>=r/(T_i+1), so w<=r/(T_i+1) and\n2*f(-w)=w^2<=r^2/(T_i+1)^2<=r^2/(2*T_i+1). This includes T_i=0.\nTogether with(7.1)-(7.2) it gives\n\n    2*f(x_n)/s^2 <=1/D_m.                            (7.3)\n\nIf a positive trajectory has no crossing at all, all nonterminal\nqueries are positive saturated and x_n=s-S_m*u>=0. A saturated\nterminal has the usual squared-difference bound1/D_m. A core terminal\nhas score at most1/A_m^2<=1/D_m: writing y=x_n in[0,u], one has\ns=S_m*u+y>=A_m*y. Thus ALL zero-crossing and single-crossing\nhistories are controlled, at every clipping ratio, without assuming\nthat such histories cover the whole family.\n\n## 8. Complete finite-clipping transfer on the large-ratio domain\n\nAssume u>=v and u/v>=Gamma_m. By Section2 this implies\nu>=d_*v. There is a useful preliminary domain with the weaker\ncondition u>=d_*v:\n\n    for every initial s<=u, the finite-clipping trajectory and\n    objective agree exactly with the one-sided function(3.3).       (8.1)\n\nThis includes all negative starts of arbitrary magnitude. To prove it,\nany negative-to-positive crossing produces a positive state at most\nd_i*v<=d_*v<=u. Every positive query in[0,u] uses g=x and immediately\nbecomes nonpositive. A negative state that stays negative has the same\nupdate for both functions. Starting either nonpositive or in[0,u]\ntherefore never visits x>u. Induction over the literal physical steps\nproves(8.1), including the terminal. Section6 yields the target bound\nfor every such start. Equality at a negative saturated start remains\navailable. No continuity limit in the clipping ratio is needed.\n\nIt remains under(T) to cover positive starts s>u. The zero-state,\nzero-crossing and single-crossing cases are covered in Section7.\nSuppose instead that after its first positive-to-negative crossing\nthe trajectory returns to positive at least once. From its FIRST\nsuch return onwards, every positive state is at most d_*v<=u,\nand upper clipping is inactive forever, by exactly the argument for(8.1).\n\nEvery later positive-to-negative crossing has magnitude at most\n\n    a*d_*v.                                         (8.2)\n\nIf its step is a leaf, multiply the positive query, at most d_*v,\nby a. If its step is a nonleaf bridge, its preceding physical step\nis a leaf. The positive\nbridge query is therefore at most a*v. Multiplication by its own\nd_i<=d_* proves(8.2). The initial return itself may occur at a\nnonleaf bridge, but its next leaf is covered by the first case.\nThis proves the assertion for every subsequent crossing, with no\nlimit on their number or positions.\n\nWhile a negative state remains negative it decreases in magnitude:\na negative core query would cross, while a negative saturated one\nadds the positive increment h_i*v. Substituting (4.2), its surviving quadratic is\n-g^T Nmat y. Incoming-optimum rows have g_star=0; their value contribution\nis nevertheless present in the preceding flow calculation. From (3.1),\n\n    -g^T Nmat y\n       =-<x_0,e^T(Pmat+A)y>+y^T Pmat y,\n    e^T Pmat=2*p82*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290, or direct expansion of the displayed outgoing\nterminal rows, now gives the ENTIRE original identity\n\n    M(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2+R(q82),\n    R(q82)=y^T K y+eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2\n                         +(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.  (4.3)\n\nFor direct checking, before completing the common square the prefix form is\n\n    (q82-s82^2)||x_0||^2+2(s82-p82)<x_0,v^T y>\n                              +eta||v^T y||^2+y^TKy+<x_0,d^Ty>.\n\nThe equality eta(p82+s82)=p82-s82 completes it to (4.3). This is an identity\non arbitrary original x_0,g_0,...,g_n, not merely a legal trajectory.\n\nThe common-gradient derivative is also explicit. Set every queried\ngradient to the same arbitrary vector G. Then y_i=x_0-t_i G and\nv^Ty=(p82+s82)x_0-U82 G. Therefore the FULL prefix restriction is\n\n    R_common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-<x_0,(d^T t)G>\n                                      +(t^TKt+eta U82^2)||G||^2. (4.4)\n\nIn particular an imbalance d is not free: it changes the derivative in\nG at G=0 by -(d^T t)x_0. Both optimum orientations are in (4.2), and\ntheir true contribution has not been replaced by a contact assumption.\n\n## 5. Law I: complete physical-tree squares, with separate boundaries\n\nUse uniform queried terminal weights\n\n    alpha_i=u=(1-p82)/n, alpha_star=p82.                    (I.1)\n\nFor each actual dyadic cell\n\n    I_(j,bidx)={bidx*2^j,...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2},\n    2<=j<=m, 0<=bidx<2^(m-j),\n\nlet Lcell and Rcell be its genuine left and right queried child sets,\neach of size l_j=2^(j-1)-1, and let rootcell be its actual checkpoint.\nDefine two coefficient columns of length n, zero outside the cell:\n\n    psi^-=(1_Lcell-1_Rcell)/sqrt(2l_j),\n    psi^+=(1_Lcell+1_Rcell-2l_j e_rootcell)\n                                      /sqrt(2l_j(2l_j+1)).  (I.2)\n\nThe first contrast retains the two child means independently. The second\nretains the checkpoint relative to both children. Each has sum zero and\nnorm one, and their inner product is zero. A descendant contrast has sum\nzero inside a child on which an ancestor contrast is constant. Thus all\nthese columns are mutually orthonormal. Inducting on a cell, its two\nchildren contribute 2(l_j-1) details, the displayed columns contribute\ntwo, and its common column contributes one, totaling 2l_j+1 coordinates.\nThe singleton H_1 cells need just their common column. Consequently\nthere are n-1 columns in (I.2), and together with e/sqrt(n) they form\nan orthonormal basis of the ENTIRE original queried-coordinate space.\nExplicitly, for a cell beginning at o=bidx*2^j, Lcell is\n{o,...,o+2^(j-1)-2}, rootcell=o+2^(j-1)-1, and Rcell is\n{o+2^(j-1),...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2}.\n\nThe literal two-parameter weight law is, for kappa>0 and theta>0,\n\n    omega^-_(j,bidx)=kappa*c^(-j),\n    omega^+_(j,bidx)=theta*kappa*c^(-j),\n    K=sum_cells[omega^- psi^- (psi^-)^T\n                                      +omega^+ psi^+ (psi^+)^T], A=0. (I.3)\n\nEvery square coefficient is explicitly positive at every depth. The\nstronger exclusion below permits independently arbitrary positive omega\nat every column, so it covers any retuning within this complete basis.\nThat extension does not insert an unknown PSD block.\n\nEquations (4.1)--(4.2) give EVERY original row coefficient. In this law\nmu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i>0; terminal weights are strictly positive. The incoming\noptimum coefficients are literally\n\n    nu_i=sum_cells,sign omega*psi_i*(sum_j psi_j t_j)\n                                                +gamma U82 v_i-2u.  (I.4)\n\nThey are signed matching coefficients, not silently assumed nonnegative.\nAn original finite coefficient will necessarily be negative regardless\nof whether these nu_i have acceptable signs.\n\nFor every q82>=p82^2 the complete factor is\n\n    M_I(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_cells,sign omega||sum_i psi_i y_i||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2+(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2.      (I.5)\n\nThis supplies all details and the common mode, since e^Tv=p82+s82>0 and\neta>0. With q82>p82^2 it is positive definite in all the original variables;\nat q82=p82^2 it has the stated explicit nonnegative factorization. No child\nPSD premise remains to prove. The only failed certificate requirement\nwill be the signs of the matched interpolation multipliers.\n\n### 5.1 An exact first forced sign, with the physical root retained\n\nLet omega^- and omega^+ now denote the two weights at the WHOLE word.\nOnly those two contrasts can contain both endpoints 0,z, or endpoint 0\nand root r. Since the child sizes are (n-1)/2,\n\n    K_0z=(-omega^-+omega^+/n)/(n-1),\n    K_0r=-omega^+/n.                                  (I.6)\n\nKe=0 implies (T82^T K)_0j=(1-b)K_0j=-a K_0j.\nHere Rvec_0=b*p82+a*u>0. The exact two signed entries are therefore\n\n    E_z=Nmat_0z=a*(omega^--omega^+/n)/(n-1)\n                                                   +gamma Rvec_0 v_z,\n    E_r=Nmat_0r=a*omega^+/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_r.           (I.7)\n\nThe last queried leaf has k_z=b, so v_z=b-u>1. The root has a suffix\nequal to the SAME-parameter H_(m-1). Write l=2^(m-1)-1. The product\ncalculation in (3.3), applied with that unchanged a,beta,c, gives\n\n    k_r=-(a+c^(-(m-1)))*beta^l,\n    v_r=k_r-u>-(a+1)-1/n>-2.                         (I.8)\n\nConsequently Bpair=v_r+(n-1)v_z>n-3>0, and (I.7) gives\n\n    E_r+(n-1)E_z=a*omega^-+gamma Rvec_0 Bpair>0.       (I.9)\n\nThis identifies a negative ORIGINAL multiplier for every parameter choice:\nif E_z>0, lambda_(0,z)=-E_z<0; if E_z<=0, (I.9) forces\nlambda_(0,r)=-E_r<0. All denominators and prefactors used here are positive.\nThe conclusion holds for every m>=1536, every s82<p82<1, every q82>=p82^2,\nevery kappa,theta>0, and even independently arbitrary positive detail\nweights. Descendant weights cannot alter (I.6). Raising q82 cannot alter\nthe sign because it changes only the last x_0 square in (I.5).\n\nThe excluded rows are actual remote queried-prefix rows. They are not\nincoming-terminal rows, omitted leaves, or fictitious child interfaces.\n\n### 5.2 All coefficient re-selections of this same factor also fail\n\nThere is a useful stronger scope statement. Keep the exact factor (I.5)\nand exact terminal distribution (I.1), but allow any other original prefix\nrow coefficients matching that polynomial. If their row matrix is Nnew,\nput Bnew=T82^(-T)Nnew-Pmat. Comparing y-y and x_0-y coefficients in (4.3)\nforces Bnew to be skew and e^T Bnew=0, equivalently Bnew e=0. Conversely\nevery such balanced-skew term leaves the same polynomial unchanged.\nThus this argument describes all coefficient re-selections of this fixed\nfactor, rather than assuming the symmetric matching is unique.\n\nFor any balanced-skew Bnew, the first diagonal change is\n\n    (T82^T Bnew)_00=(1-b)Bnew_00+b(e^T Bnew)_0=0,\n\nand the first row-sum change is zero. Hence the sum of its first-row\noff-diagonal N entries is invariant and equals\n\n    mu_0-Nmat_00\n       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+s82-v_0)\n       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+u-s82/a).                (I.10)\n\nHere k_0=b*s82/a, since pi_n=-s82=(1-b)*product_(1<=t<n)(1-h_t).\nThe last factor is strictly positive throughout the parameter domain.\nIndeed p82+(1-p82)/n is increasing in p82; at p82=s82,\n\n    n*(p82+u-s82/a)=1-s82*(n*(1/a-1)+1)>0.\n\nFor the strict last bound, a>1/3 gives n*(1/a-1)+1<2n+1<2^(m+1),\nand s82<c^(-m) gives its product with s82 less than 2*(5/6)^m<1.\nThe inequality (6/5)^4=1296/625>2 proves this for every m>=4.\nAlso K_00>0, because the complete basis has a nonzero contrast at 0\nand every detail weight is positive. Thus (I.10)>0.\n\nIf ALL original lambda_(0,j) were nonnegative, then all off-diagonal\nNnew_0j would be nonpositive and their sum could not be positive.\nThis contradiction excludes EVERY nonnegative coefficient matching of\nthis particular full square factor and its fixed terminal distribution.\nIt does not exclude a changed square factor or a changed terminal pool.\n\n## 6. Law II: chronological terminal pool and a paid skew correction\n\nChoose the positive increasing distribution on ACTUAL prefix indices\n\n    alpha_i=2(i+1)(1-p82)/[n(n+1)], 0<=i<n,\n    alpha_star=p82.                                      (II.1)\n\nIts sum is 1-p82 because sum_(i=0)^(n-1)(i+1)=n(n+1)/2.\nIts chronological cumulative is\n\n    w_i=p82+(1-p82)(i+1)(i+2)/[n(n+1)].\n\nThis is not a distribution on a handful of selected terminals. Every\ngenuine prefix sample participates; every physical leaf and checkpoint\nis still independent. In particular alpha_i<2/n.\n\nChoose kappa>0 and tau>=0, and set\n\n    K=kappa*(I-e e^T/n),\n    d=tau*(e_z-e_0),\n    A=(d e^T-e d^T)/n.                               (II.2)\n\nThen A^T=-A and Ae=d. This is a literal dense chronology correction,\nnot an unidentified circulation or an uncharged antisymmetric term.\nThe imbalance at the original first and last queried samples changes\nthe common-gradient derivative and the optimum coefficients.\n\nFor a completely scalar specification, put\n\n    Cclock_i=1+h_i*(n-1-i),\n    dtilde_0=a*tau,\n    dtilde_i=h_i*tau for 0<i<z,\n    dtilde_z=tau,\n    tbar=(sum_i t_i)/n.\n\nAll original finite coefficients come from the literal formula\n\n    Nmat_ij=kappa*(1_(i=j)+h_i*1_(j>i)-Cclock_i/n)\n            +gamma Rvec_i v_j+(dtilde_i-Cclock_i*d_j)/n,\n    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij for i!=j<n.                 (II.3)\n\nThe optimum coefficients and terminal coefficients are\n\n    mu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i+dtilde_i>0,\n    nu_i=kappa*(t_i-tbar)+gamma U82 v_i-d_i*tbar\n                                      +tau*Ppos_z/n-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0, lambda_(i,i)=0.                   (II.4)\n\nThey follow from (4.2), using d^T t=tau*Ppos_z. This specifies ALL\nordered entries, including both optimum orientations. The nu entries\nremain signed matching expressions; their signs are not assumed.\nThe value-flow calculation of Section 4 applies without modification.\n\nWrite ybar=(sum_i y_i)/n. Completing ALL detail squares in (4.3) gives\nthe single global identity\n\n    M_II(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n      +kappa*sum_(i<n)||y_i-ybar+(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0||^2\n      +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2\n      +(q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa))*||x_0||^2.             (II.5)\n\nSince sum d_i=0 and sum d_i^2=2tau^2, expanding the detail squares\nrecovers exactly kappa sum||y_i-ybar||^2+<x_0,d^T y> and the displayed\ncharge. There is no sum of independently minimized local Schur budgets.\nThe common-gradient restriction, in particular, is\n\n    R_II,common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-tau*Ppos_z*<x_0,G>\n            +[kappa sum_i(t_i-tbar)^2+eta U82^2]||G||^2.  (II.6)\n\nIt has the nonzero derivative -tau*Ppos_z*x_0 at G=0. Discarding that\nderivative would discard precisely the payment this construction makes.\n\nFull PSD of (II.5) is EQUIVALENT to\n\n    q82>=p82^2+tau^2/(2kappa).                            (II.7)\n\nSufficiency follows from the explicit squares. For necessity take any\nnonzero scalar x_0, set y_i-ybar=-(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0, and select the\ncommon value ybar so that v^T y=(p82+s82)x_0. This is possible because\nsum v_i=p82+s82>0. Finally set g_n=-s82*x_0+v^T y. These are arbitrary\nformal coordinates under the invertible map (3.1). They annihilate every\nother square. A negative last coefficient would therefore give an\nexplicit negative original-space direction. No interpolability is claimed.\nMore explicitly, take x_0=1,\ny_i=1+(v^T d)/(2kappa(p82+s82))-d_i/(2kappa), g_n=p82,\nand use (3.1a) for each queried g_i. The value of the whole polynomial is\nthen q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa).\n\n### 6.1 Two literal original signs exceed the full charge budget\n\nLet j=z-2 be the preceding queried leaf. The binomial expansion gives\n\n    (6/5)^8>1+8/5+28/25+56/125=521/125>4.\n\nRaising to the fourth power yields (6/5)^32>256. Therefore EVERY\nm>=1536 satisfies D>256n>136n, and hence\n\n    qtar82=17/(16D)<1/(128n).                          (7.3)\n\nThe factor domains are not vacuous. Moreover\n\n    17*Aword^2-16*(2Aword-1)\n                               =16*(Aword-1)^2+Aword^2>0\n\nproves pbase82^2<qtar82. not the Zhang--Jiang bridge function psi.", "omega^- and omega^+": "Positive scalar weights for each of the two contrasts of every actual physical cell.", "E_z and E_r": "The two literal first-query Nmat entries to z and r in (I.7); original multipliers are their negatives.", "Bpair": "v_r+(n-1)v_z, strictly positive in Law I.", "Bnew": "Balanced skew matrix characterizing all coefficient re-selections of the same Law I factor and terminal pool.", "Cclock_i": "Scalar 1+h_i(n-1-i) in the expanded Law II coefficient formula.", "dtilde_i": "Coordinate of T82^T d, explicitly a*tau at0, h_i*tau at interior indices, tau atz.", "tbar and ybar": "Arithmetic means of all original prefix clock scalars and y vectors, respectively.", "Aword": "1+sum h; A_j=1+sum H_j uses the same enclosing-m parameters.", "vword": "Mass-formula coefficient a/(c-2), strictly between0 and1.", "V": "Actual terminal square center -s82*x_0+v^T y.", "M(q82) and R(q82)": "Full original signed row polynomial and its exact full prefix remainder after the actual terminal square."}}
{"id": "a1febca669a733e9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T19:00:52.171226+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max82 Law I, JOINT UNVERIFIED: an explicit complete original physical-tree square factor with uniform actual-terminal pooling cannot be matched by ANY nonnegative original multipliers preserving that exact factor/pool. Literal symmetric matching forces lambda_(0,z)<0 or lambda_(0,r)<0; all balanced-skew re-selections preserve a strictly positive first-row off-diagonal Nmat sum. All independent child contrasts and queried leaves are retained; q inflation and arbitrary positive contrast reweighting do not repair this law.", "evidence": "# Full original-space squares with jointly selected terminal pooling\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, worker max, invocation82.\nOriginal minimax Goal OPEN. No new fact or verifier verdict.\n\nTASK.md and master_guidance5fee1ea5f63fd8fc authorize one invocation,\nat most two substantive analytical laws, and zero mathematical programs,\nnumerical evaluations, recurrence/trajectory replays, solvers, formal calls,\nfact_submit, installations, consults, or subagents. Those restrictions were\nretained. All the new derivations below are one joint unverified attempt;\nnone is being adopted as an accepted intermediate fact.\n\n## 1. Outcome and precise scope\n\nWe first specify positive square factors on the ENTIRE original space,\nthen match them to original interpolation rows. The factorization is not\nan unknown PSD block, an arbitrary Cholesky factor, or a temporal quotient.\nEvery queried gradient, both child mean directions and their physical\ncheckpoint, every leaf, the common mode, the initial vector, and the\nunqueried terminal are retained.\n\nLaw I uses a complete physical-tree multiresolution factor and a terminal\ndistribution positive at every prefix sample and the optimum. Its literal\ncoefficient matching has a negative original multiplier: either the first\nquery to the last queried leaf, or the first query to the root checkpoint.\nThe failure holds for arbitrary positive weights on ALL the separate\ncontrasts, not just the two-parameter weight law displayed below. A stronger\nfirst-row invariant excludes every balanced-skew re-selection producing\nthis SAME factor and terminal distribution. Increasing q82 does not repair it.\n\nLaw II changes the matching by an explicit chronology-dependent skew term,\nchanges the terminal distribution to increasing weights on actual query\ntimes, and pays the resulting NONZERO initial/common derivative. Its full\nsquare identity contains the exact global charge tau^2/(2kappa). The two\nliteral original signs lambda_(0,z)>=0 and lambda_(z,z-2)>=0 already force\nq82>1/(128n). For EVERY assigned m>=1536, qtar82=17/(16D)<1/(128n).\nThus no member of this second literal law has both full PSD and all\nnonnegative multipliers at the target. An additional balanced-skew\nre-selection of Law II is outside this second exclusion.\n\nThe two results exclude two specified square/matching architectures.\nThey do not exclude the full dual cone, prove a lower bound on W, produce\nan interpolable primal witness, or resolve the original minimax question.\nThey do not invoke the previous contact-routed or LCA receiver arrays.\n\n## 2. Accepted inputs and the unchanged problem\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite dimension,\nconvex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimizer x_star,\nand x_0!=x_star, with x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). L>0 is fixed.\nThus W=2R. The original question is whether\n\n    lim_(n->infinity) B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h)=1.\n\nThe benchmark is the original ALL-integer-split recurrence\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation\norientation, the independent-vector certificate interpretation, and the\nexact incoming-terminal-free completion. It removes NO prefix row.\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-word benchmark comparison\nB_n/D<47/50 and its CONDITIONAL implication from W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D).\nThat full-class upper hypothesis remains unproved. Its elementary mass\nformula is also available. No other mathematical fact is a premise here.\n\nFix an integer m>=1536. Throughout this report use the SAME enclosing m:\n\n    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=ell*(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    qtar82=17/(16D), z=n-1, r=2^(m-1)-1, b=1+a.\n\nHere z is the last QUERIED leaf, r is the whole-word checkpoint, and n\nis the ACTUAL terminal. All hierarchy symbols in this report are local.\nThe physical step at i is h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2 is the exponent\nof 2 dividing a positive integer. Put Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and\nt_i=1+Ppos_i for 0<=i<n. The column t is an auxiliary clock, not time\nor a gradient. Write e for the all-ones column of length n and e_i for\nits coordinate unit columns. Superscript T denotes transpose.\nThe symbol 1_E is the indicator of a stated condition E, and 1_J is the\ncoordinate indicator column of an index set J. I denotes the n-by-n\nidentity matrix. A quadratic y^T K y for vector entries means\nsum_(i,j) K_ij <y_i,y_j>.\n\nThe following elementary bounds will suffice:\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2,\n    4/3<b<3/2, n>=15, S>n.\n\nIndeed sqrt(2)>7/5, and sqrt(2)<17/12 yields\nell<29/12; multiplying by 1+1/6144 still gives c<5/2.\nThen a>(7/8)/(5/2)=7/20>1/3; and a<1/ell<1/2.\nEvery step exceeds one. The displayed n bound follows from m>=1536.\n\n## 3. Full original coordinates; no projection or quotient\n\nNormalize L=1 and the optimum jet to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain\nARBITRARY independent vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n in a real Euclidean space.\nEvery position always means the polynomial\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nFor distinct original labels define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nActual legal jets obey Q_ij<=0. A certificate at level q82 requires\nlambda_ij>=0, finite divergence out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), and PSD of\n\n    M(q82)=q82||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n\non ALL these independent vectors after the value cancellation.\n\nFor each queried i<n define y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nSet y_star=delta_star=0. Define the lower triangular matrix T82 by\n\n    T82_ii=1, T82_it=h_t for t<i, T82_it=0 for t>i.\n\nWrite g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1))^T and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1))^T; in matrix\nproducts h also denotes its column. As vector-column identities,\n\n    y=e*x_0-T82g,  g=T82^(-1)(e*x_0-y),  T82e=t.             (3.1)\n\nT82 has diagonal one, so (x_0,y_0,...,y_(n-1),g_n) and the original\nvariables are in invertible correspondence. In particular y is an\narbitrary n-tuple once x_0 is fixed. No equal-gradient relation is imposed.\n\nWritten finite products define\n\n    pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t),\n    k_i=h_i*product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t), 0<=i<n.\n\nIn particular the inverse in (3.1) has the literal vector formula\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n             +sum_(j<i)h_j*product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j.  (3.1a)\n\nAn empty product is one. Thus even the coordinate reconstruction is\ngiven by finite scalar coefficients, not an unspecified inverse solve.\n\nExpanding x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i*y_i inductively, without evaluating a\nrecurrence on a machine, gives\n\n    x_n=pi_n*x_0+sum_(i<n)k_i*y_i,\n    sum_i k_i=1-pi_n, T82^T k=h.                       (3.2)\n\nFor the second identity, the coefficient sum on a suffix equals one\nminus the suffix product: expand each h_i=1-(1-h_i) and telescope.\nThus k_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i, proving the last identity.\n\nThere are n physical factors 1-h_i=-a*c^v2(i+1). Also\nsum_(i<n)v2(i+1)=sum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=n-m.\nSince n is odd, this proves the EXACT full-word product\n\n    pi_n=-s82,  s82=beta^n/c^m>0.                        (3.3)\n\nOnly an identity for the literal physical word is asserted; s82 is not\nbeing substituted for a worst-case contraction factor.\n\nChoose a parameter p82 with s82<p82<1, and a probability distribution\nalpha_i>=0 on queried indices with sum_i alpha_i=1-p82. The terminal\nweight at the optimum will be alpha_star=p82. Define\n\n    v=k-alpha,\n    w_i=p82+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j,\n    Rvec_i=h_i*w_i-alpha_i,\n    U82=sum_i Rvec_i=t^T v,\n    gamma=2*p82/(p82+s82), eta=(p82-s82)/(p82+s82).                  (3.4)\n\nThen\n\n    e^T v=p82+s82, T82^T v=Rvec, gamma=1+eta,\n    Rvec_i=h_i*(p82+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0.\n\nThese identities follow from (3.2) and sum alpha=1-p82. In particular U82>0.\nThe exact terminal center in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is\n\n    V=x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i=-s82*x_0+v^T y.            (3.5)\n\nEvery queried target and the optimum will have positive terminal weight\nin both laws. Neither law forces lambda_(n,n-1)=2.\n\n## 4. Signed coefficient matching and its exact full identity\n\nThis section gives a literal algebraic parameterization of the two laws,\nincluding the entries that FAIL nonnegativity. It is not a claim that a\npositive square factor necessarily has nonnegative row coefficients.\n\nLet K be a specified symmetric matrix with Ke=0. In the two laws K is\ngiven by explicit positive squares, never by an unknown PSD condition.\nLet A be a specified skew matrix, A^T=-A, and put d=Ae. Thus e^T d=0.\nDefine the fully specified matrices\n\n    Pmat=K+gamma*v*v^T,\n    Nmat=T82^T(Pmat+A).\n\nTo make clear that this is a finite coefficient formula, each entry is\n\n    Nmat_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*Rvec_i*v_j.   (4.1)\n\nThe FULL original signed row array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i, i<n,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0, j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij, i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Nmat e)_i=2*p82*Rvec_i+(T82^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(Nmat^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(K t)_i+gamma*U82*v_i-(A t)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                      (4.2)\n\nThis lists every entry; no additional rows, diagonal multipliers, or\ncompletion freedom are presumed. Finite sums are definitions, not\nnumerical or recurrence executions.\n\nAll value-flow cancellations hold even before sign checking. At a prefix\nnode the total outgoing coefficient is Nmat_ii, and total incoming\nPREFIX coefficient is Nmat_ii-2alpha_i. Thus prefix divergence is\n2alpha_i. The incoming terminal edge has coefficient 2alpha_i, giving\nzero divergence at that original queried node. Terminal divergence is\n2*p82+2sum alpha_i=2. The optimum divergence is -2. All function values\ncancel in M(q82), without imposing any relation among them.\n\nDirect expansion yields Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i> for prefix\nindices, including star. Substituting (4.2), its surviving quadratic is\n-g^T Nmat y. Incoming-optimum rows have g_star=0; their value contribution\nis nevertheless present in the preceding flow calculation. From (3.1),\n\n    -g^T Nmat y\n       =-<x_0,e^T(Pmat+A)y>+y^T Pmat y,\n    e^T Pmat=2*p82*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290, or direct expansion of the displayed outgoing\nterminal rows, now gives the ENTIRE original identity\n\n    M(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2+R(q82),\n    R(q82)=y^T K y+eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2\n                         +(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.  (4.3)\n\nFor direct checking, before completing the common square the prefix form is\n\n    (q82-s82^2)||x_0||^2+2(s82-p82)<x_0,v^T y>\n                              +eta||v^T y||^2+y^TKy+<x_0,d^Ty>.\n\nThe equality eta(p82+s82)=p82-s82 completes it to (4.3). This is an identity\non arbitrary original x_0,g_0,...,g_n, not merely a legal trajectory.\n\nThe common-gradient derivative is also explicit. Set every queried\ngradient to the same arbitrary vector G. Then y_i=x_0-t_i G and\nv^Ty=(p82+s82)x_0-U82 G. Therefore the FULL prefix restriction is\n\n    R_common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-<x_0,(d^T t)G>\n                                      +(t^TKt+eta U82^2)||G||^2. (4.4)\n\nIn particular an imbalance d is not free: it changes the derivative in\nG at G=0 by -(d^T t)x_0. Both optimum orientations are in (4.2), and\ntheir true contribution has not been replaced by a contact assumption.\n\n## 5. Law I: complete physical-tree squares, with separate boundaries\n\nUse uniform queried terminal weights\n\n    alpha_i=u=(1-p82)/n, alpha_star=p82.                    (I.1)\n\nFor each actual dyadic cell\n\n    I_(j,bidx)={bidx*2^j,...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2},\n    2<=j<=m, 0<=bidx<2^(m-j),\n\nlet Lcell and Rcell be its genuine left and right queried child sets,\neach of size l_j=2^(j-1)-1, and let rootcell be its actual checkpoint.\nDefine two coefficient columns of length n, zero outside the cell:\n\n    psi^-=(1_Lcell-1_Rcell)/sqrt(2l_j),\n    psi^+=(1_Lcell+1_Rcell-2l_j e_rootcell)\n                                      /sqrt(2l_j(2l_j+1)).  (I.2)\n\nThe first contrast retains the two child means independently. The second\nretains the checkpoint relative to both children. Each has sum zero and\nnorm one, and their inner product is zero. A descendant contrast has sum\nzero inside a child on which an ancestor contrast is constant. Thus all\nthese columns are mutually orthonormal. Inducting on a cell, its two\nchildren contribute 2(l_j-1) details, the displayed columns contribute\ntwo, and its common column contributes one, totaling 2l_j+1 coordinates.\nThe singleton H_1 cells need just their common column. Consequently\nthere are n-1 columns in (I.2), and together with e/sqrt(n) they form\nan orthonormal basis of the ENTIRE original queried-coordinate space.\nExplicitly, for a cell beginning at o=bidx*2^j, Lcell is\n{o,...,o+2^(j-1)-2}, rootcell=o+2^(j-1)-1, and Rcell is\n{o+2^(j-1),...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2}.\n\nThe literal two-parameter weight law is, for kappa>0 and theta>0,\n\n    omega^-_(j,bidx)=kappa*c^(-j),\n    omega^+_(j,bidx)=theta*kappa*c^(-j),\n    K=sum_cells[omega^- psi^- (psi^-)^T\n                                      +omega^+ psi^+ (psi^+)^T], A=0. (I.3)\n\nEvery square coefficient is explicitly positive at every depth. The\nstronger exclusion below permits independently arbitrary positive omega\nat every column, so it covers any retuning within this complete basis.\nThat extension does not insert an unknown PSD block.\n\nEquations (4.1)--(4.2) give EVERY original row coefficient. In this law\nmu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i>0; terminal weights are strictly positive. The incoming\noptimum coefficients are literally\n\n    nu_i=sum_cells,sign omega*psi_i*(sum_j psi_j t_j)\n                                                +gamma U82 v_i-2u.  (I.4)\n\nThey are signed matching coefficients, not silently assumed nonnegative.\nAn original finite coefficient will necessarily be negative regardless\nof whether these nu_i have acceptable signs.\n\nFor every q82>=p82^2 the complete factor is\n\n    M_I(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_cells,sign omega||sum_i psi_i y_i||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2+(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2.      (I.5)\n\nThis supplies all details and the common mode, since e^Tv=p82+s82>0 and\neta>0. With q82>p82^2 it is positive definite in all the original variables;\nat q82=p82^2 it has the stated explicit nonnegative factorization. No child\nPSD premise remains to prove. The only failed certificate requirement\nwill be the signs of the matched interpolation multipliers.\n\n### 5.1 An exact first forced sign, with the physical root retained\n\nLet omega^- and omega^+ now denote the two weights at the WHOLE word.\nOnly those two contrasts can contain both endpoints 0,z, or endpoint 0\nand root r. Since the child sizes are (n-1)/2,\n\n    K_0z=(-omega^-+omega^+/n)/(n-1),\n    K_0r=-omega^+/n.                                  (I.6)\n\nKe=0 implies (T82^T K)_0j=(1-b)K_0j=-a K_0j.\nHere Rvec_0=b*p82+a*u>0. The exact two signed entries are therefore\n\n    E_z=Nmat_0z=a*(omega^--omega^+/n)/(n-1)\n                                                   +gamma Rvec_0 v_z,\n    E_r=Nmat_0r=a*omega^+/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_r.           (I.7)\n\nThe last queried leaf has k_z=b, so v_z=b-u>1. The root has a suffix\nequal to the SAME-parameter H_(m-1). Write l=2^(m-1)-1. The product\ncalculation in (3.3), applied with that unchanged a,beta,c, gives\n\n    k_r=-(a+c^(-(m-1)))*beta^l,\n    v_r=k_r-u>-(a+1)-1/n>-2.                         (I.8)\n\nConsequently Bpair=v_r+(n-1)v_z>n-3>0, and (I.7) gives\n\n    E_r+(n-1)E_z=a*omega^-+gamma Rvec_0 Bpair>0.       (I.9)\n\nThis identifies a negative ORIGINAL multiplier for every parameter choice:\nif E_z>0, lambda_(0,z)=-E_z<0; if E_z<=0, (I.9) forces\nlambda_(0,r)=-E_r<0. All denominators and prefactors used here are positive.\nThe conclusion holds for every m>=1536, every s82<p82<1, every q82>=p82^2,\nevery kappa,theta>0, and even independently arbitrary positive detail\nweights. Descendant weights cannot alter (I.6). Raising q82 cannot alter\nthe sign because it changes only the last x_0 square in (I.5).\n\nThe excluded rows are actual remote queried-prefix rows. They are not\nincoming-terminal rows, omitted leaves, or fictitious child interfaces.\n\n### 5.2 All coefficient re-selections of this same factor also fail\n\nThere is a useful stronger scope statement. Keep the exact factor (I.5)\nand exact terminal distribution (I.1), but allow any other original prefix\nrow coefficients matching that polynomial. If their row matrix is Nnew,\nput Bnew=T82^(-T)Nnew-Pmat. Comparing y-y and x_0-y coefficients in (4.3)\nforces Bnew to be skew and e^T Bnew=0, equivalently Bnew e=0. Conversely\nevery such balanced-skew term leaves the same polynomial unchanged.\nThus this argument describes all coefficient re-selections of this fixed\nfactor, rather than assuming the symmetric matching is unique.\n\nFor any balanced-skew Bnew, the first diagonal change is\n\n    (T82^T Bnew)_00=(1-b)Bnew_00+b(e^T Bnew)_0=0,\n\nand the first row-sum change is zero. Hence the sum of its first-row\noff-diagonal N entries is invariant and equals\n\n    mu_0-Nmat_00\n       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+s82-v_0)\n       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+u-s82/a).                (I.10)\n\nHere k_0=b*s82/a, since pi_n=-s82=(1-b)*product_(1<=t<n)(1-h_t).\nThe last factor is strictly positive throughout the parameter domain.\nIndeed p82+(1-p82)/n is increasing in p82; at p82=s82,\n\n    n*(p82+u-s82/a)=1-s82*(n*(1/a-1)+1)>0.\n\nFor the strict last bound, a>1/3 gives n*(1/a-1)+1<2n+1<2^(m+1),\nand s82<c^(-m) gives its product with s82 less than 2*(5/6)^m<1.\nThe inequality (6/5)^4=1296/625>2 proves this for every m>=4.\nAlso K_00>0, because the complete basis has a nonzero contrast at 0\nand every detail weight is positive. Thus (I.10)>0.\n\nIf ALL original lambda_(0,j) were nonnegative, then all off-diagonal\nNnew_0j would be nonpositive and their sum could not be positive.\nThis contradiction excludes EVERY nonnegative coefficient matching of\nthis particular full square factor and its fixed terminal distribution.\nIt does not exclude a changed square factor or a changed terminal pool.\n\n## 6. Law II: chronological terminal pool and a paid skew correction\n\nChoose the positive increasing distribution on ACTUAL prefix indices\n\n    alpha_i=2(i+1)(1-p82)/[n(n+1)], 0<=i<n,\n    alpha_star=p82.                                      (II.1)\n\nIts sum is 1-p82 because sum_(i=0)^(n-1)(i+1)=n(n+1)/2.\nIts chronological cumulative is\n\n    w_i=p82+(1-p82)(i+1)(i+2)/[n(n+1)].\n\nThis is not a distribution on a handful of selected terminals. Every\ngenuine prefix sample participates; every physical leaf and checkpoint\nis still independent. In particular alpha_i<2/n.\n\nChoose kappa>0 and tau>=0, and set\n\n    K=kappa*(I-e e^T/n),\n    d=tau*(e_z-e_0),\n    A=(d e^T-e d^T)/n.                               (II.2)\n\nThen A^T=-A and Ae=d. This is a literal dense chronology correction,\nnot an unidentified circulation or an uncharged antisymmetric term.\nThe imbalance at the original first and last queried samples changes\nthe common-gradient derivative and the optimum coefficients.\n\nFor a completely scalar specification, put\n\n    Cclock_i=1+h_i*(n-1-i),\n    dtilde_0=a*tau,\n    dtilde_i=h_i*tau for 0<i<z,\n    dtilde_z=tau,\n    tbar=(sum_i t_i)/n.\n\nAll original finite coefficients come from the literal formula\n\n    Nmat_ij=kappa*(1_(i=j)+h_i*1_(j>i)-Cclock_i/n)\n            +gamma Rvec_i v_j+(dtilde_i-Cclock_i*d_j)/n,\n    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij for i!=j<n.                 (II.3)\n\nThe optimum coefficients and terminal coefficients are\n\n    mu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i+dtilde_i>0,\n    nu_i=kappa*(t_i-tbar)+gamma U82 v_i-d_i*tbar\n                                      +tau*Ppos_z/n-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0, lambda_(i,i)=0.                   (II.4)\n\nThey follow from (4.2), using d^T t=tau*Ppos_z. This specifies ALL\nordered entries, including both optimum orientations. The nu entries\nremain signed matching expressions; their signs are not assumed.\nThe value-flow calculation of Section 4 applies without modification.\n\nWrite ybar=(sum_i y_i)/n. Completing ALL detail squares in (4.3) gives\nthe single global identity\n\n    M_II(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n      +kappa*sum_(i<n)||y_i-ybar+(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0||^2\n      +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2\n      +(q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa))*||x_0||^2. Accepted inputs and the unchanged problem\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite dimension,\nconvex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimizer x_star,\nand x_0!=x_star, with x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). L>0 is fixed.\nThus W=2R. The original question is whether\n\n    lim_(n->infinity) B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h)=1.\n\nThe benchmark is the original ALL-integer-split recurrence\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation\norientation, the independent-vector certificate interpretation, and the\nexact incoming-terminal-free completion. It removes NO prefix row.\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-word benchmark comparison\nB_n/D<47/50 and its CONDITIONAL implication from W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D).\nThat full-class upper hypothesis remains unproved. Its elementary mass\nformula is also available. No other mathematical fact is a premise here.\n\nFix an integer m>=1536. Throughout this report use the SAME enclosing m:\n\n    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=ell*(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    qtar82=17/(16D), z=n-1, r=2^(m-1)-1, b=1+a.\n\nHere z is the last QUERIED leaf, r is the whole-word checkpoint, and n\nis the ACTUAL terminal. Superscript T denotes transpose.\nThe symbol 1_E is the indicator of a stated condition E, and 1_J is the\ncoordinate indicator column of an index set J. I denotes the n-by-n\nidentity matrix. It is not a claim that a\npositive square factor necessarily has nonnegative row coefficients.\n\nLet K be a specified symmetric matrix with Ke=0. Thus e^T d=0.\nDefine the fully specified matrices\n\n    Pmat=K+gamma*v*v^T,\n    Nmat=T82^T(Pmat+A).\n\nTo make clear that this is a finite coefficient formula, each entry is\n\n    Nmat_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*Rvec_i*v_j.   (4.1)\n\nThe FULL original signed row array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i, i<n,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0, j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij, i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Nmat e)_i=2*p82*Rvec_i+(T82^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(Nmat^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(K t)_i+gamma*U82*v_i-(A t)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                      (4.2)\n\nThis lists every entry; no additional rows, diagonal multipliers, or\ncompletion freedom are presumed. Finite sums are definitions, not\nnumerical or recurrence executions.\n\nAll value-flow cancellations hold even before sign checking. This is an identity\non arbitrary original x_0,g_0,...,g_n, not merely a legal trajectory.\n\nThe common-gradient derivative is also explicit. Set every queried\ngradient to the same arbitrary vector G. Then y_i=x_0-t_i G and\nv^Ty=(p82+s82)x_0-U82 G. Therefore the FULL prefix restriction is\n\n    R_common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-<x_0,(d^T t)G>\n                                      +(t^TKt+eta U82^2)||G||^2. (4.4)\n\nIn particular an imbalance d is not free: it changes the derivative in\nG at G=0 by -(d^T t)x_0. Both optimum orientations are in (4.2), and\ntheir true contribution has not been replaced by a contact assumption.\n\n## 5. Law I: complete physical-tree squares, with separate boundaries\n\nUse uniform queried terminal weights\n\n    alpha_i=u=(1-p82)/n, alpha_star=p82.                    (I.1)\n\nFor each actual dyadic cell\n\n    I_(j,bidx)={bidx*2^j,...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2},\n    2<=j<=m, 0<=bidx<2^(m-j),\n\nlet Lcell and Rcell be its genuine left and right queried child sets,\neach of size l_j=2^(j-1)-1, and let rootcell be its actual checkpoint.\nDefine two coefficient columns of length n, zero outside the cell:\n\n    psi^-=(1_Lcell-1_Rcell)/sqrt(2l_j),\n    psi^+=(1_Lcell+1_Rcell-2l_j e_rootcell)\n                                      /sqrt(2l_j(2l_j+1)).  (I.2)\n\nThe first contrast retains the two child means independently. The second\nretains the checkpoint relative to both children. Each has sum zero and\nnorm one, and their inner product is zero. A descendant contrast has sum\nzero inside a child on which an ancestor contrast is constant. Thus all\nthese columns are mutually orthonormal. Inducting on a cell, its two\nchildren contribute 2(l_j-1) details, the displayed columns contribute\ntwo, and its common column contributes one, totaling 2l_j+1 coordinates.\nThe singleton H_1 cells need just their common column. Consequently\nthere are n-1 columns in (I.2), and together with e/sqrt(n) they form\nan orthonormal basis of the ENTIRE original queried-coordinate space.\nExplicitly, for a cell beginning at o=bidx*2^j, Lcell is\n{o,...,o+2^(j-1)-2}, rootcell=o+2^(j-1)-1, and Rcell is\n{o+2^(j-1),...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2}.\n\nThe literal two-parameter weight law is, for kappa>0 and theta>0,\n\n    omega^-_(j,bidx)=kappa*c^(-j),\n    omega^+_(j,bidx)=theta*kappa*c^(-j),\n    K=sum_cells[omega^- psi^- (psi^-)^T\n                                      +omega^+ psi^+ (psi^+)^T], A=0. (I.3)\n\nEvery square coefficient is explicitly positive at every depth. The\nstronger exclusion below permits independently arbitrary positive omega\nat every column, so it covers any retuning within this complete basis.\nThat extension does not insert an unknown PSD block.\n\nEquations (4.1)--(4.2) give EVERY original row coefficient. In this law\nmu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i>0; terminal weights are strictly positive. The incoming\noptimum coefficients are literally\n\n    nu_i=sum_cells,sign omega*psi_i*(sum_j psi_j t_j)\n                                                +gamma U82 v_i-2u.  (I.4)\n\nThey are signed matching coefficients, not silently assumed nonnegative.\nAn original finite coefficient will necessarily be negative regardless\nof whether these nu_i have acceptable signs.\n\nFor every q82>=p82^2 the complete factor is\n\n    M_I(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_cells,sign omega||sum_i psi_i y_i||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2+(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2.      (I.5)\n\nThis supplies all details and the common mode, since e^Tv=p82+s82>0 and\neta>0. With q82>p82^2 it is positive definite in all the original variables;\nat q82=p82^2 it has the stated explicit nonnegative factorization. No child\nPSD premise remains to prove. The only failed certificate requirement\nwill be the signs of the matched interpolation multipliers.\n\n### 5.1 An exact first forced sign, with the physical root retained\n\nLet omega^- and omega^+ now denote the two weights at the WHOLE word.\nOnly those two contrasts can contain both endpoints 0,z, or endpoint 0\nand root r. Since the child sizes are (n-1)/2,\n\n    K_0z=(-omega^-+omega^+/n)/(n-1),\n    K_0r=-omega^+/n.                                  (I.6)\n\nKe=0 implies (T82^T K)_0j=(1-b)K_0j=-a K_0j.\nHere Rvec_0=b*p82+a*u>0. The exact two signed entries are therefore\n\n    E_z=Nmat_0z=a*(omega^--omega^+/n)/(n-1)\n                                                   +gamma Rvec_0 v_z,\n    E_r=Nmat_0r=a*omega^+/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_r.           (I.7)\n\nThe last queried leaf has k_z=b, so v_z=b-u>1. The root has a suffix\nequal to the SAME-parameter H_(m-1). Write l=2^(m-1)-1. The product\ncalculation in (3.3), applied with that unchanged a,beta,c, gives\n\n    k_r=-(a+c^(-(m-1)))*beta^l,\n    v_r=k_r-u>-(a+1)-1/n>-2.                         (I.8)\n\nConsequently Bpair=v_r+(n-1)v_z>n-3>0, and (I.7) gives\n\n    E_r+(n-1)E_z=a*omega^-+gamma Rvec_0 Bpair>0. All denominators and prefactors used here are positive.\nThe conclusion holds for every m>=1536, every s82<p82<1, every q82>=p82^2,\nevery kappa,theta>0, and even independently arbitrary positive detail\nweights. Descendant weights cannot alter (I.6). Raising q82 cannot alter\nthe sign because it changes only the last x_0 square in (I.5).\n\nThe excluded rows are actual remote queried-prefix rows. They are not\nincoming-terminal rows, omitted leaves, or fictitious child interfaces.\n\n### 5.2 All coefficient re-selections of this same factor also fail\n\nThere is a useful stronger scope statement. Law II: chronological terminal pool and a paid skew correction\n\nChoose the positive increasing distribution on ACTUAL prefix indices\n\n    alpha_i=2(i+1)(1-p82)/[n(n+1)], 0<=i<n,\n    alpha_star=p82. alpha_star=p82.", "v": "Coefficient column k-alpha, whose entry sum is p82+s82.", "w_i": "Chronological cumulative p82+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j.", "Rvec_i": "Positive h_i*w_i-alpha_i, the i-th coordinate of T82^T v.", "U82": "Positive sum_i Rvec_i=t^T v.", "t_i": "Physical auxiliary clock 1+Ppos_i; Law II uses tau(e_z-e_0).", "Pmat": "Symmetric matching matrix K+gamma v v^T, gamma=2p82/(p82+s82).", "Nmat": "Full row matching matrix T82^T(Pmat+A).", "mu_i": "Signed original outgoing-optimum multiplier (Nmat e)_i;
