{"id": "752fbdb3285cf0ea", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T19:10:49.014809+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4r80 JOINT UNVERIFIED: the ancestor-pair clamping bridge is closed by an explicit THREE-CASE xi=0 coefficient rule for every actual ancestor--descendant checkpoint pair on same-enclosing-m H_(m,1/4), all m>=1536, BOTH terminal charges. Full original row weights, all-depth multiplier signs, paid clamping comparison and entire equal-sector PSD square are supplied. The old (10.5) now holds at draft scope. Restricted E optimum=1/D_m and Q payoff<=1/(D_m-d0^2); full W and original minimax remain OPEN. No fact_submit/no new fact_id.", "evidence": "# Ancestor clamping bridge: explicit coefficients and a complete joint proof draft\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED. Project gd-exact-profile-20260926, worker max4,\ninvocation80, guidance ecacb7cb9b03f152, plan750b1079379f0b82.\nNo verifier submission was authorized or made. There is no new fact_id.\n\nThe first mechanism closes the requested ancestor-pair bridge at the\nlevel of the self-contained proof draft below. In particular, it pays\nthe nonnegative-multiplier clamping cost; it does not replace that cost\nby positivity of an unconstrained determinant. The coefficient rule is\nexplicit and has three cases. All depths, actual exterior masses, and\nboth terminal charges are retained.\n\nThe second mechanism, a paid qr-cycle calculation, was started while\nthe first mechanism remained unresolved. Its complete paid formulas\nare recorded in Section12. The first mechanism was subsequently closed\nby the boundary comparison in Sections7--9. No third mechanism or\nfour-sector exploration was then undertaken: TASK.md expressly forbids\na third mechanism after a qr-cycle attempt. A lift to arbitrary queried\ngradients remains open. Nothing here establishes the full-class bound\nrequired by accepted931b9653ce0f7201 or resolves the minimax target.\n\nAll mathematical work was textual. Mathematical/numerical programs,\ntrajectory or recurrence replays, solvers, formal calls, fact_submit,\nsubagents, installations, consultations, and automatic renewal: ZERO.\n\n## 1. Exact scope, normalization, and physical masses\n\nFix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. Throughout this entire report set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon_h=1/(4m), beta_h=1-epsilon_h,\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon_h), a=beta_h/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l),\n    S_l=sum H_l, X_l=1+S_l, n=2^m-1,\n    Z=D_m=1+2S_m, d0=1+a.\n\nEvery child retains these same enclosing-m parameters. H_l is a\nphysical word. There is no reset of m, a, c, or the benchmark.\nFor each actual step index i, its dyadic level is l_i=nu_2(i+1), and\nits complete rooted subtree occupies\n\n    I_i=[i-(2^(l_i)-1), i+(2^(l_i)-1)].\n\nThese identities follow by induction on the literal concatenation:\nthe new middle has level l, and translation of a child by 2^l\npreserves all its lower valuations. Its step is 1+a*c^(l_i).\n\nChoose any actual indices 0<=j<k<=n-2 such that j is an ancestor\nof k. Thus k lies in the right child of I_j. Set l=l_j>=1,\nX=X_l, d=a*c^l, and e=a*c^(l_k). Write the WHOLE original word as\n\n    (block0,1+d,block1,1+e,block2),\n    P=sum block0, M=sum block1, T=sum block2,\n    u=P+1, v=M+1, A=u+d, B=v+e, U=u+v+d.\n\nLet z_loc be one plus the mass after k within j's right child and\nO the entire mass after I_j. The literal physical relations are\n\n    u>=X, v+e+z_loc=X, O>=0, T+1=O+z_loc.             (1.1)\n\nThe entire left child of k lies in the middle block, and its entire\nright child lies in z_loc. Consequently, with tau=5/12,\n\n    0<d<tau*X, 0<e<tau*min(v,z_loc).                 (1.2)\n\nProofs of the uniform constants used here are in Section4.\n\nNormalize a static convex differentiable objective to smoothness1,\nminimum0, and an arbitrary one of its OWN minimizers x_star=0.\nConsider its actual GD transcript whose queried gradients satisfy\n\n    g_i=p, 0<=i<=j;\n    g_i=q, j+1<=i<=k;\n    g_i=r, k+1<=i<n.                                (1.3)\n\nThe real vectors p,q,r are arbitrary, may coincide, and may live in\nany finite dimension. Family E requires g_n=r. Family Q permits\nevery legal terminal gradient and every static completion. The\nnormalized payoff is V=2f_n/||x_0||^2. For general smoothness L>0,\ntranslation and division of the objective by L give precisely\n2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), so W=2R is unchanged.\n\nThe draft conclusion is, for EVERY such ancestor pair,\n\n    sup_E V=1/Z,\n    1/Z <= sup_Q V <=1/(Z-d0^2).                    (1.4)\n\nNo restriction (1.3) is shown lossless for the original W. The\noutside-subtree pairs were the subject of the previous separate\nJOINT UNVERIFIED report a79d801952174a0a. That draft is not a\ncorrectness premise here: all algebra needed for (1.4) is derived below.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used only for the necessary original\nsmooth-convex interpolation rows and its normalization scope.\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201 is used only for the stated hierarchy and\nthe unconditional benchmark comparison in Section11. The scope of\nacceptedb98bb807d514e80d was checked; no claim identifying W with its\nsingle-cut channel is used. No external paper theorem is a premise.\n\n## 2. Literal original rows and all terminal charges\n\nLet F_p,F_q,F_r be the values at the first point of each sector.\nEqual gradients and the two convexity inequalities force values to\ndecrease by the cumulative step mass times the squared sector\ngradient. Put\n\n    Z_p=x_0, Z_q=x_0-Ap, Z_r=x_0-Ap-Bq.\n\nAn actual sample at sector mass s has position Z_p-sp and value\nF_p-s||p||^2, or the corresponding q or r expression. In particular\nthe actual named indices are\n\n    L_p=j, L_q=k, R_0=k+1, N_-=n-1.\n\nUse the ORIGINAL ordered rows\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.    (2.1)\n\nThis is exactly the orientation in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Direct\nsubstitution, without dropping any physical mass, gives\n\n    Q_(L_p,star)=F_p-<p,x_0>+||p||^2/2,\n    Q_(L_q,star)=F_q-<q,x_0>+A<p,q>+||q||^2/2,\n    Q_(R_0,star)=F_r-<r,x_0>+A<p,r>+B<q,r>+||r||^2/2,\n    Q_(L_q,L_p)=F_q-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2+||q||^2/2+d<p,q>,\n    Q_(R_0,L_q)=F_r-F_q+(M+1/2)||q||^2+||r||^2/2+e<q,r>,\n    Q_(R_0,L_p)=F_r-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2+||r||^2/2\n                                      +d<p,r>+B<q,r>.\n\nThe PAID pq cycle is the sum of both original rows:\n\n    C_pq=Q_(L_p,L_q)+Q_(L_q,L_p)\n         =-d||p||^2+(d-v)<p,q>+v||q||^2.             (2.2)\n\nThe negative p-square in this cycle is retained throughout.\n\nFor E put t_pay=T, epsilon=1/2, and add the actual row Q_(n,R_0)\nwith multiplier1. Its expression is f_n-F_r+T||r||^2.\nFor Q put t_pay=T-d0^2/2, epsilon=(1+d0^2)/2, and add\nQ_(N_-,R_0) and Q_(n,N_-) with multiplier1 each, omitting the first\nif N_-=R_0. Since the final actual step is d0 and g_(N_-)=r,\ntheir sum is exactly\n\n    f_n-F_r+(T-d0^2/2)||r||^2\n                   +||g_n+(d0-1)r||^2/2.           (2.3)\n\nIndeed Q_(n,N_-)=f_n-f_(N_-)+d0<g_n,r>+||g_n-r||^2/2,\nand f_(N_-)=F_r-(T-d0)||r||^2. This proves (2.3) even when\nthe queried r sector is a singleton. Define for either family\n\n    w=t_pay+1/2=T+1-epsilon=O+z_loc-epsilon,\n    D=U+e+w=u+d+X+O-epsilon=X_m-epsilon.             (2.4)\n\nThus 2D=Z for E and 2D=Z-d0^2 for Q. These are the actual\nterminal charges, not altered suffix masses.\n\n## 3. The xi=0 original multipliers and entire quadratic identity\n\nFor now the qr-cycle multiplier xi is zero. The core multipliers mu\nare literally as follows; all unlisted core ordered rows have weight0:\n\n| Original row | mu |\n|---|---:|\n| Q_(L_p,star) | alpha |\n| Q_(L_q,star) | beta |\n| Q_(R_0,star) | gamma |\n| Q_(L_q,L_p) | ell+eta |\n| Q_(L_p,L_q) | eta |\n| Q_(R_0,L_q) | beta+ell |\n| Q_(R_0,L_p) | R=alpha-ell |\n\nRequire alpha+beta+gamma=1, alpha,beta,gamma,eta>=0, and\n0<=R<=alpha. The two finite intermediate nodes balance: the incoming\nflow to L_p is ell+eta+R=alpha+eta, and the incoming flow to L_q\nis beta+ell+eta. The core emits1 at R_0 and the optimum absorbs1.\nThe terminal rows in Section2 transfer that emission to the original\nindex n. For the final normalized identity use lambda_ij=2mu_ij,\nincluding the terminal rows; their contributions add if labels coincide.\n\nIntroduce abbreviations\n\n    Lc=d+v, Ec=2D-u, Kc=2D-U, C=U+e,\n    hc=e+2w, theta=alpha+beta, sigma=gamma=1-theta,\n    gbar=alpha*p+beta*q+gamma*r.\n\nExpanding the weighted rows plus t_pay||r||^2 gives the quadratic\n\n    Q=(alpha*u-eta*d)||p||^2+(beta+ell+eta)*v||q||^2+w||r||^2\n      +[ell*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v)]<p,q>\n      +[R*d+gamma*A]<p,r>\n      +[(beta+ell)*e+(1-beta-ell)*B]<q,r>.            (3.1)\n\nThe weighted value/initial part is F_r-<gbar,x_0>. At p=q=r,\nQ=D||r||^2. The p row sum and the cumulative p,q row sum show that\nQ-D||gbar||^2 has zero common-gradient cross terms precisely when\n\n    alpha*Ec=A-eta*Lc,\n    theta*Kc=C-R*Lc.                                (3.2)\n\nThese equations will be imposed explicitly, not assumed from a\nnumerical optimizer. Write s=p-q and tvec=q-r. Under (3.2) the\nentire remaining quadratic is J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2,\nwhere direct collection of the p^2 and cumulative p,q coefficients gives\n\n    J11=alpha*u-eta*d-D*alpha^2,\n    J22=theta*U-R*Lc-D*theta^2,\n    J12=[sigma*(2D*alpha-A)-R*d]/2.                  (3.3)\n\nFor example the mixed coefficient before subtracting the common\nsquare is 2(alpha*u-eta*d)+ell*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v).\nInserting ell=alpha-R, beta=theta-alpha, and\neta*Lc=A-alpha*Ec reduces it to the last expression in (3.3).\nThus no transverse cross term has been left unchecked.\n\nThe COMPLETE identity, in all real vectors x_0,p,q,r and the free\ng_n for Q, is\n\n    ||x_0||^2/(2D)-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n     =2D||gbar-x_0/(2D)||^2\n        +2[J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2]\n        +terminal_square,                          (3.4)\n\nwhere terminal_square is0 for E and ||g_n+(d0-1)r||^2 for Q.\nThis follows by completing the square in x_0 after (2.3),(3.1).\nAll original intermediate sample values cancel through the stated\nsector equalities and the literal flow. PSD here is on the ENTIRE\nequal-sector formal space, not just selected Gram directions or\ndiagonal matrix entries. It is not claimed on the space in which\ngradients inside a sector are independent.\n\nNonnegative row weights, D>0, and J>=0 therefore imply\n2f_n<=||x_0||^2/(2D), because every original Q_ij is nonpositive.\nUnused rows still remain necessary constraints of a legal transcript.\n\n## 4. Uniform physical inequalities, including shallow subtrees\n\nLiteral summation yields\n\n    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1,\n    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2).           (4.1)\n\nThe closed expression has the stated initial value and satisfies the\nidentity because v_h(c-2)=a; no recurrence was run. The elementary\nbounds 7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70, obtained by squaring, imply\n\n    12/5<c<29/12,\n    2/5<a<c-2<5/12,\n    0<v_h<1, 2^l<=X_l<=c^l,\n    2/5<a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2<5/12.                     (4.2)\n\nFor c<29/12 use c<(169/70)(6145/6144) and cross-multiply;\nthe remaining strict comparison is 2028<12288. For a>2/5 use\nbeta_h>99/100 and c<29/12. The inequality a<c-2 follows from\nc(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta_h. Division of (4.1) by c^l\ngives the last ratio bound. These prove (1.2) for both selected\ncheckpoints using their actual children.\n\nSince k<=n-2, the actual suffix includes d0. Therefore\n\n    0<epsilon<13/8,\n    w>=1-a^2/2>9/10,\n    e/w<(5/12)(1+epsilon/w)<505/432<6/5.             (4.3)\n\nFor the last inequality use the right child of k: if Y=X_(l_k),\nthen e<(5/12)Y and T+1>=Y. Hence Y<=w+epsilon, and\nepsilon/w<65/36. The first lower bound for w is the worse, free\nterminal charge; the E charge is larger.\n\nTwo single-checkpoint expressions will be needed. The first is\n\n    U*w>e*(e+w).                                   (4.4)\n\nIf l_k>=2, Y>=4, U>=Y, and w>19Y/32. Thus\n(U-e)w>(7/12)(19/32)Y^2>25Y^2/144>e^2.\nIf l_k=1, Y=2+a>12/5 and e=ac=beta_h<1; consequently\n(U-e)w>(7/5)(9/10)>1>e^2. If l_k=0, U>=1 and e=a<5/12,\nso (U-e)w>(7/12)(9/10)>25/144>e^2. This proves (4.4).\n\nThe second is specific to the first, ancestor checkpoint:\n\n    Fp=D*(u-d)-u^2>0.                              (4.5)\n\nIndeed by (1.1),(2.4),\n\n    Fp=(u-d)*(X+O-epsilon)-d^2\n       >=(X-d)*(X-epsilon)-d^2.\n\nHere X-epsilon is positive. If l>=2, X>=4 and the last expression\nis greater than\n[(7/12)(19/32)-25/144]X^2=(199/1152)X^2>0.\nIf l=1, d=ac<1, X=2+a>12/5, and\nX-epsilon>=1-a^2/2>9/10. The expression is then greater than\n63/50-1>0. This includes shallow ancestors embedded in the huge\noriginal word without discarding their exterior prefix or suffix.\n\nIn particular all denominators below are positive, alpha_max=A/Ec\nlies strictly between0 and1, and\n\n    j_cap=alpha_max*(u-D*alpha_max)=A*Fp/Ec^2>0.      (4.6)\n\n## 5. Strict determinant resource at every ancestor pair\n\nDefine\n\n    Delta=u^2*v^2*w-d^2*e^2*D.\n\nWe prove Delta>0 jointly here. Put tau=5/12. For l>=4, X>=16.\nFirst suppose z_loc>=13/4. At fixed u,d,v,e,z_loc the ratio\nD/w=1+(u+d+v+e)/w decreases as O increases. At O=0,\nw=z_loc-epsilon>z_loc/2, and\n\n    D/u^2 <=(2+tau)/X,\n    e^2/(v^2*w)\n      <2tau^2*min(v,z_loc)^2/(v^2*z_loc)\n      <=2tau^2/max(v,z_loc)\n      <=2tau^2*(2+tau)/X.\n\nThe middle estimate separates v<=z_loc and v>=z_loc; the last\nuses X=v+e+z_loc<=(2+tau)max(v,z_loc). Since d<tau X,\n\n    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)\n       <2tau^4(2+tau)^2<25/32<1.\n\nThis is monotonicity of this explicit scalar ratio only, not a\nmonotonicity assertion about the endpoint optimization problem.\n\nStill with l>=4, suppose z_loc<13/4. Then e<65/48<3/2,\nv>X-5, e^2<3, and e^2/w<2 by (4.3). Moreover\nd+v+e<(1+tau)X<3X/2. Therefore\n\n    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)\n      <tau^2*X^2/(u^2*v^2)*(3+2u+3X)\n      <=tau^2*(3+5X)/v^2\n      <(3+5X)/(4(X-5)^2)<1.\n\nFor the final inequality, writing X=16+x gives\n4(X-5)^2-(3+5X)=4x^2+83x+401>0.\n\nFinally l<=3 implies X<c^3<(5/2)^3<16 and d,e<7, while\nthe ACTUAL D=X_m-epsilon>2^20. If u>=D/4, then v>=1,w>9/10 give\n\n    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)<384160/(9D)<1.\n\nIf u<D/4, the actual outer mass is\nO=D-u-d-X+epsilon>3D/4-23>D/2, and w>O-13/8>D/3.\nUsing d/u<tau and e/v<tau gives the ratio <3tau^4<3/16<1.\nAll cases are exhausted. Hence\n\n                         Delta>0.                  (5.1)\n\n## 6. Exact ellipse, without deleting the multiplier constraints\n\nSet\n\n    sigma0=hc/Kc, alpha_max=A/Ec,\n    a0=(u*v+2D*d)/(2D*Lc), b0=d*hc/(2w*Lc),\n    alpha_opt(sigma)=a0-b0*sigma,\n    R(sigma)=(Kc*sigma-hc)/Lc,\n    eta=(A-Ec*alpha)/Lc,\n    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha-R(sigma).\n\nThese satisfy both contact equations (3.2) identically. All original\nmultiplier signs are equivalent to\n\n    sigma>=sigma0,\n    R(sigma)<=alpha<=min(alpha_max,1-sigma).         (6.1)\n\nHere sigma>=0 and sigma<=1 follow from (6.1). In particular this\nis exactly the multiplier polygon, not an unspecified feasible matrix.\nEquation (4.4) implies\n\n    0<sigma0<sqrt(w/D)<1,\n    w-D*sigma0^2\n      =(U+e)[U*w-e*(e+w)]/Kc^2>0.                  (6.2)\n\nLet y=2D*alpha-A-d*Kc/Lc and\n\n    M2=u^2*v^2+4D*d^2*(e+w).\n\nUsing A*Lc-d*U=u*v in (3.3) gives\n\n    J11=M2/(4D*Lc^2)-y^2/(4D),\n    J22=w-D*sigma^2,\n    J12=(sigma*y+d*hc/Lc)/2.                       (6.3)\n\nFor example substituting eta in J11 yields\n-D*alpha^2+(u*v+2D*d)*alpha/Lc-d*A/Lc;\ncompleting this square proves its displayed constant. Completing\nthe determinant square, and using\n4w(e+w)-hc^2=-e^2, gives exactly\n\n    det J=(w-D*sigma^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2)\n                      -D*w*(alpha-alpha_opt(sigma))^2.          (6.4)\n\nIn particular det J>=0 is an ellipse. Positivity of Delta alone\ndoes not establish that it meets (6.1).\n\nThe center of its horizontal section at sigma0 is\n\n    alpha_c=alpha_opt(sigma0)\n      =[u*v/(2D)+d*(2w*U-e^2)/(2w*Kc)]/Lc.          (6.5)\n\nIt satisfies alpha_c>0 and alpha_c<1-sigma0. For positivity use\n2wU>e^2 from (4.4). For the second inequality multiply by\n2D*w*Kc*Lc: the difference has numerator\n\n    v*w*(2D*U-u*Kc)+2D*w*Lc*e+D*d*e^2>0,\n\nbecause 2DU-uKc=2D(U-u)+uU>0. Thus this point satisfies EVERY\nmultiplier half-plane except possibly alpha<=alpha_max.\n\nIf alpha_c>alpha_max, feasibility can be tested entirely on the\neta=0 edge alpha=alpha_max. Indeed (alpha_c,sigma0) lies strictly\ninside the ellipse and satisfies all the other half-planes. For any\nfeasible point, the line segment to this center stays in the ellipse\nand those other half-planes and meets alpha=alpha_max. This also\nexplains why an unconstrained-center argument alone was incomplete.\nThe explicit proof below supplies a point on that edge.\n\n## 7. Entire eta=0 section and its true upper endpoint\n\nPut alpha=alpha_max, eta=0, j_cap as in (4.6), and define\n\n    Hc=d*Kc-alpha_max*u*Lc,\n    sigma_star=d*hc*Hc/M2,\n    sigma_A=(hc+Lc*alpha_max)/Kc.\n\nOn this edge, beta and ell can equivalently be parameterized by\n\n    beta=(B+ell*Lc)/Kc,\n    sigma=1-alpha_max-beta.\n\nHence sigma ranges over exactly [sigma0,sigma_A] as ell decreases\nfrom alpha_max to0. Notice the STRICT identity\n\n    1-alpha_max-sigma_A=B/Kc>0.                    (7.1)\n\nThus beta>=0 imposes no extra restriction on this edge. The full\ndeterminant reduces to the literal concave quadratic\n\n    det J=j_cap*(w-D*sigma^2)\n                       -(d*hc-Hc*sigma)^2/(4Lc^2)\n         =j_cap*Delta/M2\n                       -M2*(sigma-sigma_star)^2/(4Lc^2).        (7.2)\n\nTo check the constant and curvature, use\nM2=Hc^2+4D*Lc^2*j_cap and w*M2-D*d^2*hc^2=Delta.\nIn particular its unconstrained maximum is strictly positive.\n\nThe following sign calculation removes the upper clamping penalty\nwhenever alpha_c>alpha_max. In that case Hc>0, since\nHc=2D*Lc*(a0-alpha_max) and a0>alpha_c. We claim\n\n                  v*Ec*Kc-2D*B*Lc>0.              (7.3)\n\nWrite Y=e+w>0 and q_ratio=e/v. We have d<5u/12, v<u,\nLc<17u/12, q_ratio<5/12, and by (1.1),(1.2),\n\n    e< (5/17)*(u-v).\n\nExpanding after division of (7.3) by v gives\n\n    Ec*Kc-2D*Lc*(1+q_ratio)\n      =u^2+(1-2q_ratio)*u*Lc-2q_ratio*Lc^2\n        +4uY+(4-2q_ratio)*Lc*Y+4Y^2.               (7.4)\n\nIf Lc<=6u/5, replace q_ratio by5/12 to lower-bound the first\nthree terms by (u+Lc)*(u-5Lc/6)>=0; the Y terms are positive.\nIf Lc>6u/5, then v>47u/60 and\nq_ratio<(5/17)*(13/47)=65/799<1/12. Replacing q_ratio by1/12\ninstead bounds those terms below by (u+Lc)*(u-Lc/6)>0 because\nLc<17u/12<6u. Again the Y terms are positive. This proves (7.3)\nat every depth with the ACTUAL u and w; no parameter was reset.\n\nFor completeness, derive the needed derivative using increments\np-r and q-r, an invertible change from (p-q,q-r) with determinant1.\nWith alpha=alpha_max and eta=0 the entries in these coordinates are\n\n    j_cap,\n    (beta+ell)*v-D*beta^2,\n    (ell*d-beta*alpha_max*u)/2.\n\nAt ell=0, beta=B/Kc. The derivative of their determinant with\nrespect to ell is exactly\n\n    [j_cap*(v*Ec*Kc-2D*B*Lc)\n                 +B*alpha_max*u*Hc/2]/Kc^2>0.       (7.5)\n\nSince d sigma/d ell=-Lc/Kc<0, (7.5) says that the derivative\nin sigma at sigma_A is negative. The strictly concave quadratic\n(7.2) therefore has\n\n                 0<sigma_star<sigma_A.             (7.6)\n\nMoreover sigma_star<sqrt(w/D): (7.2) has positive value there,\nand j_cap>0 in the first expression of (7.2) forces\nw-D*sigma_star^2>0. This proves that only the lower clamp at\nsigma0 can still matter.\n\n## 8. The lower clamping cost is paid explicitly\n\nThe diagonal-cycle case u*v>=d*U will be handled separately in\nSection9. Assume here that\n\n    u*v<d*U, alpha_c>alpha_max.                     (8.1)\n\nIf sigma_star>=sigma0, the positive maximum in (7.2) is admissible\nby (7.6). Suppose instead that sigma_star<sigma0. We show that\nthe lower-clamped choice sigma=sigma0 still has positive determinant.\n\nThe left root of det J=0 on the horizontal line sigma=sigma0 is\n\n    alpha_left=alpha_c\n             -sqrt((w-D*sigma0^2)*Delta)/(2D*w*Lc).\n\nDefine the explicit tangency threshold\n\n    alpha_tan=a0-M2/(2D*Lc*d*Kc).\n\nBecause Hc=2D*Lc*(a0-alpha_max), the inequality\nsigma_star<sigma0 is equivalent to alpha_max>alpha_tan.\nOn the other hand, direct subtraction gives\n\n    alpha_left-alpha_tan\n      =sqrt(Delta)/(2D*w*Lc*Kc)\n          *[sqrt(Delta)/d-sqrt(w*Kc^2-D*hc^2)].       (8.2)\n\nAll square roots are real and strictly positive by (5.1),(6.2).\nThe exact identities\n\n    w*Kc^2-D*hc^2=w*U^2-D*e^2,\n    Delta/d^2=w*(u*v/d)^2-D*e^2\n\nand u*v<d*U prove that the bracket in (8.2) is strictly negative.\nConsequently\n\n    alpha_left<alpha_tan<alpha_max<alpha_c.\n\nThe determinant on that horizontal line is the concave quadratic\nin (6.4), positive strictly between its two roots. Its right root\nis larger than alpha_c. Thus det J(alpha_max,sigma0)>0.\n\nThis is the previously missing clamping estimate. It does not infer\nthe clamped value from Delta>0: (8.2) compares the actual boundary\npenalty against the ellipse radius using the physical case distinction.\n\n## 9. Explicit uniform coefficient rule and full PSD\n\nHere is a literal choice for every ancestor pair and each charge.\nAll quantities are closed expressions in the original physical masses.\n\nCASE I: u*v>=d*U.\n\n    alpha=A/(2D), eta=alpha*u/Lc,\n    theta=C/Kc, sigma=1-theta=sigma0,\n    beta=theta-alpha, gamma=sigma, R=0, ell=alpha.\n\nBoth contact equations hold, because eta*Lc=alpha*u and\n2D*alpha=A. The signs are explicit: alpha,eta,ell>0,\n0<theta<1, and theta>alpha because C>A and Kc<2D.\nThe entire increment matrix is diagonal:\n\n    J12=0,\n    J11=alpha*(u*v-d*U)/(2Lc)>=0,\n    J22=(U+e)[U*w-e*(e+w)]/Kc^2>0.                 (9.1)\n\nThis includes equality u*v=d*U. The mixed coefficient is exactly0,\nso (9.1) proves full PSD, including that boundary case.\n\nCASE II: u*v<d*U and alpha_c<=alpha_max.\n\n    sigma=sigma0, alpha=alpha_c,\n    R=0, eta=(A-Ec*alpha)/Lc,\n    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha.\n\nSection6 proves alpha>0,beta>0,gamma>0; the assumed comparison\ngives eta>=0. All row signs and both contact equations therefore\nhold. Equations (5.1),(6.2),(6.4) give\n\n    J22>0,\n    det J=(w-D*sigma0^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2)>0.        (9.2)\n\nCASE III: u*v<d*U and alpha_c>alpha_max.\n\n    alpha=alpha_max, eta=0,\n    sigma=max(sigma0,sigma_star),\n    R=(Kc*sigma-hc)/Lc,\n    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha-R.\n\nSections7--8 prove sigma0<=sigma<sigma_A and\nsigma<sqrt(w/D). Thus 0<=R<alpha, ell>0, beta>B/Kc>0,\nand gamma>0. Every final multiplier in Section3 is nonnegative.\nBoth contact equations hold by their defining formulas. If\nsigma=sigma_star, (7.2) gives det J=j_cap*Delta/M2>0.\nIf sigma=sigma0>sigma_star, Section8 gives det J>0.\nAlso J22=w-D*sigma^2>0 in either subcase.\n\nFor Cases II and III, the ENTIRE increment quadratic has the explicit\nsquare representation\n\n    J22*||tvec+(J12/J22)*s||^2+(det J/J22)*||s||^2.   (9.3)\n\nTogether with (9.1) in Case I and all squares in (3.4), this proves\nfull PSD on every real equal-sector vector assignment in every case.\nThere is no numerical pair list, unknown matrix, optimization oracle,\nor assumption of favorable angles.\n\nFinally these choices solve precisely the former scalar interface\n(10.2)--(10.5) of a79d801952174a0a. They have\n\n    sigma0<=sigma<sqrt(w/D),\n    sigma<=1,\n    sigma<=(hc+Lc*alpha_max)/Kc,\n    sigma<=(hc+Lc)/(Kc+Lc),\n\nbecause their alpha obeys (6.1). At this sigma our explicit alpha\nhas det J>=0. The nearest-point alpha_clamp of alpha_opt to the\nsame permitted alpha interval can only increase the determinant in\n(6.4). Therefore the literal clamped inequality (10.5) holds too.\nIn Cases II and III the determinant is strictly positive; Case I\nmay be semidefinite exactly at u*v=d*U.\n\n## 10. (12.6)\n\nFor j11>0, the candidate maximizing this determinant on its entire\nsign interval is the explicit clipping of\n\n    xi_stat=(z_q*j11-2b_xi*J12_0)/(2b_xi^2)\n\nto [xi_low,xi_high]. Candidate result and unchanged scope\n\nFix ANY integer m>=1536, with the SAME enclosing parameters everywhere:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j (0<=j<=m),\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j) (0<=j<m),\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, n=2^m-1, D_m=1+2S_m.\n\nThe quantity d_m is only an auxiliary ruler, not an extra step.\nAll original steps, both copies of every child, and the actual last leaf\n1+a are retained. Parameters may depend on the entire\nknown word. The two assigned mechanisms are (I) p=q<b and (II) p!=q,\npq<b^2. There is no restriction on their cap ratio and no cap dilation.\nLet Z_min be the COMPLETE minimum set, and let\nrho_0=dist(Z_0,Z_min). For rho_0>0 define the actual performance\n\n    E=2F_D(Z_n)/rho_0^2.\n\nThe candidate conclusion is, uniformly on BOTH entire declared laws,\n\n    E <= 1/D_m.                                           (1.1)\n\nFor every fixed admissible geometry b,p,q and every R>0 this bound is\nattained by a below-axis start, with strictly positive cap-normal height\nat EVERY original query. Consequently the exact supremum over all\nadmissible starts for each fixed geometry and R is 1/D_m. The same\nconclusion holds for the full finite Walsh bodies and invariant starts\nspecified and proved in Section10.\n\nThis is a restricted-family upper bound and exact family optimum, NOT an\nupper bound on the full W_n. The original problem, unchanged in\nPROBLEM.md, uses EVERY horizon n, EVERY nonnegative predetermined word,\nall finite dimensions, all smooth convex objectives with an attained\nminimum, and W=2R. Nothing here changes those quantifiers or resolves\nthe original minimax target.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is used only for the original normalization\nand interpretation of ordered interpolation residuals; all rows for the\npresent actual function are derived below. In particular its terminal\nreduction does not authorize deleting prefix rows. Accepted\nb98bb807d514e80d defines the complete K channel. Accepted\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n. Accepted\n931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_n/D_m<47/50 only in that UPPER direction.\nIts separate full-class hypothesis W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) is UNPROVED.\n\nEarlier cone and scalar drafts are awareness, not accepted premises.\nThe common-radius scalar interval argument is rederived in Section8.\nThe new asymmetric step in Section7 uses both angle-cap inequalities,\nnot a monotonicity assumption when changing clipping levels.\n\n## 2. One static smooth convex function and its full minimum\n\nThis paragraph applies to any nonempty compact convex body D containing0\nin a finite-dimensional Euclidean space. Maximizing the expression in\nF_D is equivalent to minimizing ||Z-g||^2 over g in D. The minimizer is\nunique by strict convexity; write g=P_D(Z). Its necessary and sufficient\nnormal inequalities are\n\n    <Z-g,v-g><=0 for EVERY v in D.                        (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating along the segment to v. Conversely,\nexpanding ||Z-v||^2-||Z-g||^2 gives a nonnegative quantity under (2.1).\nFor g=P_D(Z) and g'=P_D(Z'), adding the two normal inequalities gives\n\n    ||g-g'||^2 <= <Z-Z',g-g'>,\n\nso P_D is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating F_D at its two maximizers gives\n\n    0 <= F_D(Z')-F_D(Z)-<g,Z'-Z>\n       <= <g'-g,Z'-Z> <= ||Z'-Z||^2.\n\nThus F_D is differentiable with gradient P_D and is 1-smooth. It is\nfinite and convex as a supremum of affine functions over compact D.\nIt is nonnegative because0 is a feasible maximizer candidate.\n\nFor the triangle, F_D(Z)=0 if and only if\n\n    <Z,w_+><=0 and <Z,w_-><=0.                            (2.2)\n\nIf both inequalities hold, every g in D has <Z,g><=0, and the maximum\nis0 at g=0. If one inner product is positive, g=t*w_sigma for a small\nenough t in(0,1] makes t<Z,w_sigma>-t^2||w_sigma||^2/2 positive.\nThis proves both directions and the complete attained minimum set\n\n    Z_min={ (x,y): b*x+p*y<=0, -b*x+q*y<=0 }.\n\nIt is the polar cone of cone{w_+,w_-}; the minimum value is0.\nFor y<0 its section is q*y/b<=x<=-p*y/b. The origin is a minimum,\nbut it will NOT replace the closest minimum in the denominator.\n\nFor arbitrary advertised L>0 use f=L*F_D and the physical steps h_i/L.\nThe same points and gradients divided by L result, and the original\nW-normalized performance is exactly E. No smaller effective word is\nsubstituted.\n\n## 3. The angle, the finite caps, and all below-axis projection faces\n\nDefine local geometric symbols\n\n    ell_+=sqrt(b^2+p^2), ell_-=sqrt(b^2+q^2),\n    e_+=w_+/ell_+, e_-=w_-/ell_-,\n    n_+=(p,-b)/ell_+, n_-=(-q,-b)/ell_-,\n    M=b^2-pq,\n    kappa=M/(ell_+*ell_-), omega=b*(p+q)/(ell_+*ell_-).\n\nEach (e_sigma,n_sigma) is an orthonormal basis; n_sigma points below\nits ray. Direct products give\n\n    <e_+,e_->=-kappa,\n    <e_+,n_->=<e_-,n_+>=-omega,\n    <n_+,n_->=kappa,\n    ell_+^2*ell_-^2-M^2=b^2*(p+q)^2.\n\nIt follows that kappa,omega are in(0,1) and kappa^2+omega^2=1.\nMoreover M<ell_+^2 and M<ell_-^2, since p,q are positive. Hence\n\n    kappa*ell_+ < ell_-,\n    kappa*ell_- < ell_+.                                 (3.1)\n\nIn particular kappa*max(ell_+,ell_-)<min(ell_+,ell_-).\nThis proves the proposed compatibility, without assuming it.\n\nFor Z=(x,y) with y<0 put t_+=<Z,e_+>, t_-=<Z,e_->. Since\n\n    ell_+*t_+ + ell_-*t_-=(p+q)*y<0,\n\nthey cannot both be positive. If neither is positive, Z is in Z_min\nand g=0. If t_sigma=t>0, the corresponding horizontal sign is positive\nfor sigma=+ and negative for sigma=-. In either case\n\n    Z=t*e_sigma+z*n_sigma, z=<Z,n_sigma>>0,\n    <Z,e_opposite>=-kappa*t-omega*z<0.                    (3.2)\n\nHere and below opposite means the other of the two signs. Define\n\n    u=min(t,ell_sigma), g=u*e_sigma,\n    r=Z-g=(t-u)*e_sigma+z*n_sigma.\n\nThe COMPLETE finite vertex normal guards are\n\n    <r,0-g>=-u*(t-u)<=0,\n    <r,w_sigma-g>=(ell_sigma-u)*(t-u)=0,\n    <r,w_opposite-g>\n       =-(t-u)*(kappa*ell_opposite+u)-omega*z*ell_opposite<0.\n                                                               (3.3)\n\nTheir convex combinations give (2.1) for every point of D. Therefore\nthis is the unique projection. These formulas establish that below the\naxis the only possible projection faces are0, the relative interiors\nof the two origin-ray segments, and their finite endpoints. The edge\njoining w_+ to w_- and the triangle interior cannot be projection faces\nthere. Endpoint ties t=ell_sigma are included in (3.3).\n\nFor later use define the one-sided Huber value on t>=0,\n\n    phi_ell(t)=t^2/2                 if 0<=t<=ell,\n               ell*t-ell^2/2        if t>=ell.\n\nAt an active below-axis state, F_D(Z)=phi_(ell_sigma)(t).\nIts finite-cap normal height is EXACTLY\n\n    delta=<Z-g,g>=u*(t-u).\n\nThus delta>0 exactly when t>ell_sigma. A cap-active projection is not\nbeing replaced by conic projection. If t<=ell_sigma then delta=0.\n\n## 4. True initial closest minimum and vertical motion\n\nIf Z_0=(X,-R) lies outside Z_min, exactly one initial ray sigma is active:\n\n    sigma=+ if X>p*R/b, rho_0=(b*X-p*R)/ell_+;\n    sigma=- if X<-q*R/b, rho_0=(-b*X-q*R)/ell_-.\n\nWhen -q*R/b<=X<=p*R/b the initial point is a minimum, all later points\nare stationary, and the gap is0. Such a point has closest radius0 and\ndoes not enter a positive-radius quotient.\n\nFor an active start write Z_0=t_0*e_sigma+z_0*n_sigma as in (3.2).\nSet\n\n    Z_star=Z_0-t_0*e_sigma=z_0*n_sigma.\n\nIts two scores are0 and -omega*z_0<0, so it is in the complete polar.\nFor any U in Z_min,\n\n    <Z_0-Z_star,U-Z_star>=t_0*<e_sigma,U><=0.\n\nExpansion of ||Z_0-U||^2 now shows it is at least t_0^2, with equality\nat Z_star. Thus Z_star is the unique closest point and rho_0=t_0.\nThe nearest point is not assumed to be (0,-R) or the origin.\n\nAt any active physical query with h>=0 the vertical coordinate obeys\n\n    y_new=y-h*u*p/ell_+  on the plus ray,\n    y_new=y-h*u*q/ell_-  on the minus ray.\n\nIt never increases. Since y_0=-R<0, EVERY query and the actual terminal\nremain below the axis, and Sections3--4 apply throughout. All diagnostic\nsteps are strictly positive, so the decrease is strict at active queries.\nEvery vertical coordinate is updated by actual GD.\n\n## 5. Complete cap/ray/polar transition law\n\nAt an active state with ray sigma and coordinates (t,z) from (3.2), take\nany physical step h=1+d>1, set u=min(t,ell_sigma), and put\n\n    v=h*u-t, eta=omega*z>0.\n\nThe new point is -v*e_sigma+z*n_sigma. Its two ray scores are\n\n    -v, kappa*v-eta.                                    (5.1)\n\nConsequently the FULL transition, with all boundaries, is:\n\n* If v<0, stay on sigma with t_new=-v and z_new=z.\n* If v>=0 and kappa*v<=eta, enter Z_min and remain there forever.\n* If kappa*v>eta, switch to the opposite ray with\n\n      t_new=kappa*v-eta,\n      z_new=omega*v+kappa*z,\n      eta_new=(1-kappa^2)*v+kappa*eta.                   (5.2)\n\nIn the last case z_new>0. The next projection is on its new ray interior\nif t_new<ell_opposite, its endpoint if t_new>=ell_opposite; the normal\nheight is positive only for strict inequality. The staying case is\nclassified by the same test against ell_sigma. At v=0 or at equality\nkappa*v=eta, both scores are nonpositive and the polar case applies.\n\nThese are exact formulas, not a recurrence offered as the final result.\nIf t<=ell_sigma then v=d*t; if t>ell_sigma then v=h*ell_sigma-t.\nThus every core overshoot, cap persistence, cap-to-core transition,\ncross-ray jump, and absorption is specified. In particular every return\nloses the positive reserve eta, and changing rays also changes its cap.\n\nFor mechanismI, p=q<b gives\n\n    ell_+=ell_-=ell=sqrt(b^2+p^2),\n    kappa=(b^2-p^2)/(b^2+p^2), omega=2*b*p/(b^2+p^2).\n\nThe initial polar interval is |X|<=p*R/b; outside it,\nrho_0=(b*|X|-p*R)/ell. Equations(3.3),(5.1),(5.2), including the\nmoving vertical coordinate, are the entire symmetric transition law.\nThe following sections evaluate its whole-word score and also close\nmechanismII without any upper bound on ell_+/ell_-.\n\n## 6. Every original query, remote row, terminal row, and optimum row\n\nFor each actual physical point including the terminal, let\n\n    g_i=P_D(Z_i), r_i=Z_i-g_i,\n    f_i=F_D(Z_i)=<Z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nThe single static function already specifies all data. For EVERY ordered\npair i,j of these samples, expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,Z_j-Z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0,                              (6.1)\n\nbecause g_i is in D and r_j is a full normal to D at g_j. This includes\nremote pairs, both orientations, every repeated return, the original\nlast query, and BOTH orientations involving the actual terminal.\nNo incoming terminal row is deleted.\n\nAt the true closest minimum Z_star, use g_star=f_star=0. Then\n\n    Q_(i,star)=<Z_star,g_i><=0,\n    Q_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i>=-delta_i<=0.                  (6.2)\n\nThe first inequality uses the COMPLETE polar, and the second uses the\nnormal at0. Positive outgoing-optimum slack -<Z_star,g_i> and positive\ncap height delta_i are different quantities. Both are paid here.\nAll Gram matrices are literal Euclidean Grams of the displayed points\nand gradients, hence positive semidefinite. Translation by Z_star and\ndivision by rho_0 give the normalized original formulation if desired;\nthat operation does not change the word or omit any rows.\n\n## 7. Asymmetric comparison that retains cap changes and objective weights\n\nFix the initial active sign sigma_0 and NEVER change this reference.\nLet C=ell_(sigma_0) and O=ell_(opposite sigma_0). The two inequalities\n(3.1) imply\n\n    O>=kappa*C and kappa*O<=C.                           (7.1)\n\nFor r>=0 define an envelope set of possible actual below-axis states:\npolar states are allowed; an active sigma_0 state must have amplitude\nat most r; an active opposite state must have amplitude at most kappa*r.\nThere is at most ONE active ray at a time. Call this condition A(r).\nInitially A(t_0) holds, with t_0 equal to the true closest radius.\n\nFor a step1+d define the scalar nondecreasing envelope\n\n    T_(d,C)(r)=max{d*min(r,C), r-(1+d)*C}.               (7.2)\n\nIt is nonnegative since its first term is nonnegative. We claim that\nEVERY actual transition from A(r) satisfies A(T_(d,C)(r)).\nThis is a set inclusion, not an assertion that all such states can be\nattained simultaneously.\n\nFirst note two consequences of (5.1) for an active ray with cap J and\namplitude t<=s. If it stays on that ray, its new amplitude is at most\n\n    (s-h*J)_+.\n\nIndeed for t<=J the signed same-ray output is -d*t<=0; for t>=J\nit is t-h*J. If it switches, its amplitude is at most\n\n    kappa*d*min(s,J).                                   (7.3)\n\nFor t<=J, h*min(t,J)-t=d*t. For t>=J this expression decreases from\nd*J, so its maximum over t<=s is d*min(s,J). The actual switch in\n(5.2) subtracts eta>0; dropping eta only enlarges this upper bound.\n\nIf the source is sigma_0, the staying amplitude is at most\n(r-h*C)_+<=T_(d,C)(r), and (7.3) bounds the opposite amplitude by\nkappa*d*min(r,C)<=kappa*T_(d,C)(r).\n\nIf the source is opposite sigma_0, its amplitude is at most kappa*r.\nThe staying bound and the first inequality of (7.1) give\n\n    (kappa*r-h*O)_+\n       <=kappa*(r-h*C)_+<=kappa*T_(d,C)(r).\n\nIts return to sigma_0 has amplitude at most\nkappa*d*min(kappa*r,O). The SECOND inequality of (7.1) is essential:\n\n    kappa*min(kappa*r,O)<=r,\n    kappa*min(kappa*r,O)<=C,\n\nusing respectively kappa^2<=1 and kappa*O<=C. Therefore that return\namplitude is at most d*min(r,C)<=T_(d,C)(r). Polar states stay polar.\nThis proves the claimed inclusion in every case, including cap/core\nboundaries and every later return.\n\nThe objective comparison also keeps its actual cap. If an A(r) state\nis on sigma_0 then its value is at most phi_C(r). If it is opposite,\nits value is at most phi_O(kappa*r). For all r>=0,\n\n    phi_O(kappa*r)<=phi_C(r).                            (7.4)\n\nBoth sides vanish at0. Their derivatives with respect to r are\nkappa*min(kappa*r,O) and min(r,C), and the former is no larger by\nthe two inequalities just proved. Integrating gives (7.4), including\nevery change of quadratic/affine objective branch. Thus\n\n    A(r) implies F_D(Z)<=phi_C(r).                       (7.5)\n\nDefine r_0=t_0 and, for EACH original chronological step h_i=1+e_i,\ndefine the auxiliary r_(i+1)=T_(e_i,C)(r_i). This is a symbolic finite\ndefinition, never a computed trajectory. Induction on the physical\nindices proves A(r_i) for EVERY i including n, and (7.5) gives\n\n    2F_D(Z_n)/t_0^2 <= 2phi_C(r_n)/t_0^2.              (7.6)\n\nThe reference cap C and the opposite weight kappa remain fixed from the\noriginal start. No child gets a new reference, a freely optimized\nstarting state, or a new cap. This resolves the clipping-imbalance\nobstruction in the naive common-radius comparison: small caps and\nlarge caps cannot be varied independently of the return angle.\n\nThe symmetric mechanism is the special case C=O. It could also use a\ncommon physical radius for both signs, but (7.1)--(7.6) give the stronger\ndirection-sensitive comparison and do not discard the angle.\n\n## 8. Complete evaluation of the auxiliary scalar whole-word envelope\n\nEverything in this section is rederived, not imported as a scalar fact.\nWrite Hub(t)=t^2/2 for |t|<=1 and |t|-1/2 otherwise. Its scalar GD map\nfor a step1+d is\n\n    T_d(t)=t-(1+d)*clip(t,[-1,1]).\n\nIt is continuous and odd. The image of [-r,r] is exactly [-E_d(r),E_d(r)],\nwhere elementary extrema on its linear pieces give\n\n    E_d(r)=d*r           for 0<=r<=1,\n           d             for 1<=r<=1+2d,\n           r-1-d         for r>=1+2d.                   (8.1)\n\nEquivalently E_d(r)=max{d*min(r,1),r-1-d}. For r>=1 the extremal\nmagnitudes are d and |r-1-d|; for r<=1 they are d*r. Continuity and\noddness ensure that the image contains every point between its extrema.\nE_d is continuous and nondecreasing, with matching formulas at both\nthresholds. Equation(7.2) is exactly C*E_d(r/C).\n\nLet Psi_j be the ACTUAL scalar chronological map for the entire word H_j,\nand let F_j(r)=max_(|t|<=r)|Psi_j(t)|. This F_j is an envelope, not the\nobjective F_D. Every Psi_j is continuous and odd, so its interval image\nis exactly [-F_j(r),F_j(r)]. Consequently\n\n    F_0(r)=r,\n    F_(j+1)=F_j composed with E_(d_j) composed with F_j. (8.2)\n\nNo maximizer is selected independently for the two children: (8.2)\ncomposes exact interval images. In particular, composing (7.2) along\nall physical steps gives\n\n    r_n=C*F_m(t_0/C).                                    (8.3)\n\nWe now EVALUATE the bound needed for all positive amplitudes, rather\nthan leaving (8.2) as an unresolved terminal inequality.\n\n8a. Hierarchy parameters and masses.\n\nSince epsilon<=1/6144<1/100, beta>99/100. Using sqrt(2)<29/20 gives\n\n    2<lambda<c<(49/20)*(101/100)<5/2,\n    1/3<99/250<a<1/lambda<1/2.\n\nLet v_0=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]. The identity lambda(lambda-2)=1 and\nstrict increase of t(t-2) for t>2 give 0<v_0<1. Summing the literal\nword gives A_0=1, A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, hence, by substitution and induction,\n\n    A_j=(1-v_0)*2^j+v_0*c^j,\n    d_j/A_j<=c-2<1/2,\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1.                                   (8.4)\n\nIn particular d_j<A_j for every j. All these are symbolic identities\nand inequalities with the same enclosing m.\n\n8b. Interior absorption and exterior formula for exact interval images.\n\nFor every 0<=j<=m,\n\n    F_j(r)<=1 for 0<=r<=A_j,\n    F_j(r)=r-S_j for r>=A_j.                             (8.5)\n\nThis follows directly by induction from (8.1)--(8.2). It holds for j=0.\nAssume it for j. At r>=A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, the first child's output\nradius is r-S_j>=A_j+1+d_j>1+2d_j. The bridge therefore subtracts\n1+d_j and leaves radius at least A_j. The second child subtracts S_j.\nThe resulting radius is r-[2S_j+1+d_j]=r-S_(j+1). At the boundary\nr=A_(j+1) this is1. Monotonicity gives the interior bound at smaller\nr. This proves both assertions and all boundary equalities.\n\n8c. Exact plateau placement.\n\nPut theta=a*beta, so 0<theta<1/2 and c*theta=beta^2<1. For 2<=j<=m,\n\n    A_(j-1)<d_j<A_(j-1)+2d_(j-1).                     (8.6)\n\nFor the first lower inequality at j=2,\n\n    c*(d_2-A_1)=beta*c^2-2c-beta\n               =2epsilon-lambda^2*epsilon^2*(1+epsilon)>0.\n\nThe last sign follows from lambda^2<6 and epsilon<1/100, which give\n6epsilon(1+epsilon)<2. Since c>lambda and lambda-1/lambda=2,\nc-1/c>2. If d_j>A_(j-1), then\n\n    d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)*d_j-2A_(j-1)>0.\n\nThis propagates the lower inequality. For the upper inequality,\nd_j-2d_(j-1)=(c-2)*d_(j-1)<A_(j-1) by (8.4).\n\nWe claim\n\n    F_j(d_j)=theta for EVERY 1<=j<=m.                  (8.7)\n\nAt j=1, d_1=beta<1 and F_1=E_a, so this is a*beta. At j>=2,\n(8.5)--(8.6) send d_j through the first child to\nd_j-S_(j-1), strictly between1 and1+2d_(j-1). Its bridge image radius\nis exactly d_(j-1) by (8.1). The second child sends this to theta by\ninduction. Both complete children remain in the argument.\n\n8d. All real interior amplitudes, including arbitrarily small ones.\n\nFor EVERY integer k>=0 and EVERY 1<=j<=m,\n\n    F_j(d_j*theta^k)<=theta^(k+1).                     (8.8)\n\nProve this simultaneously in all k by induction in j. At j=1 the\ninput beta*theta^k is below1, so equality follows from E_a. At a later\nj, k=0 is (8.7). For k>=1,\n\n    d_j*theta^k=(c*theta)*d_(j-1)*theta^(k-1)\n                <=d_(j-1)*theta^(k-1).\n\nMonotonicity and the first-child induction give an output at most\ntheta^k<=1. Its bridge image radius is at most d_(j-1)*theta^k.\nThe second-child induction at k gives at most theta^(k+1).\n\nFor any real 0<r<=d_j, choose an integer k>=0 such that\n\n    d_j*theta^(k+1)<r<=d_j*theta^k.\n\nSuch k exists because 0<theta<1. At a geometric endpoint use it as\nthe weak upper endpoint. From (8.8) and monotonicity,\nF_j(r)<=theta^(k+1)<r/d_j. For d_j<r<=A_j, (8.5) gives\nF_j(r)<=1<r/d_j. At r=0 the value is0. Thus uniformly on the entire\ninterior, with no excluded threshold or preimage,\n\n    F_j(r)<=min(1,r/d_j), 0<=r<=A_j.                   (8.9)\n\nThis controls any number of core entries, departures and later returns\nin the auxiliary map. It therefore controls all actual triangle returns\nalready enclosed by Section7.\n\n8e. Evaluated normalized objective for every positive amplitude.\n\nFor 0<r<=A_m, (8.9) gives\n\n    2Hub(F_m(r))/r^2=F_m(r)^2/r^2<=1/d_m^2<1/D_m.\n\nThe strict comparison follows without asymptotics. From a>1/3,\nA_m<c^m and m>=1536>=5, one has c^m>2^m>=32>18, hence\n\n    d_m^2>c^(2m)/9>2c^m>D_m=2A_m-1.                 (8.10)\n\nFor r>=A_m, (8.5) gives F_m(r)=r-S_m>=1 and therefore\n\n    2Hub(F_m(r))/r^2=(2r-D_m)/r^2<=1/D_m,             (8.11)\n\nbecause r^2-D_m*(2r-D_m)=(r-D_m)^2>=0. Equality in this last bound\nholds exactly at r=D_m, which is in the exterior since D_m>=A_m.\nWe have thus proved the fully evaluated envelope bound\n\n    2Hub(F_m(r))/r^2<=1/D_m for EVERY real r>0.          (8.12)\n\n## 9. Whole-word score, exact attainment, and cap-active conclusion\n\nCombine (7.6), (8.3), and phi_C(C*t)=C^2*Hub(t) for t>=0. With\nr=t_0/C>0, the ACTUAL whole-word score satisfies\n\n    E=2F_D(Z_n)/t_0^2\n       <=2Hub(F_m(r))/r^2<=1/D_m.                      (9.1)\n\nThis proves the desired uniform quantitative obstruction on all starts\nand all parameters of mechanismI and mechanismII. There is no remaining\nsupremum to bound, no terminal inequality left open, and no resetting\nof the reference side or cap at a child boundary.\n\nFor exact attainment fix ANY b,p,q,R in the declared domain and choose\n\n    X=[ell_+^2*D_m+p*R]/b, Z_0=(X,-R).\n\nIts initial plus-ray score is t_0=ell_+*D_m and its true closest radius\nis exactly that number by Section4. Let P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, the mass of\nthe first i original physical entries, for 0<=i<=n. Then the actual\ntrajectory and projections are\n\n    Z_i=Z_0-P_i*w_+,\n    t_i=ell_+*(D_m-P_i), g_i=w_+,\n    y_i=-R-p*P_i<0.                                    (9.2)\n\nIndeed 0<=P_i<=S_m, so t_i>=ell_+*(D_m-S_m)=ell_+*A_m>ell_+.\nThe projection guards (3.3) apply at every point, proving the induction\nfor actual GD, including the terminal. The cap-normal height is\n\n    delta_i=ell_+^2*(D_m-P_i-1)>0\n\nat EVERY original query and at the terminal. Thus the attaining family\nreally uses active finite caps; this is not the previously excluded\nzero-cap cone regime. The terminal value is\n\n    F_D(Z_n)=ell_+^2*(A_m-1/2)=ell_+^2*D_m/2.\n\nDividing by t_0^2=ell_+^2*D_m^2 proves E=1/D_m. The original full-W17/(16D_m) hypothesis remains\nunproved, and no minimax completion follows.\n\n## 10. Labels are s in G; nonzero characters are indexed by\nt in G\\{0}, with chi_t(s)=(-1)^(t dot s), the dot product being in F_2.\nFix positive horizontal weights b_t, choose one nonzero character u,\nand set b_u=b. (10.1)\n\nFor any Z in E_plane, its unique projection onto D_full is fixed by\nevery U_tau, since its images minimize the same strictly convex distance.\nThus it lies in E_plane and is exactly the triangle projection in (10.1).\nThe full projection potential restricted to E_plane is therefore F_D,\nand its full gradient lies in the plane. Actual full-body GD stays in\nthat plane and agrees with the triangle trajectory at every physical\nquery, terminal, and vertical update. All new assertions, including supporting assertions,\nbelong to this one joint unverified derivation.\n\n## 1. Exact candidate and scope\n\nFix any integer m>=1536, once and for the entire word. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_j=1+2*S_j,\n    n_m=2^m-1, ell=1+a, b=a*beta.\n\nHierarchy a,c,H_j and the map notation below are LOCAL notation. There is no child parameter reset.\n\nFor any fixed u,v>0, put\n\n    g(x)=clip(x,[-v,u]),\n    f(x)=x*g(x)-g(x)^2/2.\n\nLet Psi_j be the actual chronological GD map of the entire H_j on this\nONE static function. The candidate is\n\n    for EVERY u,v>0 and EVERY s!=0,\n        2*f(Psi_m(s)) <= s^2/D_m;                    (1.1)\n    sup_(s!=0) 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 = 1/D_m.            (1.2)\n\nBoth s=D_m*u and s=-D_m*v attain equality. The statement includes\narbitrary dependence of u/v on m, both initial signs, every initial\namplitude and the literal final physical leaf.\n\nWriting the exact interval image as\n\n    Psi_m([-R,R])=[-p_m(R,R),q_m(R,R)],\n\nthe two endpoint conclusions, for EVERY R>0, are\n\n    2*f(-p_m(R,R)) <= R^2/D_m,\n    2*f( q_m(R,R)) <= R^2/D_m.                       (1.3)\n\nThe original quantity remains\n\n    W_n(h)=sup 2*(F(x_n)-F(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2),\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad F(x_i), W_n(h)=2*R_n(h),\n\nover every permitted finite dimension and smooth convex function with\nan attained minimum, with every original n and nonnegative h retained.\nThe target W_n^star/U_n^ZJ ->1, or a rigorous negative resolution of it,\nis unchanged. PROBLEM.md is unchanged. A restricted-family equality\nsuch as(1.2) is not an upper bound on W_(n_m)(H_m).\n\nAccepted fact_id `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the original normalization,\nthe ordered residual convention and the distinction between actual\nscalar rows and full formal-space certificates. Its full-class\nhypothesis17/(16D_m) remains unproved.\n\nTASK.md and master_guidance `9f1dcb56a7b38fea` govern this allocation.\nPlan `40cf581f6e285416` records the new mechanism. Prior reports\n`05423f48b823e3bf` and `72141426b777f11c` were read as awareness,\nnot accepted premises. Every prior-draft premise used below is derived\nagain inside this joint candidate.\n\n## 2. Recovered domain and the new mechanism\n\nThe recovered prior state was: the uniform band\n\n    max(u,v)/min(u,v) <= 1/(c-2),\n\nthe entire tail\n\n    max(u,v)/min(u,v) >= Gamma_m,\n    Gamma_m=sqrt(2*a*d_(m-1)*D_m),\n\nall zero/single-sign-crossing histories, and the finite-clipping transfer\nfor u>=d_(m-1)*v with s<=u, had joint unverified derivations. The missing\ndomain lay between the band and Gamma_m; below d_(m-1) the upper clip\ncan reactivate repeatedly. None of those prior claims was an accepted\nfact. The old one-sided tail proof is not repeated as a new result.\n\nThe NEW mechanism proves for u>=2v, simultaneously for every complete\nchild H_j, a weak global radial bound and a sharp two-endpoint plateau:\n\n    |Psi_j(s)| <= max(v, |s|-S_j*v/3),               (2.1)\n    Phi_j(d_j*v*b^t,d_j*v*b^t)\n        <= (v*b^(t+1),v*b^(t+1)), t=0,1,2,... .     (2.2)\n\nPhi_j is the exact signed interval map; inequalities are coordinatewise.\nThe radial bound(2.1) is deliberately much weaker than the false\nprimitive-energy inequality. Its role is to prove(2.2), not to certify\nthe terminal objective by itself.\n\nA negative-to-positive return at a level-j bridge lands at most d_j*v.\nThe following full H_j therefore captures it by(2.2). The actual suffix\nthen consists of increasing ancestor levels, each followed by its own\ncomplete right child. Repeated reactivation inside any such child is\nalready included in the joint induction. This gives a closed terminal\nbound, not merely a recursive selector. The previously established small\nband, jointly rederived in Section9, overlaps u/v>=2 and completes all\nratios. This is one substantive mechanism; no second search law was\nactivated.\n\n## 3. Parameter inequalities and literal word structure\n\nHere epsilon<=1/6144<1/100, beta>99/100. Since sqrt(2)<29/20,\n\n    2<c<(49/20)*(101/100)<5/2,\n    1/3<99/250<a<1/lambda<1/2,\n    0<b<1/2, c*b=beta^2<1.                         (3.1)\n\nLet eta=a/(c-2). From lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda we get\nc*(c-2)>1 and 0<eta<1. Summing the physical concatenation gives\n\n    A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,\n    d_j/A_j<=c-2<1/2,\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1.                              (3.2)\n\nThe closed formula has initial value1, and its update follows from\n2*eta+a=c*eta. In particular S_j=A_j-1, A_j>=A_1=2+a>2 for j>=1,\nand\n\n    d_j<S_j for every j>=1.                         (3.3)\n\nIndeed d_j<A_j/2<A_j-1 because A_j>2. The d_j increase strictly;\nd_0=a<1, d_1=beta<1, and d_j>1 for j>=2 because d_2=beta*c>1.\n\nFor the small-band proof we also need, for 2<=j<=m,\n\n    d_j>A_(j-1),\n    (c-1)*d_(j-1)<A_(j-1).                          (3.4)\n\nFor the first inequality at j=2, expansion gives\n\n    c*(d_2-A_1)=2*epsilon-lambda^2*epsilon^2*(1+epsilon)>0,\n\nsince lambda^2<6 and 6*epsilon*(1+epsilon)<2. If d_j>A_(j-1),\nthen, using A_j=2*A_(j-1)+d_j/c,\n\n    d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)*d_j-2*A_(j-1)>0,\n\nas c-1/c>lambda-1/lambda=2. The second inequality in(3.4) follows\nfrom (c-1)*(d_(j-1)/A_(j-1)) <= (c-1)*(c-2)<3/4.\n\nAt the final level,\n\n    d_m^2>D_m.                                      (3.5)\n\nIndeed d_m^2>c^(2m)/9 and D_m<2*c^m, while c^m>2^m>=32>18 for\nm>=5. This covers all stated m. d_m is only an auxiliary ruler; the\nlargest physical bridge is d_(m-1).\n\nH_j has2^j-1 actual steps, by induction on its defining concatenation.\nEach occurrence of a level-k bridge inside H_j has an actual H_k\nimmediately before and immediately after it. Thus its physical prefix\nand suffix masses inside H_j are each at least S_k. All steps exceed1.\nThe first and final steps of every nonempty H_j are the leaf ell=1+a.\n\nA further exact word property is needed. After a chosen level-k bridge,\nfirst consume its following H_k. This completes its canonical H_(k+1)\nblock. If any word remains, ascend past every ancestor for which this\nwas a right child. The next step is the bridge of the first ancestor\nfor which the completed block lies on the left. Its level is larger\nthan k, and its full right child follows it. Repeating gives the suffix\n\n    H_k, (1+d_(l_1)),H_(l_1), ...,\n         (1+d_(l_r)),H_(l_r),                       (3.6)\n\nwhere k<l_1<...<l_r<=m-1. The list may be empty. This is a\ndecomposition of the literal binary concatenation tree, not a deletion\nor permutation of steps. At k=0 the first H_0 is empty. If the chosen\nstep is the last leaf, there is no subsequent step.\n\n## 4. Static function, every row, interval images and the cutoff\n\nFor u,v>0 the displayed f equals x^2/2 on[-v,u], u*x-u^2/2 for\nx>=u, and -v*x-v^2/2 for x<=-v. Its derivative is the continuous,\nnondecreasing,1-Lipschitz function g. Splitting an interval at -v and u\nproves the Lipschitz assertion directly. Thus f is finite, convex,\ndifferentiable and globally1-smooth. Its COMPLETE minimum set is{0},\nwith f(0)=0. Its true initial radius is exactly |s|.\n\nFor every original query, including the terminal, define\n\n    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i), 0<=i<n_m,\n    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n_m.\n\nThese are all queries of ONE static function. Put y_i=x_i-g_i. Direct\nexpansion of f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 gives, for every ordered pair,\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2\n        =y_j*(g_i-g_j)<=0.                          (4.1)\n\nIf y_j>0 then g_j=u>=g_i; if y_j<0 then g_j=-v<=g_i; and if y_j=0\nthe residual is zero. This proves all remote rows, both orders,\nthreshold equalities, repeated-query cases and the optimum rows after\nadjoining x=g=f=0. The complete Gram matrix is an actual scalar\nEuclidean Gram, so its quadratic forms are squares of real linear\ncombinations. This is not a full-space dual certificate. For general\nL>0 set F=L*f; the original steps h_i/L give exactly these positions\nand the same normalized payoff.\n\nGlobally f(x)<=x^2/2; on a clipped ray the difference is the square of\nthe distance from its threshold divided by2. Reflection swaps u and v\nand changes the sign of every iterate. Hence assume u>=v below.\n\nFor a step1+d, d>0, the exact image of[-p,q], p,q>=0, is[-p',q'] with\n\n    p'=max(d*min(q,u), p-(1+d)*v),\n    q'=max(d*min(p,v), q-(1+d)*u).                   (4.2)\n\nOn each ray, extrema occur at the endpoint or at its included clip\nthreshold; continuity fills the interval. The first term in either\nmaximum is nonnegative. Formula(4.2) therefore includes empty sides,\nzero endpoints and threshold ties. Let E_d be this pair map and define\n\n    Psi_j([-p,q])=[-p_j(p,q),q_j(p,q)],\n    Phi_j(p,q)=(p_j(p,q),q_j(p,q)).\n\nEvery complete map is continuous and fixes0; its image is an interval.\nThe exact pair map is coordinatewise nondecreasing by interval\ninclusion and satisfies\n\n    Phi_0=identity, Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j o E_(d_j) o Phi_j. (4.3)\n\nThis is exact composition of compatible images. It does not select\nindependent optimal child histories. No recurrence was executed.\n\nLet r=u/v>=1. The first physical level that can produce a STRICT return\nabove u from a negative state is\n\n    k_cut=min({j in{0,...,m-1}:d_j>r} union{m})\n         =min(m, floor(log_c(r/a))+1).               (4.4)\n\nThe value m means no eligible physical level. Since d_0,d_1<1<=r,\nk_cut>=2. At r=d_j the level j only touches u and is ineligible for\nstrict reactivation; the next eligible level, if present, is j+1.\n\nTo check the cutoff directly, write a negative query as -w, w>0. A\npositive return is (1+d)*min(w,v)-w and is at most d*v, with equality\nat w=v. If d*v>u, the exact set producing a return greater than u is\n\n    u/d < w < (1+d)*v-u,                            (4.5)\n\nwith the formula split at w=v. Both boundary values produce a tie at\nu. If d*v=u they coalesce at w=v. If d*v<u there is no such query.\nThese are local possibilities, not an assertion that every query in\nthis interval is reachable from any prescribed initial point.\n\nFor a child H_j with all its levels below k_cut, and an actual input\ns<=u, the finite-clipping path coincides with the one-sided derivative\nmax(x,-v) throughout that child: every return is <=u, and a positive\nquery <=u immediately becomes nonpositive. Induction over the actual\nsteps proves this guarded comparison. An input above u is not covered\nby this comparison. The proof below does not require a one-sided law\nabove this guard and retains every smaller physical step.\n\n## 5. Exact positive tail of every complete child\n\nFor every u>=v>0, every j>=1 and every real s,\n\n    Psi_j(s) <= max(a*v, s-S_j*u).                  (5.1)\n\nMore precisely, if Psi_j(s)>a*v, then EVERY nonterminal query of that\nchild is positive and saturated, the terminal is positive, and\n\n    Psi_j(s)=s-S_j*u.                               (5.2)\n\nHere is a chronological proof. A trajectory hitting0 stays at0. If\nthe terminal is positive and some negative-to-positive crossing occurs,\ntake its last such crossing, at a level-k bridge. Its positive landing\nis at most d_k*v. Every later nonterminal query is positive saturated:\na positive core query would turn negative at the next step, since all\nsteps exceed1, contradicting the definition of the last crossing and\nthe positive terminal. If T is the remaining physical mass, then\n\n    x_terminal <= d_k*v-T*u <= d_k*v-S_k*u.\n\nFor k>=1 this is negative by(3.3) and u>=v, impossible. For k=0 it is\nat most a*v. Therefore terminal>a*v excludes every such crossing. It\nalso forces positive initial sign and excludes positive-to-negative\ncrossings. Every nonterminal query is consequently positive saturated,\nwhich proves(5.2). When the terminal is at most a*v, inequality(5.1)\nholds directly. This proves both assertions for all s, including\nnegative inputs and all clipping ratios. In interval form,\n\n    q_j(p,q)<=max(a*v,q-S_j*u).                      (5.3)\n\nNo monotonicity in the clipping ratio was assumed. The terminal need\nnot be saturated: for example s=S_j*u+v gives terminal v, which is\nbelow u when u>=2v and is still greater than a*v. Every nonterminal\nquery in this example is saturated because at least the final leaf\nremains. Thus the affine-tail assertion refers to queried updates,\nwithout imposing a false terminal saturation condition.\n\n## 6. Joint weak-drift and signed-plateau induction for u>=2v\n\nAssume u>=2v for this section. We prove together, for all j>=1 up to m,\n\n    |Psi_j(s)|<=max(v,|s|-S_j*v/3) for all real s,    (Drift_j)\n    Phi_j(d_j*v*b^t,d_j*v*b^t)\n       <=(v*b^(t+1),v*b^(t+1)) for all integers t>=0. (Plateau_j)\n\nThe proof order is explicit: prove both at j=1; given both at j,\nprove Plateau_(j+1), then Drift_(j+1). No unproved parent bound is used to\nprove itself.\n\nBase plateau: d_1=beta<1, so the radius d_1*v*b^t is in the common\ncore. The single leaf maps x to-a*x, giving both endpoints exactly\nv*b^(t+1). Base drift: only outputs of absolute value>v need checking.\nA positive output>v is covered by(5.2), with displacement ell*u>=ell*v/3.\nFor a negative output-p, p>v, and a negative input, the input is negative\nsaturated, so |s|=p+ell*v. For a positive input, the leaf gives p<=a*s,\nwhether the input is core or saturated. Then\n\n    s-p >= (1-a)*s > ((1-a)/a)*v > ell*v/3,\n\nbecause a<1/2 gives (1-a)/a>1 and ell/3<1/2. This proves Drift_1.\n\nFor the unscaled plateau at j+1, abbreviate A=A_j, S=S_j and d=d_j.\nStart with the radius R=d_(j+1)*v=c*d*v. Let[-p,q] be its exact\nfirst-child image. The induction drift and(5.3) give\n\n    p<=max(v,(c*d-S/3)*v),\n    q<=max(a*v,(c*d-2*S)*v)=a*v.                    (6.1)\n\nFor the equality in the bound for q, observe\n\n    c*d-2*S < (5/4)*A-2*(A-1)\n              =2-3*A/4 <=1/2-3*a/4 < a,\n\nusing A>=2+a and a>1/3>2/7. Also\n\n    (c-2)*d < A/4 < (A+2)/3,\n\nby d<A/2 and c-2<1/2. Hence\n\n    c*d-S/3 < 1+2*d,\n    p <= (1+2*d)*v.                                 (6.2)\n\nApply the EXACT signed step map(4.2) to this actual first-child image.\nSince q<=a*v<u,\n\n    p'=max(d*min(q,u),p-(1+d)*v)<=d*v,\n    q'=max(d*min(p,v),q-(1+d)*u)<=d*v.               (6.3)\n\nThe first bound uses a<1; the second uses q-(1+d)*u<0. Thus the entire\nactual bridge image lies in[-d_j*v,d_j*v]. The second child's Plateau_j at\nt=0 bounds BOTH terminal endpoint magnitudes by b*v. This is Plateau_(j+1)\nat t=0.\n\nFor t>=1,\n\n    d_(j+1)*v*b^t=(c*b)*d_j*v*b^(t-1)\n                   <=d_j*v*b^(t-1).\n\nBy Plateau_j at t-1 and interval inclusion, the first child's actual image\nhas both endpoint magnitudes at most v*b^t<v. It is inside both clip\nthresholds. The bridge is exactly x -> -d_j*x and leaves both endpoint\nmagnitudes at most d_j*v*b^t. Plateau_j at t then bounds BOTH second-child\nendpoint magnitudes by v*b^(t+1). This proves Plateau_(j+1) for every t.\n\nTo prove Drift_(j+1), write\n\n    y=Psi_j(s), z=y-(1+d_j)*g(y), w=Psi_j(z).\n\nIf |w|<=v, the drift claim holds. If w>v, the parent version of(5.2)\ngives s-w=S_(j+1)*u>=S_(j+1)*v/3. It remains to treat w=-p with p>v.\nThe child's drift Drift_j gives\n\n    |z|>=p+S*v/3.                                  (6.4)\n\nThe child's plateau Plateau_j at t=0 also implies\n\n    |z|>d*v,                                       (6.5)\n\nsince otherwise |w|<=b*v<v. There are three possible bridge cases.\n\nCase A: z>0. A negative y could only return to at most d*v, contradicting\n(6.5). Hence y>0 remains positive across the bridge, which requires a\npositive saturated query and gives y=z+(1+d)*u. In particular y>a*v,\nso(5.2) for the first child gives\n\n    s=z+(S+1+d)*u\n      >=p+S*v/3+(S+1+d)*v\n      >=p+(2*S+1+d)*v/3.\n\nCase B: z<0 and y<0. A negative core input turns positive; therefore\nthis bridge query is negative saturated and |y|=|z|+(1+d)*v>v.\nApplying Drift_j first to s and then using(6.4) yields\n\n    |s|>=|y|+S*v/3\n        >=p+2*S*v/3+(1+d)*v\n        >=p+(2*S+1+d)*v/3.\n\nCase C: z<0 and y>0. If y<=a*v, the crossing magnitude is at most\nd*a*v<d*v, contradicting(6.5). Thus y>a*v and the first child has\nthe exact affine tail s=y+S*u. Put r_z=|z|. We claim\n\n    s-r_z >= (A+d)*v/3.                             (6.6)\n\nIf 0<y<=u, then r_z=d*y and\ns-r_z=S*u+(1-d)*y. For d>=1 this is at least(A-d)*u, and\n\n    A-d >= (A+d)/3\n\nfollows from d<A/2. For d<1, it is at least S*u>=S*v, and\nS>(A+d)/3 because A>2, d<1 imply 2*A>3+d. If y>=u, then\nr_z=(1+d)*u-y and\n\n    s-r_z=2*y+(S-1-d)*u >= (A-d)*u >= (A+d)*v/3.\n\nThis proves(6.6), including y=u. Combining it with(6.4) gives\n\n    s>=p+(S+A+d)*v/3=p+(2*S+1+d)*v/3.\n\nThe value y=0 cannot occur here, and z=0 is excluded by p>v. Since\nS_(j+1)=2*S+1+d, these three cases prove Drift_(j+1). This completes the\njoint induction, with both signed endpoints retained and all physical\nchild histories compatible.\n\nFor every real 0<R<=d_j*v, select an integer t>=0 with\n\n    d_j*v*b^(t+1)<R<=d_j*v*b^t.\n\nAt a geometric equality use that equality as the upper endpoint of\nthe next cell. Plateau_j and interval inclusion imply both output endpoints\nare at most v*b^(t+1)<R/d_j. Therefore\n\n    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j, 0<|s|<=d_j*v.              (6.7)\n\nThe value0 maps to0. Every real amplitude in the indicated domain is\ncovered; there is no finite mesh or numerical recurrence.\n\n## 7. Every return is paid through the actual terminal suffix\n\nStill assume u>=2v. Suppose a trajectory on H_m has ANY negative-to-\npositive crossing, at a level-k bridge. Its landing is in(0,d_k*v].\nIf k>=1, the following literal H_k, the first component of(3.6), maps\nit to a point of magnitude at most b*v by Plateau_k. At each subsequent\nancestor level l, the incoming point has magnitude at most b*v<v,\nso its gradient is the point itself, and the bridge produces magnitude\nat most d_l*b*v<=d_l*v. The entire following H_l captures it to b*v\nby Plateau_l. Induction through the finite suffix(3.6) proves\n\n    |x_(n_m)|<=b*v whenever such a crossing has k>=1. (7.1)\n\nIf k=0, the initial return is at most a*v<v. Its following H_0 is\nempty. If this is the final physical leaf, this point is the actual\nterminal. Otherwise the next level in(3.6) is l>=1. Its bridge sends\nthe point to magnitude at most a*d_l*v<=d_l*v; its full H_l then\ncaptures it to b*v. Every further ancestor is treated as just proved.\nConsequently the uniform conclusion, including the last leaf, is\n\n    |x_(n_m)|<=a*v                                  (7.2)\n\nafter ANY negative-to-positive crossing. Both terminal signs are\ncovered. Choosing the first, last, or any other such crossing gives\nthe same legal suffix argument. Repeated upper-clip activations INSIDE\nthese children were already included in Section6 and have not been\nerased or independently optimized.\n\nWe now pay the original initial amplitude. If 0<|s|<=d_m*v, then\n(6.7), f<=x^2/2 and(3.5) give\n\n    2*f(x_(n_m))/s^2<=1/d_m^2<1/D_m.                (7.3)\n\nIf |s|>d_m*v and there was a negative-to-positive crossing, (7.2)\nplaces the terminal in the common core and gives\n\n    2*f(x_(n_m))/s^2<=a^2*v^2/s^2\n                          <a^2/d_m^2<1/D_m.        (7.4)\n\nThese two estimates cover EVERY initial amplitude for EVERY trajectory\nhaving such a return. There is no remaining cutoff-scale or ratio gap\nwithin u/v>=2. They also cover ratios depending on m, k_cut=m, exact\nties d_j*v=u, return level2, root levelm-1 and the final level0 leaf.\n\n## 8. All remaining histories: exact zero/single-crossing estimate\n\nFor completeness the remaining bound is derived here on the SAME word.\nAt every physical index i let P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t.\nIf its level is k, h_i=1+d_k. We claim\n\n    d_k^2/((1+P_i)^2*(1+2*T_i)) <= 1/D_m.           (8.1)\n\nBecause the adjacent children are H_k, P_i,T_i>=S_k. Write A=A_k,\nd=d_k, M=S_m-S_(k+1)>=0. For some x in[0,M],\n\n    P_i=S_k+x, T_i=S_k+M-x.\n\nTheir denominator is (A+x)^2*(2*A-1+2*M-2*x). Its logarithm is\nconcave on[0,M], being a sum of logarithms of positive affine\nfunctions. A concave function is bounded below by the minimum of its\ntwo endpoint values. The endpoint x=M minus the endpoint x=0 for the\ndenominator itself is\n\n    M*(2*A*(A-1)+M*(2*A-1))>=0.\n\nThus the denominator is at least A^2*(2*A-1+2*M). Also\n\n    D_m=4*A+2*d-1+2*M.\n\nAt k=0, A=1 and d=a<1/2 imply d^2*(4*A+2*d-1)<A^2*(2*A-1).\nAt k>=1, A>2 and d/A<1/2 imply\n\n    (d/A)^2*(4*A+2*d-1)/(2*A-1)\n       <(1/4)*(5*A-1)/(2*A-1)<=3/4<1,\n\nwhere 5*A-1<=3*(2*A-1) follows from A>=2. Since also d^2<A^2,\nadding the terms2*M*d^2 and2*M*A^2 proves(8.1). Every occurrence,\nincluding the first and final leaves, is covered.\n\nIf a trajectory hits0 it has zero terminal gap. Consider a positive\nstart s and a trajectory with exactly one positive-to-negative crossing,\nat index i, and no subsequent return to positive. Every earlier query\nis positive saturated, since a positive core query would cross at its\nnext step. The crossing query is y=s-P_i*u>0 and\n\n    min(y,u)<=s/(1+P_i).\n\nFor y>=u this follows from s>= (1+P_i)*u; for y<=u it follows from\ns=P_i*u+y>=(1+P_i)*y. The crossing lands at-r, where\n\n    0<r<=d_k*min(y,u)<=d_k*s/(1+P_i).                (8.2)\n\nEvery later nonterminal query is negative saturated, since a negative\ncore query would return to positive. The terminal is -w with\nw=r-T_i*v>0. If w>=v, then\n\n    2*f(-w)=2*v*r-(2*T_i+1)*v^2<=r^2/(2*T_i+1),\n\nby completing the square. If 0<w<=v, then r<=(T_i+1)*v gives\nw=r-T_i*v<=r/(T_i+1), and\n\n    2*f(-w)=w^2<=r^2/(T_i+1)^2<=r^2/(2*T_i+1).\n\nThis includes T_i=0. Together with(8.1)-(8.2), it proves the target\nscore bound for all such histories at EVERY ratio.\n\nFor a positive trajectory with no crossing, all nonterminal queries\nare positive saturated. The terminal y=s-S_m*u is nonnegative. If\ny>=u, set z=s/u and obtain the exact score\n\n    (2*z-D_m)/z^2 <=1/D_m,\n\nbecause z^2-D_m*(2*z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2. If 0<=y<=u, then\ns=S_m*u+y>=A_m*y and the score is at most1/A_m^2<=1/D_m.\nA negative trajectory with no crossing has the identical proof with\nv and absolute positions. Without any negative-to-positive crossing,\na nonzero trajectory has either no sign change, or starts positive\nand makes exactly one positive-to-negative crossing. Hence Section7\nand this section exhaust every trajectory for u>=2v.\n\nThe proof of(8.1) is an upper estimate for a scalar expression inside\nthis trajectory partition. It does not reverse the accepted lower\nbound K_n(h)<=W_n(h) of fact b98.\n\n## 9. Joint small-band derivation and complete all-ratio closure\n\nThis section supplies the already known mechanism as a joint premise,\nnot as a new search result. Put T_band=1/(c-2)>2 and assume\n\n    1<=u/v<=T_band.\n\nFor all j, an input s>=A_j*u stays positive saturated throughout H_j\nand Psi_j(s)=s-S_j*u. These are exact exterior branches including ties.\n\nWe prove the exact capture rectangle\n\n    Psi_j([-A_j*v,A_j*u])=[-v,u].                   (9.1)\n\nAt j=0 this is the identity. Assuming it for j, write A=A_j,d=d_j.\nThe first child of the parent capture interval has exact endpoints\n-(A+1+d)*v and (A+1+d)*u, by the inductive central image and the\ntwo exact exterior branches. Formula(4.2) then gives bridge endpoints\n\n    -max(d*u,A*v), max(d*v,A*u).\n\nSince d/A<=c-2 and u/v<=T_band, these are exactly -A*v and A*u.\nThe second child gives(9.1) by induction.\n\nFor every j>=1 and every integer t>=0 we next prove BOTH coordinates\nof the plateau\n\n    Phi_j(d_j*u*b^t,d_j*u*b^t)\n       <= (u*b^(t+1),u*b^(t+1)).                   (9.2)\n\nAt j=1,t=0, the exact step formula gives\n\n    p_1=max(b*u,beta*u-ell*v),\n    q_1=a*min(beta*u,v)<=b*u.\n\nThe first coordinate is <=b*u because beta*(1-a)*(u/v)<=ell.\nFor the entire allowed band this follows from\n\n    ell*(c-2)-beta*(1-a)\n       =[c*(c-2)-beta*(2-beta)]/c>0,\n\nas c*(c-2)>1 and beta*(2-beta)=1-epsilon^2<1. This proves(9.2) for all j and t.\n\nGeometric bracketing exactly as in Section6 proves\n\n    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j for 0<|s|<=d_j*u.\n\nFor inputs in[-A_j*v,A_j*u] with |s|>d_j*u, (9.1) gives\n|Psi_j(s)|<=u<|s|/d_j. Attainment, both endpoint energies and benchmark direction\n\nFor any fixed u,v>0, start at s=D_m*u. At every original index i,\nincluding the terminal, let P_i be the actual sum of the first i steps.\nThen\n\n    x_i=u*(D_m-P_i)>=u,\n    x_(n_m)=A_m*u,\n    f(x_(n_m))=D_m*u^2/2.\n\nThus this ONE static smooth convex function, with complete minimum{0}\nand own initial radius D_m*u, attains score1/D_m. Adversarial textual audit and exact limitations\n\nThe joint induction was checked against the prior known failure modes.\nReadback found and corrected an overstatement in the wording of the\npositive-tail lemma: only nonterminal queries must be saturated. The\nexplicit s=S_j*u+v example in Section5 disproves the version that also\nrequires terminal saturation. All uses need only the exact affine map,\nwhich the corrected chronological proof provides.\nThe false inequality |Psi_m(s)+S_m*g(Psi_m(s))|<=|s| is never used.\nIts root-crossing construction has no negative-to-positive return and\nis handled by Section8. The weak drift(2.1) is compatible with that\nconstruction: its displacement allowance is S_j*v/3, not S_j times\nthe terminal clipped gradient. No objective sublevel replaces a child\nimage, so the previous quartic-loss enlargement is not invoked.\n\nThe proof does not assume one-sided clipping after the first return.\nThe upper clip may reactivate inside the following H_k. The two actual\nsigned endpoint bounds in(6.3), and the proof order Plateau_(j+1) before\nDrift_(j+1), are the new controls. Cases z>0, z<0 with y<0, and z<0\nwith y>0 exhaust the drift induction. The exceptional d_j<1 case is\nexplicitly included; j=1 is its only nonempty-child occurrence.\n\nThe cutoff is strict. At d_j*v=u there is no query above the upper\nthreshold from that bridge, but a threshold touch is allowed and every\nformula remains valid. The no-eligible-level sentinel is m, not a new\nphysical bridge. The smallest eligible physical level is2. The last\nleaf is not discarded: it is the k=0, empty-suffix case in(7.2), where\nthe bound is a*v rather than b*v. Neither the number of returns nor\nthe ratio may be treated as fixed independently of m; the proof needs\nneither restriction.\n\nThe initial amplitude is paid twice by an exhaustive partition:\n|s|<=d_m*v uses the actual geometric radial bound, and |s|>d_m*v\nuses the actual terminal cap only when a negative-to-positive return\noccurred. The residual no-return class is separately handled by its\nwhole-word prefix and suffix masses. A global terminal cap is never\nasserted for an all-saturated trajectory.\n\nNo numerical test supports any step. Textual counterexample checks of\nthe small-core, saturated-ray, root-crossing, cutoff-tie and final-leaf\ncases did not refute the new candidate. This is not independent or\nverifier acceptance.
